#2918. Tencent Music: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Ximalaya's audiobook and podcast catalog now sits alongside Tencent Music's streaming, a bet on long-form audio that could reshape its content mix.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 3:42PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Tencent Music Entertainment Group ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 17, 2026, Tencent Music (TME +3.92%) announced total revenue grew 6% year over year to 8.9 billion Chinese Renminbi (RMB). Long-term investors should care because the company is shifting from reliance on basic music streaming toward intellectual property (IP) services, including stadium concerts and physical merchandise. Three takeaways from management explain how this transition is taking shape.

Tencent Music is leveraging its popular artist catalog to produce offline concerts and physical collectibles, creating revenue streams that go far beyond standard monthly subscription fees.

Integrating Ximalaya, a major long-form audio platform featuring audiobooks and podcasts, broadens content options while improving operating efficiency across both platforms.

Offline concert momentum and long-form audio subscriptions are expanding, giving the platform multi-tiered growth drivers across digital streaming and live entertainment. The platform holds a structural advantage through its deep integration with Tencent's social ecosystem. Management is watching macro advertising spending headwinds and shifts in revenue mix closely.

Executing on these content priorities creates a defensible ecosystem that competitors cannot easily copy.

#2916. Steph Curry to be re-evaluated on Friday

The Golden State Warriors will officially re-evaluate Stephen Curry on Friday, Nov. 27, marking two weeks since the 38-year-old guard suffered an oblique abdominal tear during a loss to the Houston Rockets.

While Friday's medical check represents a crucial milestone in Curry's recovery, it serves as a checkpoint rather than an immediate green light to play. Even with positive imaging, the two-time MVP will require a ramp-up period of on-court conditioning and contact practices before returning to game action, keeping a mid-December comeback as the realistic target.

Golden State has struggled to generate offense in his absence, dropping four of its first five games without him while failing to reach 100 points in any of those contests. Before exiting the Houston game early, Curry was off to an electric start, averaging 27.8 points on 45.7% three-point shooting. Because oblique muscles are vital to a shooter's rotational mechanics, the Warriors are expected to prioritize long-term health over rushing their franchise cornerstone back.

#2914. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

#2913. Sandisk’s 8-for-1 Split Is Done. Here’s What Investors Should Do Next.

Sandisk's stock split is finished, so the only question left is whether its AI-fueled flash memory business can hold up as prices cool.

By Micah Zimmerman – Nov 26, 2026 at 4:00PM EST

When a stock splits, the headlines tend to make it sound like something big just happened, but for Sandisk (SNDK -5.57%) shareholders, the real decision starts now that the paperwork is done and the share price looks a lot friendlier.

Sandisk announced its 8-for-1 forward split alongside its fiscal first-quarter results on Oct. 29, giving every holder of record on Nov. 9 seven extra shares for each one they owned. The change became effective on Nov. 19, and split-adjusted trading began the next morning.

A stock split, which is when a company divides each existing share into several cheaper ones, works like cutting a pizza into more slices without adding any cheese. Sandisk itself said that its overall market capitalization and stockholder ownership percentages would not be affected, and it framed the move as a way to make shares more accessible, including to employees buying through its stock purchase plan.

What the split can't do is change the business, so that's where investors should focus.

Sandisk makes NAND flash, which is the memory chip that keeps your data when the power goes off, and AI has turned that once-sleepy product into a hot commodity.

In the quarter that ended Oct. 2, revenue jumped 378% year over year to $11 billion, data center sales rose 61% from the prior quarter, and gross margin, which is the share of each sales dollar left after the cost of making the product, reached 85%.

CEO David Goeckeler said in the release, "We have started fiscal year 2027 with another quarter of strong performance, with robust and growing demand for our products driving outpaced growth in our data center business."

The AI connection is more specific than it sounds, because AI models need fast storage to hold the context of a conversation. On the earnings call, Goeckeler said, "We believe this is a secular dynamic that is expanding NAND's role across the AI stack."

The part I find most reassuring is Sandisk's long-term customer deals, which it calls New Business Models or NBMs. About half of its fiscal 2027 supply and two-thirds of its fiscal 2028 supply are already committed, 95% of those deals are with investment-grade companies, and Goeckeler said the contracts have "become the predominant way we deal with our customers." CFO Luis Visoso went further on the call, saying, "Our NBM pipeline exceeds our supply throughout the end of the decade."

Those contracts matter because flash prices are cooling. Research firm TrendForce now expects NAND contract prices to fall as much as 5% in the first quarter of 2027, with declines spreading across all product categories after that. Concentration adds risk too, since two customers each made up 12% of revenue last quarter.

The stock has already priced in plenty of that worry. Shares closed at $87.71 on Nov. 23, roughly 70% below their split-adjusted 52-week high, and trade at a price-to-earnings ratio of about 5.6. Management guided for split-adjusted earnings of $6.88 to $7.13 per share in the current quarter, which puts the stock near 3 times annualized earnings.

Visoso also cautioned on the call that the March quarter "is normally our weakest sequential quarter," so results may look less dazzling by spring, but the company still bought back $5 billion of stock last quarter, and Visoso said, "Our repurchases reflect our conviction in the long-term value of the business and our disciplined approach to capital allocation."

I think long-term investors who can stomach big swings should consider a modest position in Sandisk, sized for a flash market that could get rougher before it gets better, and add only if the long-term contracts keep holding up as prices fall. The split changed the price tag, but the contracts will decide the value.

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. #2901. This Anthropic proxy play is getting traders' attention – here's where they see it going

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Since then, the fund has barely moved, trading around $26.There are other bullish signs surrounding Anthropic's IPO in the derivatives markets. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.

#2887. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2884. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2883. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

#2874. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

#2872. AMD dives on short seller report of alleged China exposure

Shares of chipmaker Advanced Micro Devices close down 7.72% to $489.19 on Thursday after investment firm Hunterbrook Media issued a report alleging that the company "has its own China problem."

Hunterbrook disclosed that it had taken a short position in AMD.

The report claims that "after the U.S. blacklisted Chinese server maker IEIT Systems, the company kept getting newer AMD firmware, which enables AMD chips to run in servers."

CNBC did not independently verify this information.

Hunterbrook also said that new export rules in Washington could "tighten restrictions on AMD's foreign business," alleging that AMD "bills over 20% of its revenue to customers in China and Hong Kong," and "depends on a Chinese partner to finish most of its chips."

The allegations come amid investigations of rival chipmaker Nvidia over its sales to companies in Asia with ties to China. AMD has been viewed as a beneficiary of the uncertainty surrounding Nvidia.

"The claims misrepresent AMD's business practices and relationships," an AMD spokesperson said in a statement. "AMD complies with applicable US export control laws and regulations and maintains rigorous processes to ensure compliance across our global operations. We remain focused on serving our customers and upholding the highest standards of integrity."

Lisa Su, CEO of AMD, told investors on the company's recent earnings call that China is an "important" market and that the semiconductor giant wants to be fully compliant with U.S. export controls.

AMD continued to slide after hours, trading down 0.45% to $487 shortly after 4 p.m.

#2869. Cavs suffer familiar loss to Knicks; Mitchell’s 30 points not enough

The Cleveland Cavaliers have all but been eliminated from the NBA Cup after the New York Knicks, who outscored them 39-17 in the fourth quarter to pick up their second win over Cleveland this season.

The Cavs are now 2-2 in the cup, making it almost certain they will miss out on advancing to the next stage. There's always next year.

Cleveland's defensive focus and energy were much improved in the first half compared to their previous loss in Chicago. The Cavs played on a string, trusting each other by not overhelping — while seamlessly communicating through switches and containing New York's off-ball screening actions by working together.

Donovan Mitchell was relentlessly getting downhill throughout the first half. Mitchell opened the game shooting 8-for-9 from inside two-point range. He entered halftime with 20 points.

James Harden chipped in with 14 points on 8-for-8 shooting from the free-throw line in the first half. Mikal Bridges led the Knicks with 17 points on 8-of-11 shooting overall.

The Cavs hit the ground running in the third quarter, controlling the tempo and putting New York on the back foot before a myriad of fouls slowed the game to a crawl. This abrupt shift allowed the Knicks to storm back and make the game competitive again.

Cleveland found themselves trailing by double digits in the fourth quarter as the Knicks began turning defense into offense. A handful of errant turnovers from the Cavs' backcourt fueled the run.

Winning the possession battle has been an emphasis for the Cavs, especially in this matchup. The Knicks have historically punished Cleveland by grabbing offensive rebounds and making it impossible to end defensive possessions.

The Cavs started off on the right foot in that regard. They were outscoring New York 11-to-1 on second-chance points and had full control of the possession battle.

The Knicks grabbed every key rebound, forced backbreaking turnovers, and hit all of the big shots down the stretch to put this game out of reach. A fate the Cavs have suffered more than once to this team. As stated, it was a 22-point bludgeoning in the fourth quarter that closed the door.

Harden and Mitchell combined for 58 points. Both struggled in the fourth quarter after playing stellar basketball before. Their six shared turnovers felt like they came at the worst times.

The Cavs end the night officially below .500 with a 9-10 record. Not how anyone hoped this season would start.

#2868. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.

#2865. Player Grades: Cavs at Knicks – Another fourth quarter implosion

The Cleveland Cavaliers took another painful loss to the New York Knicks after going off the rails in the fourth quarter.

Grades are based on our usual expectations for each player.

30 points, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 steals, 2 blocks, 2 turnovers

Mitchell was lights out in the first half, storming downhill and busting New York's point-of-attack defense. The Knicks had no answer for him as Mitchell began the game 9-for-10 from inside of two-point range.

His defensive activity was strong, as well, with Mitchell racking up stocks throughout the game.

The fourth quarter was less efficient. Mitchell's drives became erratic, including one play where he was lost in the trees twice in the same possession. There's value in putting pressure on the rim, but Mitchell has to do a better job of moving away from pressure when the time calls for it.

It was hard to choose between Mitchell and Harden for Player of the Game. Mitchell's defense tipped the scales.

28 points, 5 assists, 1 rebound, 4 turnovers

Good things happen when Harden touches the paint. He's looked more spry and youthful in recent games, dancing with his dribble to work into the restricted area and create from there. Whether it was shooting a floater, kicking to the perimeter, or dropping it into Jarrett Allen's lap — Harden put on a clinic on how to drive the ball.

Unfortunately, Harden shot 1-of-8 from downtown and wasn't able to sprint through the finish line as everything went wrong in the fourth quarter.

10 points, 5 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals

Allen was able to roam freely on defense while Harden accepted the challenge of guarding Karl-Anthony Towns on the perimeter. This strategy worked throughout the night as Cleveland was able to safely navigate New York's off-ball actions with Allen standing in the paint to clean up any mistakes. It wasn't until the wheels came off in the fourth quarter, when the Knicks were able to sprint in transition and break the mundanity of the half-court, that Allen became nonconsequential.

8 points, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 steals, 2 blocks

His last shot attempt was swatted into the stands.

#2864. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for applicability of truth in lending and consumer leasing rules for consumer credit and lease transactions

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau today announced the dollar thresholds used to determine whether certain consumer credit and lease transactions in 2027 are subject to certain protections under Regulation Z (Truth in Lending) and Regulation M (Consumer Leasing).

By law, the agencies are required to adjust the thresholds annually based on the annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W. Transactions at or below the thresholds are subject to the protections of the regulations.

Specifically, based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the CPI-W as of June 1, 2026, Regulation Z and Regulation M generally will apply to consumer credit transactions and consumer leases of $76,300 or less in 2027. However, private education loans and loans secured by real property, such as mortgages, are subject to Regulation Z regardless of the amount of the loan.

Federal Register notice: Consumer Leasing (Regulation M) (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Truth in Lending (Regulation Z) (PDF)

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551

#2863. North Korea's Kim calls for nuclear arsenal expansion, says status 'will never change'

North Korean leader Kim Jong Un called for bolstering the country's nuclear arsenal and expanding the use of tactical nuclear weapons across multiple domains, state media reported Thursday, as Pyongyang reaffirmed its determination to retain its nuclear arms.

Kim's comments followed a working visit Wednesday to facilities involved in nuclear material production and weapons research, according to the Korean Central News Agency.

At the Nuclear Weapons Institute, he urged greater attention to "operationalizing" tactical nuclear weapons for a range of missions.

Kim directly linked the development of those weapons to North Korea "sharing a border with its most dangerous, hostile state," an apparent reference to South Korea.

The visit was part of Pyongyang's efforts to counter what Kim described as "the reckless expansion of adversary states' nuclear forces and military alliances," according to KCNA. He also singled out South Korea as a threat to North Korea's security and sovereignty.

Kim reiterated that North Korea's status as a nuclear-armed state was irreversible, dismissing "denuclearization rhetoric" and saying its nuclear status "will never change."

"[Our nuclear policy] is a core element of safeguarding the sovereignty of the Republic, enshrined in the supreme law of the state," he added.

The report did not identify the sites Kim visited or disclose any production figures.

The announcement followed an assessment last week by the International Atomic Energy Agency that another extension to Yongbyon's original uranium-enrichment facility had been externally completed by late October.

The nuclear watchdog has lacked access to North Korea since 2009 and monitors its activities using satellite imagery and open-source intelligence.

Citing what he portrayed as growing threats from Washington and its allies, Kim accused the U.S. of encouraging neighboring allies to acquire nuclear-powered submarines, pursuing nuclear sharing and the joint use of nuclear weapons.

His comments come after South Korea announced efforts to acquire nuclear-powered submarines after President Donald Trump backed the plan in October 2025, according to a White House joint fact sheet. Nuclear-powered propulsion does not itself mean that a vessel carries nuclear weapons.

Trump had expressed interest in meeting with Kim at the APEC summit in Shenzhen last week, according to Reuters, but no meeting took place and North Korea did not publicly respond to the proposal.

Trump denied that Kim had ignored him, adding that communications could "start up right away," in an interview with the New York Post Tuesday. He said the two leaders had not been in contact "recently."

"We've been off to other things," Trump added.

North Korea has continued to accelerate its nuclear weapons development and rebuff South Korean and U.S. attempts to resume dialogue since negotiations between Trump and Kim collapsed in 2019.

— CNBC's Jenny Lee contributed to this report.

#2861. K-beauty stocks rally as Korean brands top Amazon bestseller lists globally ahead of Black Friday

South Korean beauty stocks jumped Thursday as the country's skincare brands dominated Amazon's bestseller rankings ahead of Black Friday.

Shares of K-beauty giant APR climbed 3.77%, while cosmetic contracts manufacturers Kolmar Korea and Cosmax gained 5.46% and 2.91%, respectively.

APR shares, in particular, have rallied sharply this week, up 28%, defying a broader market sell-off in Seoul markets, with the benchmark Kospi index down 2.65% during the same period.

A cosmetic sector index tracked by Meritz Securities had gained 12% this week until Wednesday, according to a Thursday note from the brokerage.

The rally reflects renewed optimism over earnings growth following strong performances by K-beauty brands across major e-commerce platforms in the U.S. and Europe with Black Friday sales already underway, as well as increasingly attractive valuations following months of share price declines, according to Meritz.

K-beauty products topped Amazon's skincare bestseller rankings across six major markets — the U.S., the U.K., Germany, France, Spain and Italy — as of Nov. 25, according to data compiled by Meritz Securities. Other Korean beauty brands, including Biodance, Illiyoon and Anua, also ranked among the top 10 in the U.S., the data showed.

As of Thursday morning, Korean beauty products occupied several of the top spots in Amazon's U.S. beauty and personal care category, the e-commerce website showed.

Meritz Securities expects APR shares to maintain their positive momentum through the year-end, with a more substantial rebound in early 2027.

The brokerage named APR and Cosmax as its top picks in the cosmetics sector. Korean beauty brands have established an "overwhelming presence" in the bestseller rankings of major e-commerce platforms during Black Friday sales, prompting the market to reassess the growth's potential, the brokerage said.

#2857. Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.

#2854. Art Rooney II expresses support for Mike McCarthy, calls Aaron Rodgers not a "good teammate"

After Wednesday's unprecedented chaos, it had to happen. And it did.

Steelers owner Art Rooney II has addressed the events that resulted in the benching and then the firing of quarterback Aaron Rodgers.

"A change had to be made, and I certainly agree with the change and the way it was handled," Rooney told Gerry Dulac of the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette in a Thursday interview.

The change was sparked by the notion that Rodgers gave coach Mike McCarthy an ultimatum: "Either let me call all the plays or just release me." Instead, McCarthy benched him.

It went to the next level when, as many expected, Rodgers scurried to the McAfee show to take shots at McCarthy for missing meetings, showing up late to meetings, and falling asleep in meetings — among other things, including the ultra-embarrassing "1-2-3 Packers!" moment from halftime of Sunday's loss to the Eagles.

"There's maybe a time and place after the season if he wanted to kind of unload," Rooney said. "He talks about wanting to be a good teammate, but you don't do that to your teammates a couple days before the game, that's for sure. . . .

"In general, I think, historically, what's in the locker room stays in the locker room. That's the way it's always been. That's the way it should be as far as I'm concerned."

Rooney also confirmed that the team supports McCarthy, despite Rodgers's rant and reports confirming the facts underpinning the petty delivery of them.

"I just want to say that I have 100 [percent] confidence in Mike McCarthy and I expect he'll be our coach this season, next season and beyond," Rooney told Dulac. "The big thing, what happened this week, is insubordination can't be tolerated in any organization. When you start getting ultimatums, you can't live with that."

Rooney also was asked whether Rodgers raised with the owner supposed concerns about McCarthy's health.

"Nothing," Rooney said. "Mike's as healthy as the rest of us. Health was not an issue. I'm not sure where that part comes [from]."

Rooney also addressed something that has been largely overlooked during the fast-moving drama: Rodgers's subpar performance.

"The sad thing is Mike took some of the bullets for Aaron in terms of some of the plays that were called, particularly in the red-zone troubles that we had," Rooney said. "Mike took some of the bullets for things that were going wrong down there. He tried to protect him, and it just wasn't working."

They should have known it wouldn't work between Rodgers and McCarthy. It's still unclear why either one of them thought it would. McCarthy shouldn't have wanted a reunion with Rodgers. And Rodgers shouldn't have wanted a second act with McCarthy.

Then again, it's not as if Rodgers had many choices. In 2025, he had few takers. In 2026, he had fewer. If he was going to ensure that his final NFL throw wasn't a pick-six by playing another year, Pittsburgh wasn't just the best place.

#2848. Buffalo Bills OLB Greg Rousseau single-handedly wrecking NFL offenses

To say that Buffalo Bills outside linebacker Greg Rousseau is having a career year in 2026 is quite the understatement. Rousseau leads the NFL in quarterback hits with 29, and is currently second league-wide with 10 sacks in 10 games this season. His current sack total is a new career high for Rousseau — the first time in his professional career he's reach double-digits in that category.

Rousseau deserves to be mentioned in NFL Defensive Player of the Year conversations. He seems to be a good candidate as an All-Pro selection, and is likely to receive Pro Bowl honors.

His success comes following a switch this offseason to 3-4 outside linebacker after playing the previous five seasons as a defensive end in a 4-3 system. It's the first time in Rousseau's career that he's played that role as an edge rusher out wide from the linebacker box.

When you run through Rousseau's stats this year, it's easy to imagine that he's thrilled with the position shift. In just over half a season to this point, Rousseau has set new career highs in sacks (10), quarterback hits (29), and pass defenses (8); while tying his career high in forced fumbles (3), and remains just four off his single-season-career-best of 16 tackles for loss.

When new Buffalo Bills head coach Joe Brady hired Jim Leonhard as the team's current defensive coordinator, many were very curious how the former Bills safety intended to implement the attack-based system he espoused. After all, Leonhard had never called a team's defensive plays at the NFL level, and he decided to make sweeping changes while marching forward with a large group of players hand-picked by prior head coach Sean McDermott.

With the benefit of hindsight, it's now clear that Rousseau may have been miscast as a do-it-all defensive end left to battle it out in the trenches. He played well there for McDermott, of course, but the brand of game-wrecking energy now regularly seen was at best available in spurts with him so deeply tied up in close hand combat against offensive linemen.

Leonhard and outside linebackers coach Bobby April III have transformed Rousseau into the sort of player who keeps other teams' offensive coordinators up and worrying about game plans. In his new role, Rousseau uses his albatross-like wingspan and skyscraper height to out-leverage would-be blockers. His reach and hands have become a problem for most quarterbacks, sometimes to the detriment of their depth chart.

Rousseau's jump in production is staggering, quite honestly, just from a quick glance at his career numbers via Pro Football Reference:

In preparing to face the Bills on Thanksgiving, Kansas City Chiefs offensive coordinator Eric Bieniemy was quick to praise the man he nicknamed "Stretch Armstrong," adding that KC's defense must do what it can "neutralize his strengths" and "have a plan in place, making sure that we're putting bodies on him and keeping him off our quarterback."

It's been quite some time since Buffalo had an edge rusher who instilled such fear among NFL teams, maybe dating back to defensive end Mario Williams. Now, Leonhard and the Bills must find a way to get better production out of the team's other front-seven defenders.

Amid all the praise for Rousseau's game in 2026 comes a worrying trend when evaluating his defensive teammates. Though Rousseau is a 1-man wrecking machine who's having a career season, teammates around him have failed to produce much as pass rushers.

After Rousseau, there's a steep drop in production and impact plays among the defensive line and outside linebackers. Defensive linemen Ed Oliver and Deone Walker have received praise in year one within Leonhard's system, but the overall sack production beyond Rousseau has been dreadful.

Defensive lineman T.J. Parker ranks second on the team with 3.5 sacks. Nine other players on the team have chipped in toward the sack total, however those nine defenders have only 9.5 sacks combined. Quick math reveals that Rousseau has more sacks than all Bills defenders, not named Parker, added together.

Buffalo's schedule over the next month is a gauntlet, and the defense will be tested beyond the trials early this season. Someone must step up in a complementary role as a pass rusher in tandem with Rousseau, otherwise teams may begin implementing new ways of slowing down the Bills' best defender.

In the four games since Buffalo's bye week, the defense has played a far more aggressive brand of football. Perhaps Leonhard's only just begun scratching the surface of the pressure package he intends to release on the NFL. A big test awaits Thursday night.

#2846. Knicks rally to beat Cavaliers behind unlikely hero, expanded lineup to keep slim NBA Cup hopes alive

Brown, as he noted before tipoff, sees the scheduling benefits of being eliminated from Adam Silver's beloved tournament.

But it didn't matter. The Knicks (12-6), winners of six straight overall, surged in the fourth quarter behind unlikely hero Jose Alvarado.

Mikal Bridges celebrates after hitting a 3-pointer during the Knicks' 121-102 win over the Cavaliers on Nov. 25, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

They avoided Cup elimination — at least for a couple of days — with a 121-102 win over the collapsing Cavaliers, who blew an 11-point lead late in the third quarter.

The Knicks (1-2 in group play) remain unlikely to advance. To win the group, they'd need a victory over the Heat on Friday by at least seven points and a Sixers loss to the Pacers. Advancing as the wild card is more complicated and unlikely.

But the defending Cup champs aren't technically eliminated as of Thanksgiving.

They were short-handed Wednesday without OG Anunoby (calf strain) and Miles McBride (left adductor tightness). They lost backup center Andre Drummond to a head injury in the first half.

Then they punished the Cavs (9-10) by winning the fourth quarter 39-17 with Alvarado scoring 16 of his 21 points in the final period and Brown going deep into his bench.

Jose Alvarado celebrates after hitting a second 3-pointer in the fourth quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers. Jason Szenes for NY Post

Mikal Bridges finished with an efficient 19 points. Karl-Anthony Towns extended his double-double streak to 15 (barely) with 12 points and 10 boards.

If and when Brown's squad is out of the tournament, the coach is looking at elimination as an NBA Cup half full. Losing translates to an easier schedule for the Knicks, which wasn't lost on the coach before Wednesday's comeback win.

Brown knows because the NBA shared the possibilities. Advancement potentially brings another three road games — including the final in Indiana.

Josh Hart reacts during the second quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

And elimination means two games — including one at home — over a nine-day stretch.

"You look at it and you're like, 'Whoa, hell, that's a lot of days off if you don't advance,' " Brown said. "And so, again, you wanna compete, you wanna win, but if you don't … there's an opportunity if you got some guys banged up to get rest and or to work on some things in between the games that you have if you're not involved in the Cup."

Silver won't like that answer from the defending NBA Cup champs. But Brown was speaking from experience.

The Knicks added a long road trip to their schedule last season because of the NBA Cup, then an 83rd game with the final in Las Vegas.

The results were phenomenal for the Knicks. They won the Cup, earned the prize money (each player won over $500,000) and months later captured the big NBA championship.

But the Knicks, as an organization, didn't fully embrace Silver's tournament. It took them nearly a full year to hang the banner.

Then Wednesday, the coach of the Cup champs was openly espousing the virtues of being eliminated.

Mikal Bridges rises up for a layup during the second quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

No crying over a spilled NBA Cup. For now, though, the Cup is still on the table.

"When I talk about the Cup, it adds a level of stress maybe on your mind and your body because the games are intense as you go along and you're playing more games in a short amount of time," Brown said. "I remember last year it was like, 'Game, Game, Game.' And then you win the Cup and you celebrate it that night and you hop on a plane and go play in Indiana the next night. And so it's great. You like the competition and the setup but it adds a lot of stress to you physically and mentally. I'm saying this because especially with the extended season we had last year and how taxing the games were for our playoff run mentally and physically, I'm trying to be smart."

#2842. Robots could be doing household chores in just 1-2 years, Chinese EV firm says

BEIJING — The household robot could soon become a reality, Brian Gu, co-president of Xpeng, a Chinese electric car company which has entered the humanoids space, told CNBC.

"Maybe after successful deployment into commercial settings, maybe another one to two years, we can start thinking about deploying into home settings," Gu said. "Technology is moving at a much faster clip."

Xpeng revealed its latest general-purpose humanoid robot Iron on Wednesday. The company plans to start training the robot as a store assistant in its retail car locations in the first quarter next year, after starting mass production in January. Sales are set to begin in the second quarter, with deliveries expected later in the year.

Founded in 2014 as an electric car company, Xpeng beat Tesla to rolling out driver-assist in mainland China. The Chinese automaker has launched its own artificial intelligence chips. It's also expanded into personal flying vehicles and robotics. The robotics unit this summer raised $900 million at a $6.3 billion valuation in its first funding round, led by IDG Capital.

Investors, however, appear to be a tad skeptical about the developments at Xpeng, with the downturn in the Chinese auto market also weighing on the company. Xpeng's shares, listed in both New York and Hong Kong, have dropped more than 55% this year amid broad sector declines.

China's humanoid industry has faced more regulatory scrutiny on financing in recent months after Unitree's high-profile IPO and stock slump, as well as a rush of capital into the sector. That's all raised questions about commercialization and the efficacy of the core tech.

Gu said Xpeng's robotics stands out for its rare combination of the hardware, software and real-life use cases. He's expecting to add more investors after Iron's planned commercial launch next year.

"You can always make the head talk, you can always make the leg run, you can always make the hands to do certain tasks," Gu said. "But doing a task while you're walking and answering questions at the same time, that's the most challenging part. And then doing everything well, not falling down, not getting distracted. The model has to work in sync."

Most humanoid robot startups, especially in China, focus on a portion of the tech, such as hands or the artificial intelligence-powered brain. Some in the industry also see themselves as original equipment manufacturers, with another company tying the parts together for the finished robot.

Xpeng's co-founder and CEO He Xiaopeng, a software engineer, always thought that software could solve "everything,'" Gu said. "And after he got into the EV business he realized how hard it is to make hardware ... really do the things that you want to do safely and consistently."

On Wednesday, Xpeng announced its "Turing Physical AI Architecture" for using AI and real-time decision-making tech to control cars and humanoids.

Gu told CNBC that Xpeng's robotics development only needs the driver-assist chips, which the company already has, indicating limited impact from U.S. restrictions on advanced Nvidia chip sales to China.

"Right now we don't see any change to our supply chain, and also ability to have these chips."

In 2023, Xpeng's then-head of autonomous driving, Xinzhou Wu, left to lead Nvidia's automotive chip business.

As Xpeng ramps up its humanoid robotics business, Gu said the company's existing work in autonomous driving technology translates easily to a new product line — with far higher profit margins. "It's much easier to be profitable for the robotics business than the autos business."

Robotics will likely become a meaningful part of Xpeng's business in two to three years, Gu said, with profitability coming as soon as next year. That value will "dramatically" increase with robots entering households, he said.

But Gu noted that compared to commercial environments, homes require robots to act safely around elderly people, children and pets, in addition to reacting to emergency situations. That increases requirements for battery power and performance consistency, he said.

#2841. Chiefs-Bills: 5 questions with Buffalo Rumblings ahead of Week 12

Tonight, the Kansas City Chiefs face the Buffalo Bills in a week 12 Thanksgiving's night showdown. We welcome Matthew Byham of Buffalo Rumblings— our sister SBNation site covering the Bills— for Five Questions with the Enemy.

Join fellow Chiefs fans to react to every explosive play and game-changing moment in real time.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

Download the SB Nation app on your mobile device and join the Buffalo Rumblings community through Sunday's game to get perspectives from both sides of the Week 12 matchup!

It wasn't all that surprising to me that Josh Allen and the Bills struggled to convert red zone trips into touchdowns. The Miami Dolphins under Jeff Hafley field a top-10 defense and they put together a great game plan. Unfortunately, their surprising talent is overshadowed by the mess on offense. Divisional matchups are always unpredictable, no matter any lack of familiarity (i.e., Dolphins having new coaching/roster overhaul).

The team would never admit it, but I also have to wonder if Buffalo was holding some things back a bit with a very important game only a few days later. Not necessarily in an effort to hide plays (because at this point there's a lot of tape on every team), but rather pacing themselves for the marathon ahead.

Not to take anything away from a Dolphins team that seems to be on the right track, but the Bills never trailed in that game. Head coach Joe Brady probably felt that even accumulating field goals might be too much for Miami's offense to counter.

As to your question about what the Bills need to improve? First and foremost, it would be great if tight end Dalton Kincaid (questionable w/thumb, ankle) were able to play in this one. He missed last week, and it's pretty evident that Brady's offense is vastly superior with Kincaid involved — especially as a red zone target.

But drilling down further, the Bills struggle to gain separation on routes, and it's magnified in the red zone.

Buffalo would also do well to leverage their running game more near the goal line in this one. Sometimes I believe Brady gets a bit cute trying to outsmart a defense when, time and again, they've proven almost impossible to stop in the trenches this season.

I'd like to see Brady roll out with two running backs near the goal line. It's rare for him to do that anywhere on the field, even though Ty Johnson is the team's most reliable pass catcher. Pairing Johnson with James Cook III in goal-to-go situations, while accounting for Allen's dual threat, could create real havoc for a defense. Brady just has to be willing to shift out of a few 13-personnel plays, while finding the shortest path from point A to B.

Allen should run as instinct instructs, nothing more, nothing less. I wouldn't expect Brady to confine him to one thing or another, aside from asking him to avoid arm punts in a game like this, and with weather that's going to be suspect at best. Allowing Allen to play free from constraint is the best move, while giving him options to abort anything risky.

It is interesting that Allen didn't rush a single time versus the Dolphins, but I'm left wondering if that was to protect him from any potential injury. Early on, Buffalo's offense faced 4th & 1, and they ran a traditional handoff to Cook, who went right. It was a complete paradigm shift from what we've known of the Bills' short-yardage offense. I expect him to have at least a few carries in this game, both in short- and long-yardage situations.

James Cook is vital to the Bills' success on offense. You could place bets on Buffalo leveraging Cook early and often, even if the weather was trending pleasant. Buffalo is 14-2 when Cook carries 20 times or more in a game. Under Brady, the Bills now employ a smash-mouth brand of football where they'll remain committed to the run game even if it's only getting a few yards a clip in early action.

Outsiders have begun to catch on that Brady's system is heavy on the run, despite having Allen at quarterback. Somehow, Cook seems to be fairly overlooked in many circles despite leading the league in rushing last season and roughly 30 yards out of first place this season.

Cook is slight by typical NFL standards, but he's proven tough between the tackles and moves like a hummingbird in traffic. With Cook and the maulers along the offensive line, Buffalo has been able to wear out defenses in a four-quarter battle of attrition.

That's the question everyone wants answered right now. It's fair to say that Buffalo hasn't faced the NFL's best quarterbacks in the last four games, but there's reason to be optimistic about their trajectory. That's due to confidence, which is so important in this sport.

Early in the season, defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard's group was often out of position and visibly confused pre-snap. The pass rush was the only thing working, and mostly because Greg Rousseau has been a revelation as a 3-4 outside linebacker.

The secondary has played well of late, especially since moving on from safety C.J. Gardner-Johnson (first to Damar Hamlin and now Amani Hooker — the latter via trade). Cornerback Christian Benford has been elite, working to almost completely shut down tight end Brock Bowers and wide receiver Justin Jefferson, and holding wide receiver Zay Flowers to zero burger on the stat sheet.

Make no mistake, this is a huge challenge. Kelce will always be Kelce, right (he's always so open!)? It seems like this may be the most productive group of pass catchers KC's had since Tyreek Hill wore red. Will they hold the Chiefs under their 34-point average in the last three games? It's impossible to know, but at the same time the Bills are a different caliber of team compared to the New York Jets, Atlanta Falcons (what an epic defensive drop this season), and Arizona Cardinals.

It's similar to Buffalo, where the Chiefs are either more complete or far better than the Baltimore Ravens, Minnesota Vikings, Jets, or Dolphins. I think we find out a lot more about both teams Thanksgiving night.

I know that many Chiefs fans like to say that the regular-season matchup means little, and that Buffalo has yet to defeat Mahomes and company in the postseason. However, this is a massive game simply because of the playoff bye that's at stake.

Allen and the Bills have never earned the one seed, which I believe harmed prior rosters as it relates to injury recovery/availability in the playoffs under McDermott. Currently in control of the one seed, I'd venture to guess that Allen and company are going to be locked-in and ready to take a more firm hold on the lead.

I understand you're hoping for a final score prediction, but I never pick Bills games. I think they have as good a chance as any team to defeat KC, given their season-long consistency as a dominant throwback offense. Even if the defense gives up 34 points, Allen and Cook are fully capable of besting it with 35 or more.

If it's a low-scoring affair instead due to weather, I expect Buffalo and Kansas City to each try bleeding the clock through the run game. That might be both teams' plan A regardless of the situation. The Bills are built to both bully the clock and go toe-to-toe in a shootout.

Who wins? I'd say since both teams are once again playoff favorites, they each have a pretty fair shot at postgame turkey. It comes down to the red zone, and maybe a two-point conversion or two. We'll find out what's what soon enough in what should be a thrilling Turkey Day nightcap!

#2838. Judge blocks FCC early-renewal process for Disney-owned ABC stations, for now

A judge has temporarily blocked the Federal Communications Commission from continuing its early renewal of a set of broadcast station licenses for Disney-owned ABC.

Judge Loren L. AliKhan of U.S. District Court in Washington granted Disney the preliminary injunction it sought and denied the FCC's attempt to dismiss the suit.

"This is not a close case," wrote the federal judge. "Given the extreme, indeed existential, nature of the possible penalty Plaintiffs face—loss of their broadcast licenses—the court concludes that early license renewal proceedings create a risk sufficient to chill a broadcaster of ordinary firmness."

The broadcast network had filed the lawsuit and followed the FCC beginning its early review of the ABC licenses. The government agency cited concerns around ABC's diversity, equity and inclusion efforts as the reason behind the start of the early renewal — years ahead of their scheduled expiration.

"Today's decision is an important victory for the First Amendment," said an ABC spokesperson.

An FCC spokesperson said in a statement that the part of the decision granting ABC's motion "is wrong on the facts and wrong on the law," the Associated Press reported.

In her ruling, AliKhan said that early license renewal proceedings came over a year into the FCC's enforcement push against ABC, starting with an investigation into Disney's employment practices in March 2025 and leading to the renewal proceedings in April this year.

ABC on Tuesday had said that it continued to "face irreparable harm" from the administration, which "has been acting" through the FCC, which is run by President Donald Trump appointee Brendan Carr.

AliKhan, a Joe Biden appointee, noted in her ruling that ABC had to change how it handled guest appearances for top shows "The View" and "Jimmy Kimmel Live!" with no candidate for public office appearing on the former and a senatorial candidate's appearance on the latter uploaded to YouTube instead of being broadcast.

"The prospect of license non-renewal has also factored into plaintiffs' calculus of when and how they cover President Trump and political topics," said the judge.

ABC had alleged that Trump and Carr have been making comments "intended to chill their protected speech," and that such comments have escalated since the 2024 presidential election.

The White House and the FCC didn't immediately respond to requests for comment from CNBC.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan contributed to the story

#2835. How Trump’s latest tariff moves hand India an edge over rival Asian exporters

The latest U.S. tariff rates announced Wednesday have signaled that Washington is willing to offer India an edge over its competitors if New Delhi agrees to finalize a long-pending trade deal.

The Office of the United States Trade Representative has proposed a tariff of 17% on Indian exports if New Delhi "were to reach an Agreement on Reciprocal Trade" with Washington. It added that without a trade deal, India could face tariffs of up to 25% under Section 301 investigations encompassing excess production capacity that harms U.S. interests.

Meanwhile, the USTR has proposed tariffs of 18% on Bangladesh, Cambodia, Thailand, and 20% on China and Vietnam, subject to their respective trade deals, handing India a competitive advantage over rival exporters.

The U.S. expects the announcement of a lower tariff rate will encourage New Delhi to sign off on a trade deal before the G20, Atman Trivedi, partner at Washington-based DGA Albright Stonebridge Group, told CNBC.

India has got a "better bargain with Washington than its main competitors," and it may be enough for the Indian government "to declare victory and secure domestic backing for the deal," Trivedi said.

In February, U.S. President Donald Trump had announced a deal with India, claiming that New Delhi had accepted to "Buy American" at a much higher level after the U.S. agreed to slash tariffs to 18% from 50% earlier.

The India-U.S. trade deal has been pending with both sides indicating there was little room to grant concessions, while the Supreme Court annulling Trump tariffs allowed New Delhi to demand preferential tariffs that give it an edge over competitors.

"Today's announcement increases the urgency of reaching an agreement," Ronak D. Desai, visiting fellow at Hoover Institution, Stanford University, told CNBC. He added that while the prospect of a 17% tariff may strengthen the economic case for the deal, the threat of a 25% tariff could stir the sensitivity of the Indian government.

"The Modi government will be particularly sensitive to any perception that India is conceding under American Pressure," Desai said.

India's commerce ministry on Wednesday said it "does not have structural excess capacity in any sector," denying the findings of the USTR investigation, but added that it remains committed to "working closely with the United States towards the early conclusion of the India-U.S. Bilateral Trade Agreement."

Despite India facing steep tariffs, which were raised to 50%, New Delhi's trade surplus with the U.S. rose to $57.3 billion in 2025 from $45.2 billion in 2024.

Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Trump are likely to meet in December for the G20 leaders' meeting in Florida, and several experts believe the two leaders could seal the trade deal on the sidelines of the summit.
