#2834. Rams PFF grades: Aaron Donald and Myles Garrett turn back clock

The first NFL pre-Thanksgiving game brought a delicious victory for the Los Angeles Rams on Wednesday night. LA defeated the Green Bay Packers 26-13 to move to 7-4 on the season. After starting 2-4, winning five-straight didn't seem to be in the cards. Their fortune seems to be shifting even in the face of injury.

Who stood out on an individual level from this game? Let's review the Pro Football Focus (PFF) grades and data in order to lean into some analytical takeaways.

*Final grades are not currently available as of the writing of this post. Due to the Thanksgiving holiday I am going to run with the data still under review. Close enough.

Mumpfield was the Rams' passing offense on Wednesday night. He earned 69 yards on four catches and provided the only threat vertically. The next closest pass catchers in terms of yardage were Blake Corum at 21 and Kyren Williams at 19. Xavier Smith was the only other receiver to catch a pass and his solo reception went for four yards.

Is that a reliable way to play offense and win games? Not really. The Rams must find better answers from their receiver group. They cannot rely on Davante Adams to completely reverse their fortunes on his own, especially coming back from a significant injury this late into his career.

Dedich filled in for the injured Bill Murray who had replaced Steve Avila in the starting lineup. Dedich always seems to fare well in terms of PFF grading, and it's encouraging to see him pickup where he left off after suffering an injury this summer that has limited his availability. Dedich is undersized. That may prevent him from ever becoming a full-time starter. He still has value in a reserve role.

Veteran tight end Tyler Higbee's most important contributions came as a run blocker where he earned a mark from PFF of 70.2. He added one catch for 14 yards and came close to another big play. Higbee played roughly half the game before suffering an injury and being ruled out. Davis Allen was also ruled out. The statuses of both are worth monitoring moving forward.

What more is there to say about Blake Corum's performance not just Wednesday night but over the last seven weeks? Against the Packers he grinded out 184 yards and a score for an average of 5.80. PFF credited the back with 103 yards after contact (3.22 average). The offensive line was road grading as well, as they generated 2.58 yards before contact on Corum's carries. Not a bad day at the office.

It would be a welcome development—given the amount of players across the Rams' roster that need new contracts—if Limmer could takeover the starting center job on a going forward basis. Injured starter Coleman Shelton is approaching the end of his 2025 free agent deal. Limmer has one year left on his rookie contract. He continues to excel in pass protection in the middle of the offensive line.

Matthew Stafford finished 17th in grades out of the 19 players who appeared on offense. PFF graded him at a mark of 54.2. The outlet did not credit the veteran quarterback with a single big-time throw. He had one turnover-worthy play that resulted into an interception that could have proved costly; however, Jordan Love made a mistake of his own just two plays later.

Stafford has now thrown an interception in all but two of 11 games this season. He's at 11 on the year, so averaging one per game in the aggregate. If the Rams are going to be limited offensively and lean into ball control into order to constrict the opposition into submission, one requirement of that is taking care of the football. Can the Rams trust Stafford in this area? His track record so far this year suggests not.

Rams fans are waiting for Stafford to turn his season around. It hasn't happened yet. He's still the 21st-graded quarterback by both offensive grade and passing grade. It's clear he has taken a major step back this year. The end could be nearer than expected.

It's difficult to say anything other than 2027 seems to be Ty Simpson's year to takeover.

Donald and Garrett each had five total pressures on Jordan Love even though neither finished with a sack. The Rams only brought down Love once (Bryon Young), although the pressure did ultimately force three turnovers by the Pakcers' offense. Love easily could have finished with at least five interceptions—including dropped picks by Quentin Lake and Trent McDuffie and a takeaway by Kam Curl that was reversed because of penalty.

Kinchens forced a major collision along the sideline with receiver Christian Watson that dramatically altered the rest of the game. Kinchens was ejected, which resulted in controversy. Watson entered concussion protocol and was ruled out for the second half. The Rams seemingly had no answers for the dynamic receiver up to that point, and the Packers had little going through the air in the second half without Watson.

Make it another hallmark punch out for Nate Landman. He stole the ball away from tight end Tucker Kraft for a key takeaway.

The veteran linebacker played just 25 snaps. Omar Speights had 10. Shaun Dolac played seven. The roles of these players are changing as Lake took over the green dot duties. That isn't necessarily a bad thing. The defensive line is limiting opposing rushing attacks on their own. The Rams are able to pivot more towards additional safeties to focus on tightening up through the air. It's working. Stick with it.

Lake finished with the second-highest coverage grade on the defense (87.0) behind only Landman (90.5). He dropped an interception that likely would have scored and put the Packers away even earlier. In total he finished with two pass breakups.

The Packers went after reserve corners Josh Wallace and Emmanuel Forbes often with mixed results. Each had four targets into their coverage. Wallace allowed three receptions for 15 yards and the touchdown to Watson. Forbes gave up three catches for 79 yards (36 after the catch) and he caught the interception that effectively sealed the game.

While their PFF marks were low relative to the other members of the defense (18th and 19th out of 20), these coverage numbers aren't something that will get you beat. The standard is not perfection for these players as they look to replace injured starter Jaylen Watson. There will be negative moments. The Rams must hope that the positives—like Forbes' interception—outweigh the bad.

#2832. Cathie Wood’s Ark Invest was a big buyer of Anthropic as she tells CNBC she’s ‘quite optimistic’

Cathie Wood was a major buyer of Anthropic following its debut Thursday on the Nasdaq, adding shares in all her major funds.

Wood's firm, which already owned private shares of the artificial intelligence company in its Ark Venture Fund leading up to the initial public offering, bought $90.8 million worth of Anthropic in six funds, according to a trade notification from the firm.

"We've owned Anthropic since early '23," Wood told CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Friday. "It's our best performing equity by far in ARKVX, our venture fund. ... Our confidence has grown with time as it really took over the enterprise" AI business.

Anthropic shares jumped 17% in their first day, but closed well off the highs of the session and were headed for sizable losses Friday. The Nasdaq, which was expected to get a boost from a major IPO reviving enthusiasm about the technology trade, fell by 2% on Thursday.

"We're climbing a wall of worry," said Wood. "Everybody is worried about AI hype and the political situation after the election, but I think we'll look back and say, OK this is more like the '80s and '90s, climbing a wall of worry you know with bricks much like what we have today: inflation, interest rates, wars, new technologies and so forth so quite optimistic here."

The Anthropic stakes are relatively small across the funds, but Wood typically will buy shares in companies she likes steadily during long stretches. Wood said she is "averaging down" with her purchases of Anthropic.

In her $6.1 billion Ark Innovation ETF, Anthropic is a 0.3% stake. The biggest holdings in that fund are Tesla and SpaceX, representing more than 21% of the fund together.

Ark Innovation, which retail investors piled into during the pandemic as it gained about 150% in 2020, tumbled the subsequent next two years, losing 67% in 2022 alone. Wood's following was dented after the big losses.

The fund had a comeback stretch as the bull market revved back up from 2023 to 2025, but has fallen on hard times again this year, down more than 10% in 2026. The S&P 500 is up 4.8% this year.

Wood dismissed comparisons of today's environment to the dot-com bubble.

"Back then if an Anthropic had gone public, it probably would have gone up threefold the first day," she said. "That's how crazy things were."

#2831. Knicks 121, Cavs 102: Scenes from a second-half bench bash

The Knicks' hopes of repeating as NBA Cup champions hinged on beating Cleveland (9-10) tonight and Miami on Friday, while Philadelphia had to beat Miami (which they did) and Indiana has to topple Philadelphia. Even then, advancement would come down to point differential, meaning New York (12-6) would likely need to win both games by double digits.

Consider the mission half completed. As you could deduce from the blazing blue court at Madison Square Garden, tonight's tilt was a Cup game. Three Knicks were sidelined by injury: OG Anunoby, Miles McBride, and Jordan Clarkson. Anunoby's absence was felt as the Cavaliers scored 61 first-half points (and a lopsided free-throw disparity stung, too). The adjustments Mike Brown made in the locker room paid dividends on the court. His squad applied stingier defense, got their turnovers under control, won the fourth quarter 39-17, and flipped the game to win, 121-102.

Of the six Knicks with double-digit points tonight, Jose Alvarado's barrage of fourth-quarter triples and his season-high / team-high 21 were arguably the most impactful. On a short-handed night, and with multiple starters playing through injury, Jose was once again prepared to step into the spotlight and contribute meaningful minutes. What a professional! What depth!

Three consecutive turnovers didn't help (two by Karl-Anthony Towns, one by Josh Hart) as the Knicks fell behind early in the first quarter. Midway through the period, Jalen Brunson scored seven in under two minutes to regain the lead. In this streaky contest, though, it made sense that he missed his next five attempts. Towns (12 PTS, 10 RBS, 7 AST, +21) was a game-time decision due to knee soreness, and his scoring showed the limp, producing a single point in the frame. Filling in for Anunoby in the first five, Landry Shamet made his sixth start of the season. He finished with a tidy 11 points on three made three-pointers.

The quarter was competitive until the final three minutes. After Pacôme Dadiet (yes, him!) drilled a triple to pull the Knicks within one, Cleveland rattled off a 10-0 run, powered by five consecutive points from Meleek Thomas, two Thomas Bryant free throws, and a Donovan Mitchell and-one. The latter easily had the biggest impact on the quarter, tallying 14 points, three dimes, and a steal. As for New York's bench support, Dadiet (7 PTS, 14 MIN) provided positive defensive efforts with a block and a steal, but neither Tyler Kolek nor Andre Drummond moved the needle. New York managed just six points over the final five-ish minutes, and Cleveland closed the quarter ahead, 34-23.

To start the second, the good guys outscored the villains by ten, chiseling an 11-point deficit to one. Credit Towns and his four points, two dimes, and two blocks for the comeback. Mohamed Diawara lent a triple, too, and Mikal Bridges found his rhythm, blasting out seven fast points. Naturally, Cleveland responded with a 9-2 stretch when James Harden (28 PTS, 1-8 3PT) finally got on the board. Dadiet drizzled water on our good feelings by missing three straight shots, and Brunson still couldn't regain his touch, going scoreless from the field in the period.

Playing from 55-44 down with four minutes left in the half, the hosts posted 11 unanswered points, eight of which belonged to Bridges. He'd finish the half with 17 points. This, my friends, is what's known as a circus shot:

Despite dealing with a sore shoulder, Hart picked Harden's pocket twice, converting one steal into a layup. Unfortunately, after Bridges tied the game at 55, Donovan Mitchell (30 PTS, 1-7 3PT) and Harden combined for six unanswered points, aided by a Bridges turnover. The home team won the quarter 33-27 but entered intermission trailing 61-56.

Through the half, Harden and Mitchell combined for 34 points in the half, while their bench contributed 13. New York had shot better from the field (54% to 49%) and significantly better from deep (50% to 31%), they were on the wrong end of the score due to a glaring free-throw disparity. New York had attempted just eight freebies, while Cleveland shot more than twice that. The Cavs outscored won the paint, 28-16, and the boards. Another glaring stat: Cleveland converted six Knicks miscues into 13 points, while New York squeezed just two points out of five giveaways.

Cleveland opened the second half on an 11-5 run to restore its double-digit lead. Down 74-65 midway through the period, the Knicks got a boost from an unexpected source: Tyler Nickel. The rookie sank a triple, followed by another from Landry Shamet, to cut the deficit to three. Nickel contributed five points but also committed four fouls in under six minutes—that will get straightened out with experience. Harden extended Cleveland's advantage to 85-77 with under three minutes remaining. But then another unexpected player pitched in: James Wiseman provided some valuable minutes off the bench, finishing the game with six points and seven boards in six minutes. Shamet helped the Knicks win the quarter 26-24 and enter the fourth trailing 85-82.

The Knicks opened the fourth quarter on a 12-0 run, turning a three-point deficit into a nine-point advantage in less than three minutes. Jose Alvarado (a team-high 21 points, 8-10 FG, 19 MIN) was the catalyst, drilling consecutive triples and picking Donovan Mitchell's pocket to set up a Dadiet layup. Hart took a turn orchestrating the offense, collecting four assists during the opening surge. The Ohio Players missed their first five shots, four of them by Mitchell, and committed two turnovers as the Knicks piled on the points.

Wiseman (!) helped goose New York's lead to 13 with four points and three offensive rebounds in a brief fourth-quarter stint. When Mitchell and Harden scored five unanswered points to trim the deficit to eight, Alvarado responded with a jumper, and Jalen Brunson buried a triple, his only basket of the quarter. Alvarado was the unquestionable star of the fourth, scoring 16 of his 21 points, including four three-pointers. Hart was nearly as impactful, contributing eight points, six assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block in the frame. The Knicks closed on a 7-0 run, their defense holding Cleveland scoreless over the final two minutes and change.

Welcome back to associate head coach Chris Jent, who returned to the sidelines after a month away.

The Knicks hang around town to face the Heat on Friday in the last of their first-stage Cup games. Mo is folding and pressing your recap. Happy Knicksgiving, turkey fans.

#2825. Nets topple Bucks in NBA Cup clash to snap five-game skid

The teams with the two worst records in the Eastern Conference faced each other Wednesday night in Milwaukee, but no one needed to accuse either the Nets or the Bucks of tanking in this one.

Neither team can win the draft lottery after the season because both franchises essentially traded their respective 2027 first-round picks years ago, but the Nets at least halted their five-game losing streak with a defensively improved 104-97 victory in an NBA Cup game at Fiserv Forum.

"Just do it again. Every time we have a good performance where we fight and we compete, we follow it up with some bulls–t, with some bad ones," Noah Clowney told the YES Network after the game. "So we're trying to stack it this time and keep two or three good ones in a row."

Julius Randle netted a team-high 28 points as the Nets (4-13) posted their first NBA Cup victory after losses earlier in the season against the Pistons and the Magic.

Julius Randle, who scored a team-high 28 points, shoots a floater during the Nets' 104-97 win over the Bucks on Nov. 25, 2026, in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Michael Porter Jr. was held to 12 points on 4-for-14 shooting with 11 rebounds to follow another quiet scoring night (11) Monday in Minnesota.

Egor Dëmin had an active game with 15 points and a plus-15 rating in 25 minutes off the bench.

Clowney nailed three 3-pointers among his 17 points, and Day'Ron Sharpe contributed 11 points, 14 boards and five assists for the Nets.

Tyler Herro, who had a career-best 50-point game Saturday against the 76ers, scored 21 of his 28 points in the second half for the Bucks (2-17).

"We've lost a few in a row, so we've gotta try to get a win no matter who we're playing against," Porter told YES before the game. "They're an NBA team. They are at the bottom of the standings right now, but so are we, so it's gonna be a dogfight, and we gotta try to get out of here with a win."

The NBA instituted rule changes in the offseason to discourage tanking for draft position, essentially penalizing the teams with the worst three records in the league with lower odds to secure the No. 1 overall pick in the lottery.

The Nets' 2027 first-round pick is controlled by the Rockets from an unprotected pick swap as part of Brooklyn's deal to acquire James Harden in January 2021.

The Bucks, who dealt away superstar forward Giannis Antetokounmpo to the Heat over the summer, also had committed to a pick swap involving their 2027 first-rounder when they acquired guard Jrue Holiday from New Orleans in 2020.

Noah Clowney, who scored 17 points off the bench, puts up a shot during the second quarter of the Nets' road win over the Bucks. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

The Nets had allowed at least 70 points in the first half four times during their five-game skid, but this slow start was more due to offensive inefficiency.

Brooklyn missed five of its first six shots and spotted the Bucks a 9-2 lead in the first three minutes.

But Porter knocked down two straight 3s as part of a 15-1 run en route to a 28-24 edge through 12 minutes.

A 3-pointer by Clowney and a jumper by Randle extended the cushion to 10 early in the second. The Nets led by as many as 13 before the Bucks closed within 51-47 before intermission.

"We have to close quarters better," Randle, who had 16 in the half, told YES.

Rookie point guard Mikel Brown Jr. scored all six of his points in the second half after posting 17 in his first NBA start Monday in Minnesota.

Brown finally recorded his first two buckets after seven misses to push Brooklyn back ahead before two late dunks by Sharpe and a traditional 3-point play by Dëmin helped the Nets carry a 77-71 edge into the final quarter.

"It all came from the defensive side. I was more locked in, and everything else came to me naturally," Dëmin said.

Day'Ron Sharpe, who scored 11 points and grabbed 14 rebounds, slams home a dunk during the Nets' win over the Bucks in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Keon Ellis (plus-14) and Grant Nelson provided some defensive energy off the bench to open the fourth, and Porter converted an and-1 to stretch the lead back to 11 with 10:38 remaining.

"Overall, proud of the effort. Our guys played hard," Nets coach Jordi Fernández said. "Guys were asking for subs, and that tells me that everybody did everything possible.

"That's how it's supposed to be … and that's who we want to be. Now let's see if we can play this hard two games in a row."

Moritz Wagner didn't play in the second half due to what the Nets said was left knee soreness.

#2822. Steelers owner Art Rooney II says Aaron Rodgers was not a 'good teammate,' expresses confidence in Mike McCarthy

The Pittsburgh Steelers are feeling good about their decision-making following the fallout from Aaron Rodgers' appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday. Rodgers was promptly released from the team and head coach Mike McCarthy was retained despite some scandalous accusations from his former quarterback.

While it was overall a pretty awful day for the team, Steelers owner Art Rooney II expressed optimism Thursday, saying he has "100 percent confidence in Mike McCarthy," via an interview with the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette.

Rooney also spoke about the decision to release Rodgers, saying the quarterback was not being a "good teammate" when he publicly aired the team's grievances following his benching, per the Post-Gazette.

"There's maybe a time and place after the season if he wanted to kind of unload," Rooney said. "He talks about wanting to be a good teammate, but you don't do that to your teammates a couple days before the game, that's for sure."

Then he added, "In general, I think, historically, what's in the locker room stays in the locker room. That's the way it's always been. That's the way it should be as far as I'm concerned."

Rodgers didn't take that track. Instead, the quarterback ripped his head coach relentlessly during an appearance on McAfee's show. Rodgers claimed McCarthy routinely fell asleep during meetings, led the team in a "1-2-3 Packers" chant and questioned the coach's physical health.

Rooney addressed that final accusation, saying McCarthy is "as healthy as the rest of us." The owner added, "health is not an issue."

Rooney then further continued to express support for McCarthy, saying the coach took criticism for changes Rodgers made during games, particularly in the red zone.

"The sad thing is Mike took some of the bullets for Aaron in terms of some of the plays that were called, particularly in the red-zone troubles that we had," Rooney said. "Mike took some of the bullets for things that were going wrong down there. He tried to protect him, and it just wasn't working."

Rooney then confirmed that Rodgers was released after trying to deliver an ultimatum to McCarthy. Rodgers reportedly said he wanted to call offensive plays or be released. McCarthy told Rodgers he doesn't get to make those calls, and decided to bench the veteran with the intention of releasing him in the coming days. Rodgers apparently fast-tracked that release with his interview on Wednesday.

The owner also expressed confidence in now-starter Mason Rudolph, saying the team was "excited and lucky" to have him step into the starting role.

At 6-4, the Steelers are still very much in the playoff hunt in the AFC. And after everything that occurred Wednesday, it might take a playoff appearance to salvage McCarthy's tenure with the franchise.

Rooney may have given the coach a vote of confidence, but Rodgers' words will be tough to ignore if the team continues to struggle on the field.

#2820. Vance says he was assured Jensen Huang was not under DOJ investigation

Vice President JD Vance said Wednesday that Attorney General Todd Blanche told him Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang was not under investigation.

"I was told by the Department of Justice and multiple officials in our national security apparatus that he was under no investigation," Vance told reporters in Ohio on Wednesday after being asked what assurances he had gotten that Huang was not under investigation. "Not even that he was about to be prosecuted; that he's under no investigation."

Vance spoke two days after President Donald Trump told CNBC in an interview that an investigation had been done.

Trump was asked what Blanche had told him about potential criminal liability for Huang and the question of whether Nvidia chips had ended up in China in violation of U.S. export laws.

"They did a pretty good investigation, and they felt that he, himself, was exonerated," Trump said Monday.

Reuters reported Wednesday that the Department of Justice did not conduct a formal investigation into Huang before Trump pushed for his reinstatement as CEO.

Blanche had concluded there was no evidence Huang had broken any laws based on investigations into Nvidia clients Super Micro Computer and Megaspeed, according to the report.

The DOJ did not immediately respond to a request for comment on the Reuters report. And the White House didn't immediately respond to a request for comment on Vance saying Huang was under no investigation when Trump had said there was one. Nvidia didn't immediately respond to a request for comment about Vance's remarks.

Blanche on Tuesday dodged questions about whether there had been an investigation.

"What the president said is that he talked to a lot of people going into the decision that he made to encourage the board to reinstate him," Blanche said after being asked whether the DOJ had investigated Huang and, if so, what were the results of the probe.

Huang was reinstated as CEO of Nvidia on Monday, after the chipmaker's board of directors said it had received "input from senior levels of the United States government" that Huang was not a national security risk. Trump took credit for Huang's reinstatement in the interview with CNBC shortly after Nvidia's announcement.

Huang resigned following The New York Times reporting multiple affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who is alleged to be a spy for Beijing and who was an executive for the Singapore-based cloud provider Megaspeed.

The reporting added fuel to allegations that Nvidia should have known its artificial intelligence chips were being diverted to China and being used by Chinese companies, violating U.S. export laws.

"The product they create is a very important part of us staying ahead of the SI arms race with China. We really do care about ensuring those chips do not fall into the wrong hands," Vance said, using the Trump administration's preferred acronym for AI, which the president calls "super intelligence."

"We actually see this is an opportunity for Nvidia to focus on complying with American export controls, and we think Jensen Huang is going to take that very seriously," Vance continued.

"We received the assurances that he was not under any significant — he was under no investigation — and the accusations against him were based on anonymous reporting, not on actual evidence," Vance said.

On Tuesday, Trump left the door open to pardon Huang in an interview with the New York Post, but Vance said on Wednesday that the CEO doesn't need one.

"I don't think he needs a pardon at all," Vance said. "I haven't seen any evidence again that he committed a crime. And if there is evidence that he committed a crime, then that's up to relevant authorities to actually bring a prosecution against him."

Huang is scheduled to appear for a closed-door interview with House committees about his dealings with Megaspeed on Tuesday. At the same time, Nvidia will hold its annual conference where it showcases new technology in Washington.

— Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.

Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy has the support of team president and owner Art Rooney after Aaron Rodgers accused his now former coach of sleeping on the job.

Rooney made it clear that McCarthy's job is safe in a statement to the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette on Thursday — and he called out Rodgers for "insubordination" regarding the quarterback blasting McCarthy during Wednesday's installment of "The Pat McAfee Show."

"I just want to say that I have 100% confidence in Mike McCarthy and I expect he'll be our coach this season, next season and beyond," Rooney said. Pittsburgh Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy speaks during a news conference after an NFL football game in Philadelphia, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. AP Photo/Chris Szagola

Rooney explained that Rodgers was not being a "good teammate" by publicly bashing McCarthy two days before their Black Friday game against the Broncos.

"It's easy to say, knowing what I know today, what would I do? But we had a good experience last year, and so I kind of wasn't expecting it to go this way this year."

McCarthy coached Rodgers with the Packers from 2006 through December 2018 and they won a Super Bowl by beating the Steelers to cap the 2010 season.

#2815. Rapid Recap: Nets 104, Bucks 97

The Milwaukee Bucks follow a string of solid games with one of their worst in a long time, losing at home to the Brooklyn Nets, 104-97. Tyler Herro led the Bucks with 28 points and five assists, while Kel'el Ware led the Bucks bench with 13 points and 14 rebounds. Julius Randle led the Nets with 28 points, and Noah Clowney led their bench with 17 points.

The game started with the Bucks on the attack, forcing three personal fouls in the first 90 seconds and exploiting the mismatches created when four forwards and one guard are the opposing starting five. Myles Turner wasted no time pulling out his three-ball from Toronto as the Bucks quickly built a 9-2 lead. But of course, the Nets started finding their shot as the Bucks let their feet off the gas, going on a 16-2 run as Milwaukee's offence dried up like the Sahara Desert. The Bucks didn't score another field goal until the 4:08 mark, when Jaime Jaquez Jr. finally buried a three, which set off an 8-2 run that included a sweet baseline dunk by Kel'el Ware. Despite being in the bonus for more than eight minutes, the Bucks couldn't take advantage, shooting only six free throws for the quarter and ending the period down 28-24.

The second quarter started where the first quarter ended: with Ryan Rollins throwing up ill-advised shots—he was bad in this one. Meanwhile, the Nets steadily built a 41-28 lead through the first five minutes. Thankfully, the young Bucks off the bench played their typical brand of high-energy defense and held Brooklyn to six points over the next five minutes, but unfortunately, the home side couldn't buy a bucket themselves. Milwaukee did at least outscore Brooklyn 11-4 in the final few minutes to cut the deficit to four, with Ware giving fans a reason to get hyped with a monster dunk before Jaquez scored a quick lay-up to end the half on a high note. Milwaukee headed into the locker room down 47-51.

Coming out of halftime, the Bucks kept up the pace and quickly regained the lead, largely thanks to 11 quick points from Herro, and it finally felt like the home team had found a flow. Unfortunately, that flow ended as Rollins decided to dominate the ball, force shots, and make head-scratching decisions in general. Eventually, the Nets crept back ahead after another scoring drought by the Bucks late in the third quarter. If not for Ware imposing his will in the paint, the Nets may have run away with it. Instead, the big man finished the third quarter with 10 points and 10 rebounds, scoring the final four to keep the deficit at six, 77-71.

The Nets extended their lead to 10 pretty quickly to open the final frame, as the Bucks struggled to get paint touches, something that seemed like it would come easier after getting in the paint so effectively against Philadelphia and Toronto. Milwaukee made a few pushes, but each one ended in an unforced error; they just couldn't get out of their own way. The game ended ugly as the Bucks turned it over nine times in the fourth quarter alone to give this one away. One of the more disappointing efforts of the last few years, frankly, and that's saying something.

26: the number of free throw attempts the Nets had, which allowed them to hang in the game despite their horrid shooting.

Ryan Rollins needs to be glued to the bench for a game (or five).

#2813. U.S. proposes new tariffs on 14 trading partners, including China and the EU

The U.S. is preparing new tariffs on 14 economies, including China, the EU, Japan and India, following investigations into what the Trump administration says are excess manufacturing practices that disadvantage U.S. commerce.

The administration launched those and other trade investigations in March, weeks after the Supreme Court unraveled a major piece of President Donald Trump's tariff-heavy economic policy by striking down his "liberation day" duties. Trump has since been accused by critics of his tariffs of trying to revive the canceled duties using other legal authorities.

The latest action targets foreign economies that create, sustain or contribute to structural excess capacity and production in manufacturing sectors that the U.S. finds unreasonable and burdensome or restrictive to its commerce, a senior U.S. official told reporters Tuesday evening.

The new tariffs are "targeted," not blanket rates, and will vary for each economy, and many will not exceed the rates established in previously reached trade deals, said the official, who spoke on condition of anonymity to discuss the forthcoming duties.

The rates, which mostly range from 15% to 20%, are "not really additive" and generally will not stack on top of existing duties, U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer told CNBC's "Squawk Box" Wednesday morning.

"In effect, it'll look more like a top up," Greer said. "When it all nets out, it really will just top up a few percentage points."

An exception is China, which faces a 20% rate under the new tariff policy, "so it will go up some," Greer said. But at the same time, both countries are moving to remove tariffs on some of each other's goods in what Greer called "stability-generating activity through this action."

The senior U.S. official said Tuesday that the 20% rate will come on top of the so-called forced technology transfer-related tariffs of roughly 25%, which were implemented during Trump's first presidential term. U.S. tariffs on Chinese goods will therefore reach about 45%, give or take for different goods, the official said.

Average U.S. tariffs rates on Chinese goods stood at about 36.5% as of July, according to the Congressional Research Service.

Greer also said on CNBC that the new policy likely will not take effect for months.

"We've talked to our trading partners about this. They know what we're proposing," he said. "I think a lot of this is baked in, and I don't think any of these would actually take effect earlier than a couple months from now."

The administration says the tariffs target what it calls "overcapacity" — excess industrial production, often supported by government subsidies, that allows cheaper goods to flood global markets and undercut U.S. producers.

China has disputed the allegations. "The so-called 'overcapacity' does not really exist," China's foreign ministry said Wednesday before the official reports were released by the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative, according to Reuters.

The overcapacity levies are being imposed under Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974. The Trump administration invoked the same statute to launch investigations into 60 economies over alleged forced-labor issues.

Those investigations, which also began in March, led to broad tariffs of 10% to 12.5% on goods from dozens of countries covering 99.4% of U.S. imports.

The Section 301 actions came weeks after the Supreme Court struck down the near-global tariffs that Trump had enacted under the International Emergency Economic Powers Act.

Shortly after the court's ruling, Trump invoked Section 122 of the Trade Act of 1974 to impose a 10% global tariff. That tariff expired after 150 days, and was immediately replaced with the forced-labor-related duties.

An ongoing lawsuit accuses the Trump administration of citing forced-labor issues as a "pretext" in pursuit of reviving Trump's worldwide tariff regime.

#2811. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2809. Falcons/Vikings Coverage Map: Wide Broadcast Area for Kirk Cousins Return

This Sunday's game at U.S. Bank Stadium between the Atlanta Falcons and the Minnesota Vikings will feature Kirk Cousins' return to the Twin Cities as the Vikings' quarterback to take on the team he left Minnesota for before the 2024 season. The folks from 506 Sports have released their broadcast maps for this Week 12, and the FOX map for this week is below. The Falcons/Vikings game is represented by the red area.

There's your broadcast coverage map for Week 12, folks. Hopefully there are quite a few of you who are in the red area on the map for this week's game.

#2808. Taking on Starlink: Europe’s largest satellite factory opens in push to rival Musk

The largest satellite factory in Europe was inaugurated in Belgium this week, boosting the continent's efforts to challenge Elon Musk's Starlink and reduce reliance on U.S. space infrastructure.

Privately held Belgian satellite manufacturer Aerospacelab secured a 2.4-billion-euro ($2.6 billion) contract in September to manufacture 264 of the 348 satellites planned for IRIS², the European Union's answer to SpaceX's Starlink business.

Aerospacelab said this will "form the backbone of Europe's sovereign, secure, and resilient connectivity infrastructure by 2030." It will be named Ectro from Nov. 30.

Its new oval-shaped, 20,000-square-meter facility in southern Belgium, inaugurated by Belgian King Philippe, will be able to produce up to 500 satellites annually, which would make it the world's third-largest satellite production site, according to the Belgian royal palace.

Employees are expected to move into the megafactory in January, and production is slated to begin by the end of 2028, according to AFP.

Satellite connectivity has become increasingly important to everyday life as well as national security.

It provides internet access to remote locations and defense equipment like warships and fighter jets, and lets governments and militaries communicate securely where Earth-based networks are unavailable or disrupted.

The importance of such capabilities has become particularly apparent in Ukraine, as the country's military relies heavily on Starlink connectivity to fight Russian forces. Recent restrictions to the service over Russian-occupied territory reported by Ukrainian officials further underscored the way geopolitical conflict may depend on commercial businesses.

But Europe still has a long way to go in challenging Starlink's dominance.

SpaceX says it has more than 11,000 Starlink satellites in low Earth orbit, making it by far the largest satellite constellation in the world, and plans up to 100,000 satellites.

Among the challengers is Amazon, whose satellite business, Amazon Leo, has deployed over 400 satellites and plans an initial in-orbit constellation of more than 3,200.

Meanwhile, French Eutelsat operates more than 600 satellites in low Earth orbit through its OneWeb constellation. The company is also part of the SpaceRISE consortium alongside SES and Hispasat, tasked with developing and operating IRIS².

The European Commission has called IRIS² a "strategic asset to achieve space sovereignty in the face of commercial satellite constellations, such as Starlink." The aim is for first launches to take place by the end of 2029, with initial services expected soon after.

In August, the Commission and SpaceRISE consortium announced they were expanding the main constellation from 282 to 348 satellites, increasing its secure communications capacity for EU governments by 60%.

Defense and security applications were the main drivers of the expanded scope, the Commission said.

#2807. Nets snap a five game losing streak, beat the Bucks 104-97

The Brooklyn Nets and Milwaukee Bucks came into tonight's game like two school kids trotting onto the playground, one pointing and chirping at the other "you're the worst defense," while the other bickered back "no, you're the worst defense."

Each youngster had some merit to their argument. Brooklyn had just given up 130+ in three of their last four games, and ranked dead last in defensive rating ahead of the contest. Milwaukee ranked behind them by a few fractions of a point.

Brooklyn would eventually win the squabble, and the game, but it'd take them some time to get there, not that anyone's complaining here.

Brooklyn came out the gates without a whole lot of ball pressure. In fairness, they had Danny Wolf matched up initially against Tyler Herro, which leaves you very little spatial insurance to work with. However, the Nets also looked out of sync rotating around the Milwaukee offense, which didn't help either. As Brooklyn shot 1-6 from the field and the Bucks pranced out to a 9-2 lead, it just looked like more of the same in the early going.

As I mentioned, we'd eventually find out otherwise. And while real turnaround would soon come on the defensive end, things had to start for Brooklyn at the other.

After Milwaukee's opening jolt, the Nets went on a 13-0 run in the heart of the first to go up 22-12. Michael Porter Jr., who spoke to Brain Lewis this week about how he's struggled to "find his rhythm," was in his grove amid the stretch, putting in eight first period points.

Getting five from Day'Ron Sharpe and Julius Randle each, as well as eight points off the bench in the period, the Nets led after one by a 28-24 margin.

Despite being less of a scorer this time around, Mikel Brown Jr. looked comfortable out there early on as well, starting for a second straight game. He shot 0-5 from the field in the first half but clocked in as a +4 with two assists with zero turnovers.

Also like last game, Grant Nelson joined him on the floor for a few minutes in the second. Nelson only finished with one point, but grabbed six rebounds, contributing to a strong collective effort from the team on the glass, where the Nets won 52-44. Porter Jr. also grabbed 11 rebounds, his most in a game all season if you discount the overtime affair vs Boston.

Brooklyn would need every one of those boards especially in the first half, where neither team shot it at a particularly high clip, as the Nets posted .400/.348 splits, while the Bucks had .432/.273 marks.

At the break, Brooklyn led 51-47, largely thanks to Randle, who had 16 points on 7-10 shooting from the field and 2-4 from deep. MPJ went quiet in the second, still rocking with eight points by then. Day'Ron Sharpe put us on early triple-double watch with five points, 10 rebounds, and four assists at the break.

Herro, however, had all the stats to begin the third. He poured in nine points during the period's first four minutes to put his team back in the lead for the first time since the 6:57 mark of the first. The Nets, meanwhile, opened the period 3-12 from the field and 0-5 from deep. Two of the makes came from Brown Jr, so at least there's that.

Sarcasm aside, as one young guard for Brooklyn started to turn it on, another followed. After Milwaukee looked ready to pull away, Egor Dëmin checked in, scoring five points himself and assisting on two baskets in just a minute and a half. His effort helped ignited a 13-2 run that put the lead back in Brooklyn's hands.

At the same time, Randle continued to mine inside for points. He'd eventually go for 28 in the game on 10-16 shooting. That not only gave Brooklyn consistent offense, but slowed things down and allowed them to set up easily at the other end. Even with Herro's early offensive excellence, the Bucks went 7-23 from the field in the quarter, and the Nets led going into the fourth by a 77-71 score.

"I think everybody was able to just put their minds together and not just focus on team defense, but their own island, like how we say 'guard your yard,'" Dëmin said. "I think that's where it came from. Everything else was easier because everybody was so locked in and super focused on how to guard the man in front of you rather than relying on someone else around."

And believe it or not, in that period, the defense continued to improve. On Milwaukee's second possession of the frame, Porter Jr. poked the a ball away in the backcourt, which Nelson dove across mid court to save. Still, the Bucks got it up and forward, but then Keon Ellis came charging over, intercepting the rock and then dishing it ahead Porter Jr. for an and-one finish. That was just one of the Nets seven steals in the fourth.

"Go out there and play with physicality," Sharpe said. "Go out there and play with ball pressure. Execute with staples and have each others back. I felt like we did a great job at that tonight."

Sharpe never got the triple double, but did finish 11 points, five assists, and 14 rebounds while shooting 5-10 from the field and hitting a three.

The Nets got up by 11 early in the fourth. After a few turnovers early in the frame, the Bucks cut it back down to eight, and we looked in store for a tight finish.

But in a game where Brooklyn's supposed fatal flaw had carried them, it was only right that Noah Clowney, who's struggled with his shot all year, iced things out. In the fourth, he added nine points on 4-6 shooting, scoring from all over the court to keep the lead afloat. He finished with a season-high 17 points, also grabbing five rebounds while shooting 7-13 from the field.

"This is the Noah Clowney that we need," Fernández said. "It gives us a starter off the bench and it's very, very good for this group, because it brings a player on both ends."

Dëmin, who's also had a difficult start to the year, leveraged his size to play some inspiring defense down the stretch as well, finishing as a +5 in the quarter. For the game, he had 15 points, four assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block while shooting 5-11 from the field.

"Yeah, he went back to doing the simple things, and it started with his physicality on defense," Fernández said. "They felt him the whole game. His length. He can rebound, he can be in the gaps…Proud of him for sure."

Sure, the Nets still turned it over on back-t0-back possession with the Bucks only down 11 and under a minute to go. Dëmin missed also missed an open dunk. They're still a young, flawed team. They didn't solve all their issues tonight, but they did win the night, and snapped their five game losing streak.

Final: Brooklyn Nets 104, Milwaukee Bucks 97

Mo Wagner subbed out at the end of the first period and did not return for the rest of the game. At half, the Nets ruled him out with left knee soreness.

"I don't know. I was about to sub him in, and I saw him limping, and I was like I got to save him from himself," Fernández said. "He was there to do whatever it took for his team and his teammates, and at the end I decided to not bring him back in. Obviously, I don't know what it is, but the good thing, the great thing is he was cheering for everybody. He was cheering fro Grant [Nelson] the whole time, and that means a lot for the group, and it speaks to the type of person he is."

We'll provide updates on him once they're made available.

Day'Ron Sharpe had a season-high 14 rebounds tonight and also recorded his 5th double-double of the season with 11 points. It matches his double-double total from last season, tied as the 2nd most in his career (6, 2023-24). This was his 7th career game with 10+ PTS, 10+ REB and 5+ AST.

Noah Clowney had a season-high 17 points off the bench tonight against the Bucks. He has tied his season high with three 3-pointers made.

Michael Porter Jr. logged his third double-double of the season with 12 points and 11 rebounds. It is his 15th time recording at least 10 rebounds as a Net.

Brooklyn gets right back in action after the holiday with a road matchup against the Toronto Raptors on Friday. The North has lost seven of their last eight, including a contest to the Bucks on Monday. They're certainly looking at this one as a "get right" game. The game tips off at 7:30 p.m. EST.

#2806. Latin America has the copper and energy the AI build-out needs. Infrastructure is the test

Latin America’s copper, energy and mineral resources could play a growing role in supplying the global artificial-intelligence build-out, as data centers and electrification drive demand for power and raw materials.

But for investors, it's less about whether Latin America has the resources than whether governments can turn them into viable projects.

"We are going through an acceleration in energy demand growth. We need every possible energy source available," Michele Della Vigna, head of natural resources research for Goldman Sachs, told CNBC. "Ultimately, what drives economic growth is energy," he added.

Ben Kumar, head of strategy for wealth, investment and public policy at 7IM, suggested Latin America could benefit, given the physical materials needed for AI equipment and data centers. "We are currently neutral in the short term, but if the materials and mining cycle continues, we could see this moving to positive in a few months," he said.

The investment case varies across the region. Chile has copper; Argentina is expanding oil and gas exports; and Brazil has abundant renewable power and significant rare earth reserves.

Chile's copper sector illustrates both the supply potential and the constraints to expanding supply. Copper production fell 8.5% in the first nine months of 2026, according to the state copper commission Cochilco, as lower ore grades, maintenance, and operational disruptions constrained supply.

But demand for refined copper is set to grow 1.6% this year and 2% next year, according to the International Copper Study Group. Supply is struggling to keep pace with demand from expanding electricity grids.

Chile's copper commission has raised its 2027 price forecast to $5.95 a pound, from $5.10. Codelco, the state-owned copper producer, may also have scope to increase supply, including through proposed public-private partnerships.

Rafael Ch, partner and senior analyst for Latin America and emerging markets at Signum Global Advisors, sees demand continuing to grow. "As countries expand electricity grids, they need more copper regardless of which power source they choose," he said.

The scale of the opportunity will depend in part on how quickly energy demand grows. Goldman Sachs forecasts that global power demand for data centers will grow by 170% by 2030. The IEA projected in April that global data center electricity consumption would rise from 485 terawatt-hours in 2025 to 950 TWh in 2030.

The investment case also depends on whether the region can capture more value from the resources it produces. Latin America and the Caribbean refined only 26% of the copper they mined in 2025, according to the IEA.

"Cautious optimism is justified and there will be massive critical mineral demand," said Monica de Bolle, senior fellow at the Peterson Institute for International Economics.

"But improving refining capacity will be crucial to capture more of that value," she added.

Some projects have already secured financing. Chilean explorer Marimaca Copper Corp selected a preferred lender for a $400 million debt facility to build a mine on Oct. 28. The funding will go towards developing an open-pit mine in the north of Chile that will produce 50,000 metric tons of refined copper annually. Production is expected from 2029.

Argentina shows the other side of the opportunity: strong oil and gas resources, but insufficient infrastructure to get more production to global markets.

"I see an opportunity in the infrastructure that gets Argentine oil and gas to market. A producer in Vaca Muerta can have excellent wells and still struggle to expand exports without pipeline capacity and access to ports.

That gives investors a reason to look at transport and export infrastructure alongside production," Rafael Ch, partner and senior analyst for Latin America and emerging markets at Signum Global Advisors, added. Vaca Muerta is Argentina's vast shale basin.

Argentina is also attracting investment in drilling and export infrastructure. International capital markets have reopened to investment in drilling and pipelines in Argentina. That investment has already driven the build-out of a pipeline and port. Argentina will then be able to export far more crude oil. Chevron and American oil tycoon Harold Hamm have also increased their investments in Argentina.

Brazil's case rests partly on its renewable-heavy power mix. About 89% of the country's electricity was generated from renewable sources in 2025, according to the country's Ministry of Mines and Energy.

"That puts Brazil in a strong position to benefit as AI and data centers drive up demand," Stephanie Link, chief investment strategist and portfolio manager at Hightower Advisors, told CNBC.

But while Brazil has the world's second-largest reported rare-earth reserves, its estimated mine production is far smaller than China's. USGS estimates put Brazil's output at 2,000 metric tons in 2025, compared with China's 270,000 metric tons. Brazil's reserves are estimated at 11 million metric tons by the USGS, second only to China's 44 million metric tons.

For investors, the gap between Brazil's vast reserves and limited current production is both the opportunity and the execution risk.

For investors, the policy question is whether governments can make projects viable and keep them on track.

Luis Costa, Citi's global head of emerging markets strategy, said governments need to offer incentives, particularly in the early stages of projects, because infrastructure lags the region's resource wealth. He also stressed that support must last: infrastructure projects can take several years to develop.

The World Bank's Latin America and the Caribbean Economic Update for 2026 projects growth for the region of 2.2%, and highlights the "restricted fiscal space, sticky inflation and high borrowing costs."

"The economic policy and fiscal policy needs to get in line as markets are globally becoming more sensitive to fiscal stories," said Costa.

Brazil will need to establish fiscal credibility to attract investment, particularly if higher public spending puts additional pressure on debt and borrowing costs. "If investors doubt the government can stabilize public debt, companies' long-term financing costs could stay high even as the central bank lowers its policy rate," Ch added.

With Argentina set for national elections in October 2027, political continuity will also matter for energy investment. "A return to tighter currency controls or persistent deficits would make companies more likely to delay projects, even in an oil field with attractive economics," said Ch.

Pro-business President Javier Milei faces a tight race for re-election, though a change in leadership would not necessarily undermine medium- to long-term investment.

"There is also the possibility of a pragmatic Peronist ticket emerging, which markets would be satisfied with," said Nicolas Jaquier, emerging-market fixed-income portfolio manager at Ninety One.

For those governing across Latin America, policies to unlock its wealth of resources will be key for it to emerge as a winner from the AI demand boom.

"Supply chain security is becoming more and more relevant, and if commodities take off Latin America can be a clear and obvious winner," said Kumar.

#2805. NFL honors John Madden with Thanksgiving-themed coins, Madden uniform patch for Thursday slate

Five years after the death of John Madden, the NFL is keeping the memory of the broadcaster's connection to Thanksgiving alive.

The league announced last week that a Madden coin, featuring Madden's silhouette on one side and a six-legged "turducken" (a chicken stuffed in a duck, stuffed in a turkey) on the other, will be used for coin flips for the three NFL games on Thanksgiving Day.

All players in those games — Detroit Lions-Chicago Bears, Dallas Cowboys-Philadelphia Eagles and Buffalo Bills-Kansas City Chiefs — will wear patches on their uniforms with the Madden silhouette. The Los Angeles Rams and Green Bay Packers, who played on Wednesday (or "Thanksgiving Eve," as the league calls it), also wore the patches.

Stencils honoring Madden will be placed in the end zones and along the sidelines of the three home fields during the matchup, and panels on the stadium wall will feature Madden in his coaching days with the Oakland Raiders.

Along with those Madden-themed additions, the MVP of each game will receive a trophy fashioned to look like a turkey leg-football amalgamation. The Thanksgiving MVPs will also earn a $10,000 donation from the NFL Foundation to a high school or youth football program in their name.

Madden, one of the defining figures of the sport as both a coach and broadcaster, died in 2021 at the age of 85. Alongside longtime broadcasting partner Pat Summerall, Madden called NFL games for decades on CBS and later Fox, becoming associated with Thanksgiving games thanks to his enthusiasm for turduckens and turkey legs.

The league first introduced the specialized Madden patches and coins in 2023, two years after the football legend's death, and have continued the tradition over the past few years.

#2804. Diving deeper into Cavs 121-102 loss to Knicks: Even when the stars are at their best, this team isn’t

The Cleveland Cavaliers looked like they could compete with the New York Knicks until they couldn't. Cleveland went into the final quarter with a narrow 85-82 advantage, but then lost the fourth 39-17 to drop this game 121-102.

19 points on 9-of-15 shooting, 8 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 block

Bridges covered up for pretty pedestrian performances from Jalen Brunson and Karl-Anthony Towns. Bridges provided efficient scoring, was an impactful rebounder, and was good defensively. This all added up to the Knicks winning his minutes by 33.

30 points on 12-of-24 shooting, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 steals, 2 blocks

Mitchell was unstoppable in the first half. His 14 points in the opening quarter and six more in the second put the Cavs in a good position to steal this game. Mitchell seemingly couldn't miss before the break, going 8-for-11 from the field with most of his damage coming at the rim.

The magic went away in the second half. Mitchell scored just 10 points on 4-of-13 shooting in the final two quarters. Cleveland then proceeded to lose those minutes by 15.

The Cavs needed a special performance from Mitchell to defeat the defending champions. They had that in the first half, but not the second.

15 points on 6-of-14 shooting, 12 assists, 7 rebounds, 3 steals, 2 blocks

Hart is a winning player. He does all the little things that championship-level teams need from their supporting cast. Rebounding, additional ball handling, defensive versatility, and ancillary playmaking are attributes you hope to accumulate in your entire supporting cast. Hart provides that as just one player.

Those skills were all on display against the Cavs. Hart rebounded well, forced turnovers, and was a useful facilitator. The Cavs have often decided to cheat off Hart offensively. He didn't make them pay as a shooter, but he used the extra space to find openings for his teammates.

On a night when both teams got similar contributions from their stars, it was the play of the supporting players, like Hart and Jose Alverado (21 points on 8-of-10 shooting), that made all the difference.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Browns throwback shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cleveland gear HERE.

5 points on 2-of-8 shooting, 1 rebound, 0 assists

Merrill has had a tough time finding his shot this season. There's many things Merrill does well, but those are mostly muted if the outside shot isn't falling, as was once again the case here.

Head coach Kenny Atkinson has asked a lot out of Merrill this season. He's been consistently forced to guard up a spot or two when he's on the court — particularly when he's been inserted into the starting lineup. That isn't an ideal place for him considering he's undersized for that role, and has been sharing the floor with guards that don't pull their weight defensively.

I don't envy Merrill. He's been continually put into positions to fail this season.

10 points on 5-of-7 shooting, 5 rebounds, 1 assist

Allen is supposed to be a key member of this team, but you wouldn't necessarily know that from watching games like this. The Cavs need Allen to be a force inside as a roller and rebounder with Evan Mobley out of the lineup. He wasn't either of those things, and played with little to no energy, which is why the Cavs lost his minutes by 32.

The Cavs are built around their guards. If that backcourt is going to work on both ends, they need their interior players to consistently show up. They haven't, which is one of the many reasons for the underwhelming start to the season.

The Cavs benefited greatly from Bryant's energy and willingness to be physical. Both of those things stood out on a team that is in desperate need of interior size and presence. It's no coincidence the Cavs played their best basketball of the evening when he was on the court.

28 points on 7-of-19 shooting, 5 assists, 1 rebound

This was a solid night from Harden. He kept the offense moving, took smart shots, and continually forced his way to the line (13-for-14). A poor shooting night from three (1-for-8) kept this from being a great offensive game.

Defensively, the Cavs had some success with hiding him on Towns. Harden held his own in that matchup, resulting in the defense not completely falling apart with him on the floor.

Harden did a good job of forcing his way inside. The offense is in a much better place when he can consistently do that.

Jose Alverado scored as many points off the bench (21) as all five of Cleveland's reserves combined.

This was a bad night from Cleveland's supporting cast, particularly their bench. Tyrese Proctor had just two points on 1-of-6 shooting, Jaylon Tyson went 2-for-8 from the field with four turnovers, and Mario Hezonja registered no points in 10 minutes.

Conversely, New York got key contributions from their role players, including Alverado. He scored 16 of his 21 points in the fourth to help put the Cavs away.

This team makes much more sense on paper than it does on the court. The Cavs have more well-rounded role players than they have had in recent years. This has shown through when they get opportunities to shine. Yet, that may not be what works with this particular core.

Typically, the Cavs are at their best when Mitchell and Harden are playing well, as they were on Wednesday. This formula has been the recipe for their top performances, but the Cavs didn't come close to pulling off the upset because they got next to no help from their supporting cast. No player besides the starting backcourt scored more than 10 points. It's nearly impossible to win games against an elite opponent when your role players add nothing.

Somehow, the Cavs have constructed a team that relies heavily on their guards to be at their best to win alongside a supporting cast that doesn't really fit with how the guards play. That is a problem, and how you get nights like this where Peyton Watson and Mario Hezonja combine to take just five shots from the field.

The Cavs are pot committed to this backcourt both in terms of their recent contract extensions and how they play. Atkinson hasn't indicated that he wants to move away from this isolation- and high pick-and-roll-heavy offense that caters specifically to the two guards. But that style isn't conducive to a team that has struggled to knock down catch-and-shoot threes (36.3%, 16th in the league), doesn't have a versatile defense, and features two bigs that need to be force-fed to be effective offensively.

Koby Altman has built an interesting supporting cast. On paper, it would fit nicely with a team that played like the 2024-25 Cavs. However, this isn't that team. Any hopes of playing that way went out the window when they traded for Harden last winter.

Cleveland has been one of the most inconsistent groups this season. Few teams have as impressive highs and as disappointing lows. Many factors contribute to that, but one of them is that the puzzle pieces don't really fit together. Or at the very least, they don't fit with how they're being used now.

There's no easy fix for this. Getting a team to drastically change how they play 19 games into the season is nearly impossible. At the same time, there isn't an easy way to pivot off either the star or supporting players due to their contracts. It's difficult to see a trade drastically changing the outlook of this team. They're more or less committed to this current group and playstyle. And I'm just not sure what the pathway is to being a legit title contender, which is what you would hope they'd be coming off a run to the Eastern Conference Finals.

Even when the stars are at or near their best, this team isn't.

#2803. Mike Brown Explains Decision to Play Tyler Nickel in Win vs Cavaliers

Forward Tyler Nickel wasn't on head coach Mike Brown's minutes sheet Wednesday night, but once the New York Knicks fell behind Cleveland 72-61 early in the third quarter, Brown decided to give the rookie a shot.

Nickel, who is on a two-way contract, checked in with 8:33 left in the third for his first regular-season NBA game, and New York went on to win 121-102 at Madison Square Garden.

Brown said he was mostly looking for a spark.

"We were down and I just thought we needed a lift from some place. I thought if he came in and hit a three, the bench would get into it and get everybody hyped," Brown told reporters after the game, in a clip shared by SNY.

Nickel gave his coach exactly that when he hit a 24-foot three on a pass from guard Jalen Brunson to cut Cleveland's lead to 74-68, and less than two minutes later he finished a running layup off a feed from center Karl-Anthony Towns to make it 78-75.

He only played in the third quarter and finished with five points on 2-of-3 shooting and four fouls, while New York was plus-8 with him on the floor.

Brown also liked how Nickel kept running into people, since he felt the Knicks needed to turn the game around in the physical part of it.

New York trailed 85-82 going into the fourth quarter and outscored Cleveland 39-17 from there, as guard Jose Alvarado scored 16 of his 21 points in the quarter and made five threes in the game.

Alvarado also gave New York a spark off the bench in Toronto on Nov. 17, so the second unit has had some good nights lately.

I like this call a lot because Brown trusted a player from the very end of his bench at a point when his regulars needed help.

New York was missing forward OG Anunoby, guard Jordan Clarkson and guard Miles McBride, so Brown was already working with a thin group after the injury report came out before the game even started.

Four fouls in about nine minutes is a lot, but Brown sent him in looking for a lift and came away liking how hard he played once he got out there.

I don't see Nickel turning into a regular part of the rotation, though he showed his coach he'll be ready the next time his name gets called.

Beating Cleveland also made up for the 115-107 loss at the Garden on Nov. 11, a night when Brunson took the blame for the loss, and since Wednesday's game was part of NBA Cup group play, it meant a little more.

The win was the sixth straight for New York, which is now 12-6 and third in the East, tied with Boston and 2.5 games behind first-place Philadelphia.

The Knicks are off for Thanksgiving before they host Miami on Friday, and Atlanta visits on Sunday.

Brown is going to have to keep digging into his bench until Anunoby and the other injured players get back, and after what I saw Wednesday, I'd feel fine about him calling Nickel's number again.

#2802. S&P warns Nvidia credit rating could be cut over 'rising' management and governance risk

Nvidia's credit rating could be cut by one notch over the next 90 days if the company continues to have "deficiencies" in its management and governance framework, S&P Global Ratings said in the wake of the Jensen Huang's ouster and reinstatement as Nvidia CEO.

"News regarding the CEO and questions regarding potential export control violations have exposed vulnerabilities in Nvidia's management and governance framework, especially in regard to its board structure, composition, and effectiveness," S&P said in a Wednesday note.

Nvidia currently holds an AA rating, the third-highest level.

S&P has placed the chipmaker on "CreditWatch with negative implications," though any potential downgrade would be limited to one notch, to AA-, unless investigations or disclosures lead the agency to conclude that Nvidia's reputation or future prospects have also been harmed.

A downgrade can pressure bond prices, push yields higher and raise borrowing costs. Nvidia priced $25 billion of investment-grade senior notes in June, with maturities from 2028 through 2056, shortly after S&P upgraded the company from AA- to AA.

Nvidia has about $32.5 billion of bonds in the U.S. high-grade corporate bond index, according to Bloomberg.

S&P's Wednesday note follows Moody's Ratings changing its outlook on Nvidia to stable from positive on Nov. 18, citing governance concerns, and leaving that outlook unchanged after Huang's return on Monday.

In its note, S&P cited the "long tenure" of certain board members as a reason their independence may be "compromised" and said Nvidia's management and governance risk was "rising."

The ratings agency added that it remained unaware of any remedial action taken by the board to address what could be "meaningful" management and governance deficiencies.

Chairman Harvey Jones and early investor Tench Coxe have served on Nvidia's board since 1993, while Brooke Seawell joined in 1997. Mark Stevens served from 1993 to 2006 and rejoined the board in 2008.

S&P also flagged questions around geopolitical risk management and internal controls arising from Nvidia's exposure to China.

"While Nvidia management has delivered significant returns for its stakeholders over decades, weak executive oversight and lack of succession planning have brought questions around the "key man" risk to the forefront," S&P's Andrew Chang and David Tsui said.

Chang and Tsui noted that Huang's resignation and subsequent reinstatement did not resolve questions about the board's oversight of executive conduct.

The agency also intends to meet with Nvidia's board and management to discuss plans to address those deficiencies, particularly around "board independence, effective management oversight and a credible leadership succession plan."

Huang returned less than two weeks after Jones said Nvidia's directors did not intend to reinstate him. The board later voted unanimously to bring Huang back, sending Nvidia shares up as much as 36% during Monday's session.

Nvidia shares are down about 12.5% since Monday's close.

#2801. What channel is Thanksgiving football on? How to stream the Bears vs. Lions, Eagles vs. Cowboys and Chiefs vs. Bills games today

If you buy something through a link in this article, we may earn commission.

Football fans, take note: Three NFL games are happening on Thanksgiving Day — Bears vs. Lions, Eagles vs. Cowboys, and Chiefs vs. Bills. The games will air across CBS, Fox and NBC. Here's what you need to know to watch football this Thanksgiving, plus when and how to watch every other Week 12 matchup.

Time: 1 p.m. ET, 4:30 p.m. ET, 8:20 p.m. ET

Streaming: Paramount+, Peacock, Fox One, Tubi and more

The Bears vs. Lions game kicks off Thursday's NFL action at 1 p.m. ET. The Eagles vs. Cowboys game is at 4:30 p.m. ET, and the Chiefs vs. Bills game is at 8:20 p.m. ET.

The Chicago Bears vs. Detroit Lions game airs on CBS; the Philadelphia Eagles vs. Dallas Cowboys game airs on Fox (and streams free on Tubi); and the Kansas City Chiefs vs. Buffalo Bills game airs on NBC.

Chicago Bears vs. Detroit Lions: 1 p.m. (CBS)

#2800. Wealthy Europeans look beyond stocks amid AI selloff. Here’s where they’re investing

Wealthy Europeans are looking to alternative asset classes to build exposure to artificial intelligence themes amid turbulence in public markets.

Goldman Sachs Alternatives' inaugural survey of high-net-worth and ultra-high-net-worth investors found 47% of European respondents saw alternatives as the best way to gain exposure to AI and future tech growth, compared to just 24% of U.S. investors.

The survey found a significant gap between the U.S. and Europe in terms of investor knowledge of alternatives, which include assets like private equity, property and infrastructure. Goldman said 82% of European respondents are familiar with the alternative investments market — compared to 50% of U.S. investors.

Goldman polled 1,000 investors with at least 1 million in investable assets in their local currency across the U.K., France, Germany, Italy and Switzerland. The survey was carried out between June 29 and Aug. 18 this year.

The report on the findings, published Wednesday, did not specify the proportion of respondents who already have a stake in some kind of alternative investment — but wealthy Europeans appeared particularly bullish on the space.

Private equity made up the largest proportion of current alternatives holdings. Among wealthy Europeans with alternatives exposure, 62% said they invested in private equity. The second most commonly held asset class was private real estate, which was backed by 51% of European alternatives investors. Hedge funds were held by 37% of European alternatives owners, and private infrastructure was held by 30%.

One in four respondents who did not already own an alternative investment said they were likely to invest in the market in the coming 12 months. Among those already in the space, 26% said they were likely to increase their allocations over the next year, while just 5% said they planned to decrease their exposure.

Of the European investors who already owned alternatives, 54% said their assets had performed as expected, while 44% said their assets had exceeded their expectations to the upside. Only 2% said their alternative investments had performed worse than expected.

"Recent reforms gave individual investors unprecedented access to alternative asset classes like private equity, venture capital, and private credit," Kyle Kniffen, global head of alternatives for third-party wealth at Goldman Sachs Asset Management, said in Wednesday's report.

He added that wealthy investors "look ready to jump at the opportunity."

"Our survey shows satisfaction among current alts owners is high and adoption looks set to increase," Kniffen said.

Alternative investments have increasingly become a staple of high-net-worth individuals' investment portfolios.

Over the past year, JPMorgan Asset Management's private wealth alternative assets in the Asia-Pacific region have jumped 40%, while BNY Wealth says alternative investments now account for 60% of single-family office portfolios — recently surpassing traditional assets for the first time.

But investing in alternatives carries risks. Private equity, for example, can lock up capital for long periods, and although the number of exits ticked slightly higher last year, the value of those deals fell.

Concerns about private credit, meanwhile, have dominated headlines, with JPMorgan CEO Jamie Dimon warning on Oct. 14, 2025, that "cockroaches" were likely to be lurking in private markets.

— CNBC's Lee Ying Shan contributed to this article.
