#2799. Raiders See Slight Power Rankings Bump Despite Loss To Broncos

The Las Vegas Raiders 3-0 start feels like forever ago.

Entering Week 12, the Raiders are 4-7, and the only win in that span came against the New York Jets.

Still, Klint Kubiak's team has played competitive football time and time again, and Las Vegas has more wins before Thanksgiving than the team had in all of 2025 with Pete Carroll as the head coach.

Las Vegas is coming off a 38-24 loss to the Denver Broncos on the road, and it was a one-score game late in the fourth before Fernando Mendoza threw a pick-six to cornerback Ja'Quan McMillian.

The Raiders are an exciting bunch at times, and Mendoza's first NFL touchdown pass came via a long catch-and-run by running back Ashton Jeanty.

Going into Week 12, NFL.com's Nick Shook had updated power rankings, and the Raiders actually moved up one spot, going to No. 20, despite the loss to the Broncos.

A big reason why the Raiders didn't stay at No. 21 is because the Pittsburgh Steelers and Aaron Rodgers suffered an embarrassing loss to the Philadelphia Eagles in Week 11, and that isn't pretty.

Week 11 was a bit of a promising showing for the Raiders, even in the loss. They were right there until late in the fourth quarter, and wide receiver Jack Bech showed that he could be a reliable target for Mendoza, and tight end Brock Bowers was out once again.

For Fernando Mendoza, the No. 1 pick and reigning Heisman Trophy winner has been thrown right into the fire.

His first-ever regular season start came against the Seattle Seahawks, the reigning Super Bowl champions.

Then, Mendoza played the Broncos, last year's AFC No. 1 seed, in a wild atmosphere in a loud Mile High.

The Raiders face the Cleveland Browns in Week 12 before a much-needed bye, and then they face the Los Angeles Chargers, so that might be an easy win for the Raiders.

Las Vegas then finishes against the Broncos, Tennessee Titans, Arizona Cardinals and the Chiefs, so there is a scenario where this team finishes with a 7-10 record.

If that is the case, it would be a successful first season for Kubiak, especially considering that veteran Kirk Cousins was traded to the Minnesota Vikings at the deadline.

The Kubiak hire continues to look like a home run, and there is a lot to look forward to with this team, which is not something anybody has said in previous years.

#2798. ‘Ready for any storm’: UAE minister optimistic on economy as IMF lifts forecast

The UAE is "very optimistic" about achieving 7%-8% growth next year, the country's economy and tourism minister Abdulla bin Touq Al Marri told CNBC after the latest data from the International Monetary Fund showed the country would avoid a recession in 2026.

The IMF last week revised the UAE's real GDP forecast to 0.2% growth in 2026 from a 3.9% contraction predicted a month ago. "The UAE is coming back, and that adjustment within one month shows that there is a huge potential aspect when it comes towards the end of the year," the minister told CNBC's Dan Murphy.

He said the tourism industry was already picking up with occupancy rates returning to "almost near normal" and international carriers including British Airways and Air France rerouting flights to the UAE. The minister expects economic growth to be higher than 0.2% this year.

The Iran war has disrupted economic activity in the UAE, with missile and drone attacks from Tehran and maritime disruptions weighing on energy trade, tourism, aviation and private-sector confidence.

Earlier this month, U.S. President Donald Trump said that renewed attacks on Iran were "highly unlikely" if negotiations produce a "very strong agreement." However, on Monday, Trump threatened that Tehran could face fresh U.S. strikes next year if the two countries failed to strike a deal, as per Fox News.

"We can we cannot promise the world for any storms that will land on our shore, but we can always promise that the UAE's economy is ready for any storm," Al Marri said.

He added that next year the UAE's economy will have a "different" model than the one before the conflict and investments will be focused on building factories and "things that we really need in times of crisis."

The World Bank in October said it expects the UAE's economy to recover next year, driven by rising oil production and exports and a rebound in tourism, trade, transportation, financial services, and construction.

It warned that the improvement in economic activity in the UAE largely depends on the duration and intensity of the Iran war, with a faster de-escalation likely to accelerate the recovery in services exports and private-sector activity.

#2797. Packers Winners and Losers: Week 12 vs. the Rams

On Thanksgiving Eve, the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams faced off in a game which had heavy impact on the playoff chances for both teams. RG Kevin Zeitler made his first start for the Packers. EDGE Micah Parsons made his long awaited return after sustaining a torn ACL during the 2025 season. It was also another chapter in the Matt LaFleur vs. Sean McVay saga, and, for the first time since the two both became NFL head coaches, McVay and the Rams came out victorious, 26-13. Let's take a look at what worked, and what didn't.

It feels so good typing these words right now. After wondering what the heck was taking so long with the return of the Packers star edge rusher after a December 2025 ACL tear, it was revealed that the damage was much more severe than many of us realized. Last night, finally healthy, Parsons made his first start of the 2026 season, playing about 37% of total defensive snaps. And he looked…great!

Parsons nearly got to QB Matthew Stafford a few times, and then just before halftime, we all got to see the sack we'd been waiting 346 days for. With how things have been called all year for the Packers, I was certain this would be flagged for roughing the passer. Parsons finished the night with 1 sack and 2 QB hits, and was credited with 4 pressures on 26 snaps. Good stuff, and it'll only get better.

WR Christian Watson was the only reliable pass catcher for the Packers last night, and he was knocked out of the game in the first half after taking a nasty blow to the helmet, which saw Rams S Kam Kinchens ejected. Prior to the hit, Watson was the only one able to hold onto the ball or…do anything right, really. The routes were nice. The hands were strong. He finished the night catching all 4 targets for 65 yards and 1 TD. His concussion status will be one to monitor as the Packers head into their next game next Sunday.

The Packers have had a problem with TE Tucker Kraft all season, and now, it's got me begging them to stop giving him the ball. Kraft has had some pretty egregious drops this year, has played like he's scared of getting hit, and has just been generally BAD. Last night, he logged his second fumble of the year, and the Rams were able to turn it into 3 more points. Kraft finished the night catching 3 of 5 targets for 24 yards. They don't really have any other options at TE, so I guess we just hope for the best? And cry a little?

Last night was the first time I've seen QB Jordan Love visibly angry, and it came on the heels of an interception that seemed to have been caused by WR Matthew Golden running the wrong route. Golden has been great this year. He even showed flashes of that last night with his big 39 yard gain, but this error killed any sort of momentum the Packers were developing. Golden finished the night with 1 catch on 5 targets for 39 yards.

It may not be a good idea to stop running and then just watch as the defender intercepts your quarterback.

We can blame the quarterback (not really), we can blame the offensive line, we can blame the receivers, we can blame the run defense, we can blame the head coach! At the end of the day, this goes back to GM Brian Gutekunst, who put the team together. Now—there are things he can't control, like the drops, and the injury bug flying through the locker room. But the poor depth at offensive line has been the catalyst for almost all of the bad things to happen to the Packers offense this season.

Jordan Love has been running for his life. He's being hit while throwing. The RB room doesn't stand a chance. The receivers have had almost no time to get open. An even mediocre offensive line fixes a lot of this. By failing to put together an o-line group that can sustain any sort of run game, any sort of passing game other than "sling it and hope for the best", Brian Gutekunst has bungled the 2026 roster.

The 6-5 Packers make their next appearance next Sunday, December 6, against the 3-7 New Orleans Saints. The game is at 1 pm ET/12 pm CT, and yes, that's a threat.

#2795. After losing his leg in a Hamas ambush in Gaza, IDF veteran sets sights on 2028 Paralympics

Born in Ukraine, Kopylov immigrated to Israel from Russia with his family in 2019. He enlisted in the Kfir Brigade's 90th Battalion in 2022, motivated by a desire "to give back" to Israel.

Two and a half years after losing his right leg while fighting in the Gaza Strip, wounded IDF veteran Daniil Kopylov has competed in a world para-cycling championship and is now aiming to qualify for the 2028 Paralympic Games in Los Angeles.

Speaking to The Jerusalem Post as Israel marks National Appreciation Week for wounded IDF soldiers and victims of terrorism, Kopylov, a former Kfir Brigade combat soldier, discussed his decision to enlist, the incident that killed three of his friends, and the physical and psychological challenges of rebuilding his life.

"Sometimes we don't appreciate these things," Kopylov said, recalling that he was initially disappointed not to have won a medal at the world championship. "Then I thought, two and a half years ago, I was in a hospital bed, and I couldn't move. I couldn't eat on my own. I couldn't go to the bathroom on my own. And today, I participated in a world championship in para-cycling, and it's amazing."

Born in Ukraine, Kopylov immigrated to Israel from Russia with his family in 2019. He enlisted in the Kfir Brigade's 90th Battalion in 2022, motivated by a desire "to give back" to the country that had welcomed him.

"Everyone wanted to help me. Everyone wanted me to succeed. It wasn't like in Ukraine and Russia, where everyone was for themselves," he said, reflecting on his time at a youth village following his immigration to Israel. "I just wanted to give back to the community and to serve in the army."

Kopylov was stationed along Israel's border with Lebanon at the outbreak of the Israel-Hamas War in 2023. After Hezbollah began firing on northern Israel the following day in solidarity with Hamas, his unit was tasked, he said, with helping prevent an October 7-style invasion in the North.

"They [Hezbollah] also wanted to invade Israel in the North," he said. "So my unit was responsible for making ambushes and not letting them invade the North the same way Hamas invaded the South."

Later, while he was attending a squad commanders' course, his unit was deployed to Gaza to relieve another unit.

"Our mission was to rescue, to search for hostages, and to clear the buildings of terrorists," he said.

On March 1, 2024, during the two-week mission, Kopylov's unit was caught in an explosion in Khan Yunis.

"The whole building just exploded and collapsed on us," he recalled.

The explosion killed three of his fellow soldiers: Sgt. Dolev Malka, Sgt. Afik Tery, and Sgt. Inon Itzhak.

The blast nearly severed Kopylov's right leg below the knee, yet he remained conscious and managed to apply a tourniquet to stop the bleeding before being evacuated.

"I was conscious the whole time, and I didn't feel the pain because of the adrenaline," he recalled.

Kopylov spent three days unconscious in a hospital in southern Israel and underwent multiple operations. Part of his intestines was removed because of shrapnel injuries, and his right leg was subsequently amputated above the knee due to an infection. He spent another five months in a rehabilitation hospital.

Initially, Kopylov said, he was focused on recovering and regaining his independence as quickly as possible. About four months later, however, the full significance of his injury became apparent.

"After I got my prosthetic, I think it was the first time that I realized that I will never be able to come back to normal life, and that the prosthetic is not comfortable, and that it's a long process to get used to it," he said. "It will never replace my real leg, and for the rest of my life, it will be difficult to do things."

The hardest part of his rehabilitation, however, came after he left the hospital, Kopylov said.

While hospitalized, he had been surrounded by people offering him support, including hospital staff and representatives of the IDF and Defense Ministry.

Upon leaving, however, he had to navigate the bureaucracy of the Defense Ministry and contend with the physical and psychological consequences of his injuries alongside the ordinary challenges of adult life, including employment, studies, and relationships.

"Sometimes you're in an apartment, and you're alone ... and there's no one to talk to, no one to help you," he said.

It was as part of his rehabilitation that Kopylov took up cycling. While he initially viewed it as a hobby and a means of coping with the psychological consequences of his injury, it eventually became a source of purpose, discipline, and structure in his life.

He now cycles six times a week, trains in the gym twice a week, and competes internationally.

Kopylov said he thinks about his three friends who were killed in Gaza almost every day.

On Israel's most recent Remembrance Day, he visited the family of one of the fallen soldiers. He also shares their stories during lectures he gives in Israel and abroad, including in the United States and the United Kingdom.

"I think that I'm able to do all of this stuff because of them, because of their sacrifice, and because they're not with me today," he said.

Beyond his ambition to qualify for the 2028 Paralympics, Kopylov hopes to start a family and is considering pursuing a career in physical therapy.

Asked what he wished Israeli society better understood about the lives of wounded soldiers, Kopylov called for greater consideration of the everyday difficulties they face.

"I don't want people to feel pity for me," he said, adding that he wanted people to be more attentive to those living with injuries and to offer help without making them feel sorry for themselves.

Kopylov also noted that people sometimes change the way they treat him upon discovering that he lost his leg during the war.

"I want people to treat each other with respect before they see that [they are speaking to] an injured soldier," he said.

Asked whether, knowing the personal cost of his military service, he would still choose to make aliyah and enlist as a combat soldier, Kopylov acknowledged that he sometimes imagines what his life would have been like had he not been wounded.

"I wouldn't need to suffer. I wouldn't need to deal with the prosthetic," he said. "But on the other hand, I think, if not me, if not my friends who fell, who would defend, not just the country, the Israeli citizens, but who would defend my close family? Who would defend my siblings and my friends?"

"If not me, who would do that?" he asked.

Ultimately, he said, he believes he would make the same decision.

#2794. Dillon Brooks gets ejected with 23 seconds left vs. Rockets after getting into it with Kevin Durant

Phoenix Suns guard Dillon Brooks and Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant exchanged words at the end of Houston's 116-104 win on Wednesday night.

Durant was chippy all night, making sure to deliver extra trash talk against his former team.

With less than 30 seconds left in the game, the Rockets led by 10. In a wild moment, Durant and Brooks weren't really playing; they were just talking to each other near halfcourt while the action continued.

Houston's Fred VanVleet hit a jumper with 23.8 seconds left to make it a 114-102 game in the Rockets' favor. Brooks and Durant were chirping at each other and got in each other's faces.

The referees assessed each player a technical foul. Because Brooks had received a technical foul earlier in the quarter with 4:32 left, he was ejected. VanVleet drained a three and motioned to Brooks; they chirped and were both assessed technicals.

#2793. Packers By The Numbers: Stats from the Week 12 loss to the Rams

As we do every week in this space, we're looking at some stats from the Green Bay Packers' most recent game, courtesy of SumerSports. Today we're looking at the 26-13 loss to the Los Angeles Rams in Week 12.

I don't have metrics on the broadcast, but I can tell you with some amount of certainty that there was less dead air in this game than any other game this season. Luke Kuechly brought some really nice insight into some of the plays, but my goodness, fellas, you're allowed to take a breath.

And stop trying to make "Thanksgiving Eve" happen. It's not going to happen.

After leaning into a heavy personnel grouping last week, this week saw the Packers go back to primarily playing out of 11 personnel (1 RB, 1 TE, 3 WR).

And, lest you think the numbers are skewed due to a small number of plays in the second half (and needing to catch up when they did have the ball), those full game numbers are roughly in line with the first half approach.

They were primarily a shotgun-based passing attack offense in this game. For the game, they were in shotgun on 84.1% of snaps and had a pass rate of 68.2%. Again, those numbers basically align with the 1st half. They were 78.6% shotgun and 64.3% pass in the 1st half.

As we've seen them do all year, they switched up their run game approach in this game. After going with a power-heavy approach against the Vikings, they were back to a zone-based attack this week.

Inside Zone didn't work overly well for them, but they did well with Outside Zone and Power. I'm curious to dig a little deeper into that when the film hits. Was that a matter of a smaller sample size, or was that something they could have leaned a bit heavier into if they wanted to?

Jordan Love had a pretty rough day in the stat sheet. He went 17/29 for 198 yards, 1 TD and 2 INTs for an EPA per Play of -0.31. That would go down as his worst game of the year by EPA per Play (behind the -0.22 he posted against the Vikings last week). As I mentioned at the top, I thought Kuechly did a nice job breaking down what Love was expecting on that 1st INT, and the second INT certainly seemed to be a case where Bo Melton cut off his route. So there are certainly things to consider (and things we'll certainly be breaking down this week), but, right now, the numbers are the numbers and the numbers are bad.

Love did seem to have a little more time to throw in this game, but, as with every game this season, time is relative. The average time to pressure against Love was 2.3 seconds, 0.26 seconds below the NFL average. His average time to throw of 2.62 seconds seems downright functional, and would make this his 5th longest time to throw in a game this year. It's still 0.08 seconds below the NFL average. So protection was better, but still below average. Still, it felt functional in a way it certainly did not against the Vikings.

Love came into this game shredding Man coverage, and the Rams came into the game playing one of the highest rates of man coverage in the league. Love continued playing well against man, but the zone stuff is ugly.

We don't know how long Christian Watson will be out, but it sure would be nice if Tucker Kraft could find his game a little bit. He clearly is not all the way back from his injury (whether that's physical or mental, I don't really know), but the offense won't really have a chance if he can't find a way to turn it on.

Micah Parsons played limited snaps in his return to the field, but he was impactful while he was there, notching a 21.4% pressure rate and an average time to pressure of 2.47 seconds.

Parsons posted a pressure rate of 20.2% and an average time to pressure of 2.76 seconds last season, so he looked like Micah Parsons while he was on the field. That was certainly encouraging.

With Parsons back, I was certainly hoping for more from Lukas Van Ness, but we didn't really see that. He had a pressure rate of 6.7% and an average time to pressure of 2.6 seconds. With as run-heavy as the Rams were (66.2% run rate for the game, 81.4% in the 2nd half) it's hard to glean too much in terms of how the pass rush will look with Parsons back. Something to keep an eye on going forward.

Chris McClellan turned in a nice game with 4 assisted tackles, 4 solo tackles and 1 tackle for loss. I'm excited to take a look at him when the film comes out.

#2792. Prediction markets outlook for stocks in 2026 dims amid AI sell-off

As stocks continue their tech-driven sell-off, prediction markets traders are seeing a bleaker outlook for the remainder of the year.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

Commanders GM pumps the brakes on Brandon Aiyuk hype appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

#2790. Game Recap: Suns run out of fuel in 116-104 loss to Rockets

The Phoenix Suns fought valiantly, but couldn't finish the road trip undefeated as the Houston Rockets held off a late push by the Suns and earned a 116-104 victory. Phoenix clawed within two at 99-97, but the Rockets finished on a 17-7 run.

Alperen Sengun finished the game with 23 points, 11 rebounds and 11 assists, recording his third triple-double of the season. Seven of those rebounds were on the offensive glass. Steven Adams also snatched six offensive boards, and the Rockets won the overall rebounding battle 51-38.

Oso Ighodaro had 13 points on 6-for-8 shooting, but managed just two rebounds in 20 minutes before fouling out. Khaman Maluach added 12 points of his own and made several outstanding plays, but is still a little too raw to compete with the burly Adams and All-Star Sengun.

The Suns were an abysmal 5-for-32 from three-point range in the game. Jordan Goodwin was 2-for-4, which made the rest of the team 3-for-28. That looks like a typo. Posting a -13 on the boards and shooting 15.6% from the three-point range makes it hard to win a basketball game. A bad shooting night is one thing, but the rebounding situation is starting to look like a fatal flaw for this squad.

Miles Bridges struggled through his worst offensive game of the season, recording just two points while shooting 0-6 from three-point range and 1-8 overall. Devin Booker had a game-high 25 points and Jalen Green chipped in 16, but they were 6-18 and 8-21 from the field, respectively. Booker did knock down 11-of-12 free throws to continue his impressive performance from the line this season.

The Suns' bench group played well overall. In addition to Maluach's performance, Goodwin scored 11 points and grabbed five rebounds, but played just 17 minutes. Kevin Durant recorded a double-double with 20 points and 10 rebounds, while Amen Thompson recorded his own with 18 and 10. That is two more than the Suns had.

The Rockets' starters were just too much for Phoenix to contend with tonight. Plain and simple.

The Suns' aspirations of advancing to the next round in the NBA Cup have been quashed with this loss.

Phoenix will try to get back in the win column when they host the Dallas Mavericks and Cooper Flagg at 7:00pm Friday.

The Rockets raced out to an 11-4 lead with Kevin Durant getting five quick points. The Suns recovered to tie it up at 11, and for a long stretch in the quarter it looked like we might be watching a defense-optional game.

KD kept pouring it in and already had 11 points when the Rockets went up 22-18. At that time, the Rockets held a 10-2 free-throw disparity over the Suns.

The Suns were trying to play physical, but the referees didn't let them impose their will, opting to let Durant and Adams flop to get calls. Maluach ended up with a fairly weak flagrant one as he somehow managed to plant Adams face first on the ground. Or maybe Adams just laid down that way on purpose.

Jordan Goodwin entered and hit a quick three-pointer to pull the Suns within one at 23-22. Goodwin is shooting a career-high 38.6% this season from three-point range.

Goodwin hit his second three-pointer to give the Suns a 32-27 lead courtesy of a 10-2 run. The first quarter ended 33-29 in the Suns' favor. Booker finished the quarter with 13 points and survived a massive block by Amen Thompson.

Goodwin was fouled on a three-point attempt after making his first two. He sank all three free throws to give the Suns their biggest lead of the game at 38-31. The Oso/Kennard/Gillespie/Brooks/Goodwin lineup meshed well together, but the Rockets started punishing them inside.

Steven Adams grabbed four quick offensive rebounds. There just isn't a player on the Suns with the size and strength of Adams.

He helped the Rockets go on a 13-7 run to cut the Suns' lead to 45-44. Maluach was still making his presence felt. A few plays after a block on Sengun, he nailed a jumper as the shot clock expired. In between that, he mishandled the ball on offense for a turnover. Such are the highs and lows with the 20-year-old.

Sengun was still winning the matchup overall. After another offensive rebound, Sengun hit a three to cap an 11-2 run that put Houston up 57-51. That turned into a 20-7 run by the time the quarter was over, and Houston led 66-56 at the half. The Suns had squandered their early lead and would trail the remainder of the game.

Houston led the battle of the boards 27-15, including 12-4 on the offensive end. The Rockets shot 26 free throws, but made just 14, while the Suns were 11-14 from the charity stripe.

#2788. ‘He’s Such a Troll,’ ‘Absolutely Amazing’ — NFL World Reacts to Lions WR Amon-Ra St. Brown Using Bears HC Ben Johnson’s Celebration

Ben Johnson's Stanky Legg has a new performer, and he plays for the other side. Detroit Lions wide receiver Amon-Ra St. Brown broke out the Chicago Bears head coach's signature dance after a catch in the opening minutes of Thursday's Thanksgiving meeting at Ford Field.

Johnson does the move to celebrate Bears wins, usually with his players gathered around him in the locker room. Seeing a Lion borrow it drew an amused response right away, with former NFL cornerback Jason McCourty among those enjoying it.

St. Brown also has history with the coach whose celebration he borrowed. Johnson was Detroit's offensive coordinator before he left for Chicago after the 2024 season, and both teams entered Thursday at 8-2.

The celebration began on impulse. Johnson first did it after the Bears' 27-7 Monday night win over the Philadelphia Eagles in Week 3, and he described the moment earlier this season: "Those guys were waiting for me like that, and so I didn't know what to do. That was a panic move is what it was."

The move caught on with the GS Boyz' 2008 song of the same name, and Johnson's version centers on a wobbling left knee set in motion by his right arm. After beating the New England Patriots on October 22, several players copied their coach, Caleb Williams among them, and Johnson worked in a stutter-step after a win at Seattle.

PICK THE PLAYOFF FIELD: PFN's Free NFL Playoff Predictor

McCourty kept his verdict on St. Brown's version simple: "Amon-Ra with the stanky leg celebration is hilarious!"

The dance has also left the locker room. T-shirts and stickers followed, and cornerback Kyler Gordon slapped one of those stickers on Johnson's chest after the win in Seattle. The Bears also pair the dance video with their "Good, Better, Best" clips after wins.

Even a reporter on the Bears beat enjoyed the Detroit edition. Patrick Finley, who covers the team for the Chicago Sun-Times, put it plainly: "Amon-Ra St. Brown doing the Stanky Legg after a one-handed catch is objectively fun."

BE THE GM OF YOUR FAVORITE TEAM: PFN's Free NFL Mock Draft Simulator

Fantasy analyst Adam Koffler went bigger still: "Amon-Ra doing the Ben Johnson after every catch is absolutely amazing."

Other voices saw trolling between NFC North rivals. Zirksee read the moment that way: "Amon-Ra hitting the Ben Johnson celly after the first down catch, these teams are such trolls."

Podcast co-host Lexi landed on the same word for St. Brown: "I thought that's what I saw … he's such a troll."

Barstool Sports podcaster Dan Katz needed only a few words: "The Amon-Ra stanky leg. Mean."

NFL and fantasy football creator Bryce DeGroat fixed on the timing: "Amon-Ra hitting the Bears stanky leg celebration 1 minute into the game is WILD."

The ribbing between St. Brown and Johnson goes back to the coach's exit from Detroit. St. Brown's warning came as Johnson took the Chicago job: "Two times a year, Ben, we're going to [expletive] you up." Johnson, as St. Brown tells it, answered in kind: "I'm going to [expletive] you up."

The Lions swept both meetings last season, Johnson's first in Chicago. Thursday's game is the first of two this season, and the teams meet again January 3 at Soldier Field.

SET YOUR LINEUPS: PFN's Free Fantasy Start/Sit Optimizer

PFN's NFL WR Impact Rankings have St. Brown graded at 88.7 this season, entering Thursday's game, ranking fifth among 81 eligible wide receivers. On Thursday, his three catches produced 69 of the Lions' 94 passing yards as Detroit led 3-0 with 13:41 left in the second quarter.

#2787. U.S. tariff-hike threats pile pressure on partners to sign trade deals

The Trump administration has unveiled a fresh tariff salvo on more than a dozen trading partners, with China, Vietnam, and India facing some of the steepest potential rates and mounting pressure to conclude trade deals with Washington.

The proposed tariffs, framed as curbing excess industrial capacity, start at 15% for the European Union, South Korea, Switzerland and Taiwan and rise to as much as 35% for Vietnam.

The U.S. Trade Representative's Office said it intends to cap the new duties at lower levels for countries that have completed trade deals or are still negotiating with Washington.

The overall impact on the region is likely to be limited, said Mark Williams, chief Asia economist at Capital Economics. The proposal "puts most of the targeted countries back where they were earlier in the year," he said, after the Supreme Court struck down Trump's emergency tariffs in February.

Still, the proposal could give some countries an incentive to speed up trade talks in order to lock in lower tariff rates, analysts said.

On Wednesday, U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer said that the new policy likely will not take effect for months, with a senior U.S. official saying the duties could take effect as early as January.

China faces a capped rate of 20%, on top of an existing 25% tariff imposed during President Donald Trump's first term. The rate could rise to 40% if the trade truce reached in South Korea last October is not extended beyond January, the USTR said.

Beijing has rejected Washington's claim of excess capacity while vowing to take measures necessary to protect its rights.

On Thursday, Chinese Commerce Ministry spokesperson He Yadong called on Washington to "rectify its wrongdoing and return to the right track of resolving issues through dialogue."

He was responding to Washington's plan to raise tariffs following its Section 301 investigations, while the ministry also urged the U.S. to implement $30 billion of reciprocal tariff cuts agreed by the two sides in late September.

The renewed tensions come despite several conciliatory meetings between Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping this year, as well as what Williams described as Trump's "apparent lack of concern" about allegations that Chinese technology firms have circumvented U.S. chip export controls.

The two leaders are expected to meet for a fourth time this year at the Group of 20 leaders summit in Miami in December.

"While not happy, Beijing seems prepared to live with the new rates provided that they don't exceed 20%," said Wendy Cutler, senior vice president at the Asia Society Policy Institute.

By retaining the option to raise levies if bilateral talks deteriorate, Washington is using the tariffs as leverage to "secure expected shipments of critical minerals and rare earths, and further purchases of U.S. agricultural goods," Cutler said.

Washington has warned that tariffs on Chinese goods could double to 40% if the USTR finds that Beijing is not engaging. "If the trade war did start up again, today's measures provide the U.S. with some ammunition," Williams said.

Even the sweeping actions against the other 13 trading partners appear aimed squarely at China, said Williams, who tallied that the word "China" appears 253 times in the USTR's report on Vietnam, and 315 times in the document on the European Union.

"Foremost among the 'unreasonable acts, policies, and practices' that these countries are criticised for is being slow to restrict imports and FDI from China," Williams said.

While the proposed tariffs generally lift average rates only modestly, India and Vietnam stand out because of the wider gaps between the rates they would face with and without a deal, analysts said.

India and Vietnam face proposed tariffs of 25% and 35%, respectively, but those rates could fall sharply to 17% and 20% if ongoing talks with the U.S. produce reciprocal trade agreements.

The two countries have "a strong incentive" to conclude deals with Washington to secure the lower rates, Cutler said.

Vietnam's trade ministry said Thursday that it was working with the U.S. to complete a trade deal as soon as possible, according to Reuters. India could also gain an edge over some Asian rivals through comparatively lower levies if New Delhi concludes its long-pending trade deal with Washington.

Singapore faces a similar challenge. The proposal sets a maximum potential rate of 20% on its exports, net of existing Section 301 tariffs, which could fall to 10% if it reaches an agreement on reciprocal trade with the U.S. or concludes other negotiations addressing the USTR's findings, according to Singapore's Ministry of Energy, Trade and Industry.

The ministry said it disagreed with the findings and was "very disappointed" with the proposed action, arguing that it failed to account for the structure of Singapore's economy.

Speaking to reporters, Deputy Prime Minister Gan Kim Yong said that about 33%, or 9.4 billion Singapore dollars ($7.2 billion) of direct exports will be affected, and the effective tariff rate on affected Singapore exports will rise to 10.3% from the current 7.8%, should Washington impose the highest levy of 20%. "I'm also very surprised that this has happened because this is not something that you would expect from an important major trading partner," Gan added, saying "We have been working together for many years, and we have an FTA with the U.S.," Gan said, referring to the U.S.-Singapore Free Trade Agreement that was in force since 2004.

Under the FTA, Singapore imposes zero tariffs on U.S. goods, while the U.S. has run a trade surplus with the city-state for more than 20 years, the ministry said. That surplus grew to $33 billion in 2025 from $27 billion in 2024.

Singapore said it would submit further comments during the public consultation period and urge the USTR to reconsider.

Exemptions for semiconductors and other electronics should cushion the impact on South Korea and Taiwan, whose economies are heavily concentrated in those sectors, analysts said.

The USTR singled out South Korea for its concentration in memory-chip production and what it described as outsized government support. Yet it excluded the country's chips from the proposed excess-capacity tariffs.

Taiwan, which faces an excess-capacity tariff cap of 15%, also benefits from exemptions for other electronics, including mobile phones and networking equipment. Its effective tariff rate would rise to about 9% from 6%, Williams estimated.

Switzerland was hit with a shock 39% tariff rate back in 2025, targeting significant exports, from chemicals and pharmaceuticals to gold, watches and chocolates. In November 2025, it negotiated a deal that slashed U.S. duties to 15% in return for a commitment to invest billions and increase manufacturing Stateside.

In March 2026, the U.S. launched further Section 301 investigations into Switzerland over "unfair or discriminatory trade practices," landing on a recommendation to impose an additional 15% tariff.

Switzerland's Economy Ministry told CNBC that the country rejected the allegations raised during the investigations and said it had already presented its arguments during bilateral consultations with the U.S. Treasury.

The ministry added that it assumed the latest recommendation was consistent with the deal struck in November 2025 and said it was in close contact with U.S. authorities as it reviews next steps.

Officials for both Switzerland and the European Union said during trade discussions last year that while they had a trade surplus with Washington in goods, the relationship was balanced when accounting for both trade and services, where the U.S. dominates in areas such as technology and finance.

Brussels said it was assessing the U.S. report but rejected its finding that Europe was a source of industrial overcapacity.

"To the contrary, Europe is among those most exposed to overcapacity," European Commission Spokesperson Olof Gill said in a statement.

"The EU and the U.S. share a real concern about global overcapacity, much of it state-driven and concentrated in third countries. We regret the lack of joint action," Gill said.

The EU expects any measure taken from the investigation to respect the existing joint agreement of a 15% all-inclusive ceiling on EU goods, Gill added.

EU trade ministers are due to speak with U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer on Monday.

Citi analysts noted they expected a hit on European retailers if the measures stay as announced, with the likes of Adidas, H&M, Puma and Primark potentially seeing downgrades on full-year 2027 earnings.

Inga Fechner, senior economist for global trade at ING, said in a Wednesday note that the U.S. "tariff wall keeps changing, but it isn't coming down," adding that "U.S. protectionism is becoming increasingly difficult to unwind."

While the newly Democrat-controlled Congress could try to restrict presidential tariff authority, remove individual duties or apply greater scrutiny via hearings and investigations, actually reversing the measures will be difficult while Trump retains veto power, she said.

"We don't think the blue wave will mark a return to free trade. The Biden years already showed that tariffs in strategically important sectors enjoy support across political camps."

Protectionist moves become more targeted, she added, adding to a complex system of country- and sector-specific duties based on different legal authorities.

— CNBC's Evelyn Cheng contributed to this report.

The Spurs hadn't lost a road game yet this season, but that came to an end tonight as Damian Lillard with 29 points and Deni Avidja with 33 rallied Rip City to a 5 point win in a game that was a back and forth contest all night. Former Spur Sidy Cissoko had 16 points and made some key plays down the stretch as Victor Wembanyama's 44 points weren't enough to get the Silver and Black to a win. The Spurs shot poorly from three, but the bigger problem was lack of attention to detail, as some critical mistakes and missed free throws down the stretch were the deciding factor, as the Blazers thrilled the home crowd and pulled out a big NBA Cup win. The Spurs will have to win their game on Friday to have a shot at advancing the the knockout rounds in the Cup tournament. After another empty trip by the Silver and Black, the Blazers had the ball and called timeout. The Spurs cut the lead to one score with 17 seconds left, but the Blazers were done giving gifts to the Spurs, and they closed it out with free throws, winning 116-111.

#2785. Former teammate calls Aaron Rodgers' Mike McCarthy takedown 'despicable'

Aaron Rodgers' scorched earth approach with Mike McCarthy was like the drop of a bomb. The fallout from its explosion has carried further than just the current Pittsburgh Steelers roster.

Former Green Bay Packers players from the Rodgers and McCarthy era – 2006 to 2018 – have started to weigh in on how the veteran quarterback handled his split from the Steelers. Greg Jennings, the wide receiver who spent seven years in Green Bay, and guard T.J. Lang, an eight-year Packer, both expressed their surprise at Rodgers' approach.

Neither player confirmed the quarterback's stories about McCarthy, and both had their criticisms for his explosive appearance on ESPN's "Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday, Nov. 25.

AARON RODGERS: Veteran QB goes scorched earth on Mike McCarthy after Steelers benching

Rodgers' scathing comments about McCarthy came hours after the coach benched the quarterback in favor of Mason Rudolph late Tuesday, Nov. 24. In the aftermath of the "Pat McAfee Show" appearance, Pittsburgh waived the veteran quarterback ahead of Week 12.

Here's what Jennings and Lang said about Rodgers' comments:

The former Packers wide receiver said he could not corroborate any of Rodgers' stories about McCarthy during the time they shared in Green Bay. He also noted his surprise at the lack of questions for A.J. Hawk, a former linebacker for the Packers who spent the 2006 through 2014 seasons in Green Bay and a mainstay on the "Pat McAfee Show."

"A large part of me just wanted someone to ask A.J., who was sitting right there, 'A.J., do you recall any of this stuff?' Please, someone, ask A.J., 'Can you confirm, or can you second any of this?'" Jennings said on FS1.

The former receiver said he also reached out to some of his other previous teammates in Green Bay, who also said they could not confirm any of Rodgers' stories about McCarthy.

Lang, who spent the final two years of his career with the Lions, made an appearance on Detroit sports radio station 97.1 The Ticket on Wednesday, Nov. 25. He said Rodgers' decision to air out all of his grievances publicly was the exact kind of behavior the quarterback used to call out.

"I think it's despicable," Lang said. "I think that Aaron Rodgers just became everything that he has b----ed about and moaned about over the last 10 years with former coaches, former teammates. Airing grievances out, giving opinions out in the media.

"He has blacklisted I don't know how many former teammates from just simple comments telling the truth because he was sick of it."

The two-time Pro Bowler also cast doubt on some of Rodgers' comments, given that the Steelers chose to bench, then release the quarterback. Lang specifically called Rodgers out for saying he and Steelers general manager Omar Khan shared laughs about McCarthy's miscues as the head coach behind his back.

"Then why would Omar Khan side with Mike McCarthy? Why would Omar Khan allow Mike McCarthy to bench Aaron Rodgers and basically side with Mike McCarthy?" Lang said.

#2784. Norse Atlantic suspends all flights, warns it may file for bankruptcy after share price collapse

Norse Atlantic suspended all of its flights on Thursday and warned it may have to file for bankruptcy after its stock collapsed on Wednesday following its warning about its "critical" financial situation.

Oslo-listed shares of Norse closed 85% lower on Wednesday.

"The board of directors continues to consider options to secure short-term financing," Norse said in a Thursday statement. "If a short-term financing solution is not secured urgently, it is the board's intention to file for bankruptcy."

The airline added that "there can be no assurance that a viable solution will be found."

Checks by CNBC showed the airline was still selling tickets for flights on Friday and beyond as of 9 a.m. Oslo time on Thursday.

Warning signs first popped up when PK786 from London to Islamabad and PK710 from Manchester to Lahore were cancelled because of "an unexpected operational suspension," the Pakistani flag carrier said on X.

The airline had been set to operate two Pakistan International Airlines flights Wednesday under a wet-lease agreement, in which one airline provides the aircraft and crew to another. The agreement had only been signed in early October.

PIA said it would operate the routes using its own Boeing 777 aircraft from the next day.

Norse's own flight Z0 783 from London Gatwick to Orlando was also shown as canceled Wednesday on live air traffic website Flightradar24. London Gatwick's website listed the flight's status for Thursday as "enquire airline."

In a bid to stave off financial troubles, Norse cut 75 jobs in May as part of an effort to reduce costs by $50 million, after Eivind Roald replaced founder Bjorn Tore Larsen as CEO in November last year.

The Norwegian carrier said Wednesday that its situation depended on securing financing for short-term liquidity needs and that it was in discussions about a potential strategic takeover.

"Norse is also engaged in discussions with selected parties on short term financing, however, there can be no assurances that such discussions will result in a viable solution," the airline said.

The Norwegian carrier has come under strain due to soaring jet fuel costs since the onset of the Iran war.

India's largest airline, low-cost carrier IndiGo, entered an agreement with Norse Atlantic in early 2025 to lease six 787-9 aircraft as it launched flights to Europe and Britain ahead of the arrival of its own widebody planes.

The agreement concluded at the end of October this year, with IndiGo citing airspace constraints, elevated fuel costs and currency pressures.

At its peak in 2024, Norse Atlantic had 4,822 scheduled flights. That number dipped to 4,695 in 2025 and 2,565 in 2026, according to aviation analytics company Cirium. The airline had 122 flights scheduled for November this year.

Norse Atlantic CEO Roald told CNBC in May that he was not "overly concerned" about access to fuel and said the airline had no indication at the time that the crisis would have an immediate impact. The airline said in October that it was continuing to operate at reduced capacity because of "prolonged high fuel prices."

Correction: This story was updated to reflect that Norse shares closed 85% lower on Wednesday.

#2783. The Nasdaq is in freefall. Here's one stock to look at

I asked Claude after Friday's close what I should do about the tanking stock market. Its advice was surprisingly useful: avoid panic selling and look for opportunities. A 40 VIX, which it touched Friday, might provide those opportunities. Specifically, a higher VIX (higher options premiums) signals fear, but it also creates an opportunity to sell elevated premium in some premium stocks, like Amazon.

Amazon's cloud business is growing at its fastest pace in nearly five years. AWS revenue rose 39.3% YoY in the third quarter, and management reported a $578 billion backlog. Trailing 12-month operating cash flow rose 29% to $168.3 billion. However, infrastructure investment accelerated, resulting in more than $42 billion in negative cash flow. The competing forces of accelerating growth and the cost of supplying it could keep the stock in its range over the next four weeks. Its shares are only off 14% from its recent highs, comparatively better than the Nasdaq Composite's near 19% decline.

AWS demand supports the lower end of the range. The backlog gives Amazon a basis for adding capacity even as investors scrutinize the cash cost. Cash spending could temper an upside rerating.

The next leg higher may require evidence that new capacity can earn attractive returns at this scale, and that evidence may be in short supply over the next couple of months.

Sell the Dec. 18, 2026 AMZN 235/285 strangle for an indicative $5.90 credit per share.

Max reward — $590 per strangle if AMZN finishes between $235 and $285 at expiration, before fees.

Breakevens at expiration — $229.10 and $290.90.

Max risk — Unlimited on the uncovered call, but one should think of the trade as risking getting short at a net price above $290, a nearly 13% premium to today's closing price. The downside loss would reach $22,910 per strangle if AMZN fell to zero. The credit is roughly 2.3% of a $260 reference share price over four weeks, or nearly 30% on a simple annualized basis. That comparison uses the share price as its denominator. Actual return on capital depends on margin and the cost to close or settle the trade.

Buying in bear markets, as the Nasdaq just touched, is never easy. But those opportunities don't present themselves too often. What are you going to do with it?

#2782. Prediction: JSN will break Calvin Johnson's receiving record in 2026

Seattle Seahawks superstar wide receiver Jaxon Smith-Njigba is on the verge of making NFL history, and without ifs or maybes, will do so once the 17-game schedule is complete.

Smith-Njigba currently leads the NFL in receiving yards with 1,207 and is on pace to surpass Calvin Johnson's single-season receiving yards record of 1,964 yards with an astounding 2,051.9 yards. His consistency, explosive playmaking and ability to command targets have made him the centerpiece of Seattle's passing attack. With seven games remaining this season, the record is firmly within reach.

The Seahawks face a challenging final stretch of the year, with games against the San Francisco 49ers, Dallas Cowboys, New York Giants, Philadelphia Eagles, Los Angeles Rams, Carolina Panthers and Rams making up the team's final seven matchups of the year from Weeks 12-18 respectively.

JSN figures to fare well against the Cowboys, who have allowed the second-most receiving yards to opponents this season with 2,770, while the 49ers and Eagles have middle-of-the-pack defenses against the pass. However, the Panthers have allowed the second-fewest receiving yards to opponents in the league this season with 1,961, while the Rams have allowed the fourth-fewest (1,990) and the Giants the fifth-fewest (2,064), making those three tough matchups for Smith-Njigba to prepare for.

Nevertheless, given the stakes of the season and Smith-Njigba's pace entering the final stretch of the year, history is there for the taking. Seattle also needs to produce much better offensively for the rest of the season coming out of its bye week. Expect JSN to rise to the occasion, elevate the offense and ultimately break Johnson's record.

#2781. Nvidia sellers seek new buyers as U.S. curbs China AI chip access, sources tell CNBC

Nvidia chip sellers are seeking new buyers for the semiconductor giant's hardware, three people told CNBC, after the Trump administration tightened rules to curb Chinese companies' remote access to the most advanced artificial-intelligence systems.

Companies have put an unusually high volume of Nvidia graphics processing units, or GPUs, on the market since new measures restricting Chinese companies renting access to advanced Nvidia chips were announced on Nov. 16, the people said.

Jack Duhamel, president of AI infrastructure marketplace GPU Mart, and two people familiar with the matter told CNBC the surge in GPUs for sale was directly attributable to the new rule. Duhamel added that one company looking to sell Nvidia chips told GPU Mart the sale was related to the new rule.

The two people said it appeared chip sellers were seeking to offload them because they were originally for use in data centers in which Chinese companies would rent access. They asked not to be named because they were discussing private information.

The Department of Commerce's Bureau of Industry and Security, or BIS, announced an interim final rule as part of a clampdown on Chinese companies bypassing export controls, which effectively ban the sale of the most advanced Nvidia chips to China, by accessing them remotely in overseas data centers. The rule came into effect on Nov. 19.

Deals to find buyers for thousands of B300 GPUs, one of Nvidia's most advanced offerings, have been floated since the rule was announced, said Duhamel and one of the people.

Nvidia declined to comment. BIS did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

Nvidia has been facing increasing scrutiny from U.S. lawmakers over Chinese companies' access to its advanced chips.

The chip giant warned its customers that the proposed Remote Access Security Act — which has passed the House of Representatives but is yet to pass the Senate — would give the U.S. government broader authority to control access to cloud services, potentially exposing both parties to "legal action," sources previously told CNBC.

In July, the U.S. accused a Chinese AI lab, Moonshot AI, of accessing Nvidia chips via a facility in Thailand. CNBC reported on Nov. 5 that the company had sought access to the chips via data centers in Europe.

U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer told CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Wednesday that the new rule announced last week was intended to close "loopholes."

"For a long time, we have not allowed the sale of Blackwell chips, which are the highest AI chips or similar chips to China directly," he said.

"Some rules were issued by the Commerce Department last week to close off indirect access, either smuggling through places like Southeast Asia or remote access from data centers in third countries."

During Nvidia's third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 17, then-interim CEO Colette Kress said the company was still studying the controls, but expected performance to remain strong because demand for chips far exceeded supply.

#2779. ‘Your booth is waiting’: Denny’s welcomes Jensen Huang back as Nvidia CEO

Jensen Huang is back at the helm of Nvidia — and Denny's has a booth waiting for him.

The American diner chain welcomed the billionaire's return as Nvidia CEO by revealing that it had reinstated a commemorative plaque marking the birthplace of the artificial intelligence chip giant after learning of its removal on Nov. 13.

"Some things you just don't take off the menu. Jensen Huang is one of them," Denny's said in a post on X.

The plaque marks the booth at a Denny's restaurant in Silicon Valley, California, where Huang and his co-founders discussed the idea for Nvidia, which was founded in 1993.

The restaurant chain said it learned on Nov. 13, a day after Huang's resignation, that the plaque had been removed.

"We put the plaque right back up where it belongs," Denny's said, without specifying who had removed it or why.

Huang's ties to the diner chain, however, stretch back well before Nvidia's founding. He worked at Denny's as a teenager, washing dishes and serving customers before going on to build one of the world's most valuable companies.

The restaurant chain also disclosed that it had contacted Nvidia's communications team on Nov. 10 to express its pride in their shared history.

Huang's return came less than two weeks after his sudden resignation rattled investors and raised questions about corporate governance and succession planning at the chipmaker.

While his return has put those issues in the spotlight, Denny's offered a simpler message to its former employee.

"Jensen, your booth is waiting and the coffee's on. We're always open, 24/7," the restaurant chain said.

#2777. Switzerland’s biggest bank set for showdown with lawmakers as capital fight intensifies amid crisis planning

UBS's crisis planning has won qualified support from market regulators, which called its plan "executable" in principle. But Switzerland's biggest bank still faces a major fight over sweeping plans that would force it to hold more capital against its foreign subsidiaries.

FINMA, the Swiss markets regulator, said Wednesday that UBS's resolvability — or its ability to fail safely, without wreaking havoc on the economy — has improved since it integrated rival Credit Suisse, with progress made in implementing the emergency plan across group-wide resolution planning.

The preferred resolution strategy — involving plans to allow for recapitalization and restructuring through the use of loss-absorbing bail-in bonds — is "executable," FINMA said. The regulator formally approved UBS's recovery plan for the first time since its ​Credit Suisse integration.

How UBS would withstand a future crisis remains in sharp focus among Swiss authorities ever since the lender's emergency takeover of stricken rival Credit Suisse in 2023.

UBS is now the country's only global systemically important bank, and the Swiss government wants it to hold stronger capital cushions, specifically in relation to its foreign subsidiaries, which would help it withstand another banking collapse.

UBS has pushed back against the overhaul, saying the measures are disproportionate and would damage its international competitiveness, forcing it to hold an extra $16 billion in capital.

A parliamentary showdown now looms, with the National Council, Switzerland's lower house, scheduled to debate the proposal Dec. 16.

On Tuesday, the lower house's economics committee backed plans to force the bank to support its foreign subsidiaries with 90% high-quality core Common Equity Tier 1, or CET1, capital. The Council of States, the country's upper house, approved the 90% plan in September.

The Swiss government had earlier proposed a 100% threshold. UBS wants it to be no more than 50%.

UBS CEO Sergio Ermotti told CNBC in an interview Oct. 28 that 90% would not allow the company to be a "competitive player" long-term. But he would not be drawn on speculation that the measures could push the bank to relocate out of Switzerland.

In a statement, the bank said the economics committee's recommendation would herald further excessive tightening of Swiss capital requirements, "which are already among the most stringent globally."

FINMA said in its Nov. 25 update that the goal is to avoid government solvency support and emergency law.

"The aim is to enable the authorities, in the event of a crisis, to choose the option that offers the best prospects of success in safeguarding financial stability and ensuring the continuation of systemically important functions," the regulator said.

#2776. Rockets Scurry Over Suns To Another 116-104 Victory

Another game, another 12pt victory that never felt comfortable, but never seemed much in doubt, either. While the previous opponent, the LA Clippers are not a good team, the Suns are good, or at least decent. They beat the Rockets in Phoenix not that long ago, in a game that has been described as strange, or one where the Rockets seemed asleep.

Because I've been dealing with an household electrical problem (that took out the refrigerator) which, of course, cropped up on Thanksgiving Eve; this might be a shorter recap. (I fixed the problem BTW, Thanksgiving is saved, hooray for me.) It's also a shorter recap because it's hard to pin much of a narrative on the past two Rockets game. They look like easy wins, but weren't. The Rockets didn't play their best, but won. The defense didn't seem great, but was. No one player stood out as the star, or the goat, of the team. The offense didn't look great, but it was above NBA average.

In both of the games, Clippers and Suns, it seemed as though the Rockets knew they were more talented than their opponent, turned on the afterburners for a short time, and then just played keep away.

Here's what I mean – quarter by quarter scoring tonight:

The Rockets scored a lot for the first half, then just kind of added slightly to a lead that spent the time from around the middle of the second quarter onward fluctuating between about 10 to 6 points.

The Suns obviously came out in this game with a plan to play physically with the Rockets. This lead to a whopping 26 free throws in the first half for the Rockets. In troubling news, the Rockets made only 14 of those 26 free throws, or this could have been a substantially more comfortable win. Sengun went 2-6, Thompson 4-9, Jabari 3-5, though Durant was more consistent, at 5-6. Where the good free throw shooting from the earliest part of the season has gone is anyone's guess. It hasn't cost the Rockets as it did in Denver, but neither the Suns nor the Clippers are a strong a team as the Nuggets. If it doesn't improve, though, it might cost them as soon as Saturday, when the Rockets face the 15-4 Philadelphia 76ers.

The Suns changed tack in the second half, apparently, and the Rockets only shot six more free throws in the second half.

The Rockets continue not to have a single fulcrum in terms of scoring or usage, but it's clear the engine remains Alperen Sengun. Sengun is averaging 22pts/11rbs/7ast/1stl/1blk/3TO on 55%FG shooting, 63TS%, 50%3pt on 26% usage in 33mpg. If you asked most NBA fans who was playing better, Sengun or Luka Doncic, they'd all say Doncic. But Doncic is shooting worse overall at 46%, his True Shooting Percentage is lower, despite him taking over 10FT per game, his assists are about 3/4 of an assist higher, than Sengun, his rebounds 4 lower, and his usage is sitting at a staggering 41% compared to Sengun's 26%. The argument can be made that Doncic's gaudy points total is largely driven by usage, while Sengun is the more effective player. Yes, Doncic is a heliocentric player, so perhaps he gets less help (perhaps), but that's by design.

Anyhow, Sengun has been fantastic, those are excellent numbers, and his effect is similarly profound for the Rockets. He remains, despite everything, 24 1/3 years old. He's also making 50% of his threes.

At some point we will have to have a serious conversation about Ime Udoka's truest soulmate, Marcus Smart. He's an intense player, and an incredibly streaky shooter. He also can simply lose his mind in a game, and throw a punch, or beef with a fan, or any of a variety of undesirable things. Tonight he almost single-handedly lead a Phoenix comeback tonight by committing a shooting foul on three consecutive plays before he was thankfully removed. Were they all fouls? Pretty much, yes.

We also got a waning seconds, late game stare down standoff, between Dillon Brooks and Kevin Durant. It looked for an instant like Durant was contemplating throwing a punch. If he was Marcus Smart, he probably would have done it. Instead he settled for an unnecessary dunk with seconds remaining. This marked another good game for Durant with 20pts on 7-15 shooting but a woeful 1-7 from three. Extrapolating from that he was almost perfect with his mid range shot, and it looking exactly as great as 7-8.

The next highest scorer was Amen Thompson at 18 points, and it could have been better if he made more free throws. Amen has returned to form, and he had a lot of space to work with near the rim tonight as the Suns seemed extremely focused on Sengun.

The Rockets looked like they might give this one away in the fourth quarter, but the man the Rockets missed last season, Fred VanVleet did indeed ride to the rescue, steadied the ship, toed the line, and took the wheel. So for a second game things might look a bit better on the scoreboard than the Rockets played. At least their mental fragility didn't wreck them. Dillon Brooks would not have let them hear the end of it.

The Rockets and Suns overall played a pretty feisty, chippy game with a lot of the player chosen with the Rockets pick last year Khaman Maluach trying to molest Alperen Sengun in a basketball sense. This was not successful, but he's a powerful player with a nice close shooting touch who might one day be excellent. Today was not that day.

Tomorrow, though, is Thanksgiving! On behalf of TDS, happy Thanksgiving to all who celebrate, and our best wishes to those who don't.

#2774. Palm Beach Turkey Trot sees about 1,700 racers, walkers on 5K route

During an especially rainy November, Palm Beach was blessed with a breezy, sunny Thanksgiving morning, perfect weather for the beloved Turkey Trot 5K race and walk in the North End.

It seems locals agreed too, as roughly 1,700 made their way to Bradley Park for the 18th edition of the event, organized by the Town of Palm Beach United Way.

This year also saw two new winners for the overall best time in the men's and women's categories. Taking home the prize for best overall female time was 44-year-old Laena Romond of Brookfield, Connecticut, with a time of 18:47.

The prize for best overall male runner went to Lake Clarke Shore native Lucas Hernandez-Trujillo, 20, with a time of 17:12.

Hernandez-Trujillo, a sophomore at the University of Florida, told the Palm Beach Daily News the race had become a yearly tradition after he took part three years ago with friends from high school.

"It's always great to just see my friends, the community — and just to race," he told the Daily News. "I love running and it's great to see everyone come together as a community."

The top two finishers in each of the 10 age divisions for male and female runners also won trophies. Every participant also received a Turkey Trot medal after crossing the finish line.

Thanks to the days of rain preceding the event, runners and walkers took an alternative route to avoid standing water on the Lake Trail.

They ran north on Bradley Place to Wells Road, where they took a right and then a left on Crescent Drive. At the end of Crescent, the took another left, heading north on North County Road before taking a left on East Woods Road and looping around via North Lake Way and South Woods Road. They then took the same path back to the finish line.

While some took an intensely competitive approach to the run, others participated in a more relaxed vein as a family tradition. The latter included Wellington resident John Kriegy and 20 members of his family, all of whom showed up in either turkey costumes or fall-colored tutu-style skirts.

It was the Kriegy family's 12th year participating, he told the Daily News. This year was special, however, as they were joined by family visiting from California and six cousins from New Zealand.

"The part of the family from New Zealand, we haven't seen them since 1994," he told the Daily News. "But we always have family participating — my cousins from England usually come — but this time we got all the New Zealand cousins."

Also joining this year's Turkey Trot was a Wellington-based mother-daughter duo, Joan Brewer and Jenny McNabb. At 87, Brewer was the oldest participant in the Thanksgiving race, but her age has done little to stop her energetic spirit.

In July, Brewer traveled to Calgary, Canada, to take part in the 5K 2026 Stampede Road Race, she told the Daily News.

"There were thousands and thousands of people, and I walked for two hours," she said. "So, I figured if I could do that, I can do the Turkey Trot."

This year served as a victory lap, of sorts, for Brewer, who survived a stroke in 2023, just a year after taking part in her first Palm Beach Turkey Trot.

Her daughter still marvels at her mother's commitment. "When we originally conceived of this, I said, 'Mom we could just watch, or just turn around whenever,'" McNabb said. "But she said, 'No, no, I want to cross the finish line.'"

Participants this year also included Town Hall staff, including Palm Beach Fire-Rescue Chief Sean Baker, who was joined by his colleagues, all of whom were rocking shirts given to them by the Palm Beach Police & Fire Foundation.

Town officials, meanwhile, could be spotted among the spectators cheering on racers, including Town Council President Pro-Tem Lew Crampton and Council Member Nicki McDonald.

Palm Beach Mayor Danielle Moore also made an appearance to welcome attendees and lead the countdown to start the race. Former Mayor Gail Coniglio also took the stage to announce the top times and handout trophies following the race.

The sold-out event was capped at around 1,700, but even so, organizers registered around 40 racers just before the race, said co-chair Francesca Coniglio Finck.

"It definitely feels very special" to see the large crowds, said Coniglio Finck, who co-chaired the event with her husband William Finck and her sister Gabrielle Coniglio Fricker, whose husband, Scott Fricker also was a co-chair.

"The impact it has on the community clearly shows based on the event's growth," she continued. "It shows people care about their community."

That's because a key impact of the event is the money it raises through registration fees and sponsorships to help provide meals to Palm Beach County's less fortunate. This year, Town of Palm Beach United Way was able to provide meals to 7,601 people through 30 partner agencies, according to the organizaton.

The race started at 7:30 a.m. The event also included music, a Kids Drumstick Dash, a charity raffle, face painting and balloon animals for the kids. Local vendors Pumphouse Coffee Roaster and Aioli provided drinks and bakery treats.

The Palm Beach Police & Fire Foundation was the event's Premier Sponsor. Island Champion sponsors included Capehart Photography; Douglas Elliman Real Estate | Lisa Wilkinson and Cara Coniglio McClure; Findlay Galleries; Flagler Technologies; Hotel Planner; and Luxury Lifestyle Foundation.

Bruce Gendelman Insurance Services, Christian Angle Real Estate, Crest Air, Pumphouse Coffee Roasters and Sanders Circle Foundation comprised the Leadership Sponsors.

Community Champion Sponsors included Barron International Group, Café L'Europe, Caraccio Pools Construction, College Admissions Counseling Group, Future Waves, Goosehead Insurance, Haynes Scaffolding & Supply, J.E.T. Fitness, Kourtney Pulitzer Properties, Moonlight Graham, Morgan Stanley, Simon Isaacs Real Estate, and Surovek Gallery.

#2773. What is ‘texture-maxxing’? The food trend putting crunch on the menu

Texture-maxxing is the latest food trend taking social media by storm as consumers, driven by aesthetics and taste, look for a sensory experience.

British supermarket Waitrose's latest food and drink report highlighted "texture-maxxing" as one of the trends of the year. It describes the phenomenon as a multi-sensory indulgence that is "fuelled by texture-maxxing crunch and intense flavour hits," pushing shoppers to fill their trolleys with crunchy peanut butter, dumplings, and trifles.

"This year, the sound of food went mainstream," Waitrose's Senior Development Chef Will Torrent said in the Wednesday report. "Driven by social media's obsession with audible, multi-sensory cooking, we're creating high-contrast meals that combine shatter-crisp crunch with pillowy chew."

"The emphasis in social media content has flipped from narrative voiceovers to ASMR-inspired audio, where the evocative sounds of food are just as important as the visuals," he added.

Food trends on social media have ranged from the obvious to the absurd. Fibermaxxing and proteinmaxxing have taken off in the past few years. Meanwhile, food and beverages like Dubai chocolate, protein coffee, and ube were some of the big social media hits.

Nestlé's USA 2026 trends found that 75% of Gen Z and 80% of millennials sought out texture in snack cravings with the hashtag "crunchtok," amassing 1.5 billion views.

Waitrose noted that shoppers looking to maximize on texture bought its viral No.1 Alphonso Mango Dessert, which involves "cracking through the crisp shell" to access a "smooth mango mousse and passion fruit curd on a crunchy white chocolate base."

Waitrose's TikTok and Instagram videos of eating the dessert were watched by more than two million people, with a whopping 6,173 hours and 36 minutes of play time on TikTok. Its videos featuring crunchy toast also went viral.

The U.K. grocer's sales of jarred chili crunches, which include crispy rayu oils such as itsu Crispy Chilli Oil, saw a 13-fold jump. Meanwhile, crunchy peanut butter outsold smooth.

Dumplings also had a moment, with sales of chicken gyoza dumplings up 116% this year and searches on waitrose.com for gochujang and peanut butter udon noodles jumped 60%.

"We've seen an explosion in demand as shoppers treat dumplings not just as a quick weeknight bite, but as a total sensory experience," Waitrose's Executive Chef Stephen Parkins-Knight said.

"Driven by the rise of texture-maxxing, people want that ultimate contrast: delicate skins, juicy fillings and crispy skirts, topped with very chilli crunch. With innovation ranging from fusion fillings to rich dipping broths, dumplings have taken a delicious centre stage in 2026," he added.

Texture-maxxing also extended to desserts, as shoppers bought twice as many chocolate trifles compared to 2025, and sales of strawberry trifles increased 160%.

Tiramisu was also popular, with searches for waitrose.com's tiramisu recipe page increasing 155% year-on-year.

#2772. Spurs drop NBA Cup game to Trail Blazers despite Wembanyama’s big game

It felt like a playoff game in the Moda Center on the night before Thanksgiving. Maybe it was the drive to win an NBA Cup game, or maybe it was the Portland Trail Blazers wanting revenge on the San Antonio Spurs from last year's playoffs, and a thumping earlier this season. Either way, the Blazers were motivated, and the crowd was raucous. The Spurs kept pace for most of the game, but faltered late in a 116-111 loss.

It looked like Victor Wembanyama would will the Spurs to victory without De'Aaron Fox and Dylan Harper, who missed the game with injuries. Wembanyama had it going in the post, scoring 44 points on 17-for-30 shooting. Immediately, he took Blazers center Donovan Clingan into the low post, scoring over him or forcing him to foul, eventually leading to Clingan fouling out.

However, Wembanyama's effort wasn't enough to overcome Portland getting stellar performances from two of their stars. Damian Lillard looked like prime Dame, hitting three after three over the Spurs defenders. Lillard had 29 points and 6 assists while shooting 5-of-12 from three. He was outshined by his teammate, Deni Avdija, who hit the big bank three at the end of the game to seal the win for Portland. He had 33 points, 12 rebounds, and 7 assists on an efficient 11-of-21 shooting from the field.

San Antonio came into the fourth quarter seemingly taking control. A Devin Vassell fadeaway three-pointer to beat the third-quarter buzzer put the team up three points. After struggling to shoot the ball in the first half, the team got it going in the third quarter, shooting 6-of-10 from three-point range. With Portland out of challenges and its big man in foul trouble, the Spurs had several advantages to steal a win on the road.

Immediately, the Blazers went on a 12-2 run to take a 7-point lead early in the fourth quarter. They played with a lot of force on both ends. That was particularly clear when Sidy Cissoko drove right into Wembanyama and dunked over the top of him. The former Spur had 16 points, knocking down four of his ten three-pointers.

All game long, but especially down the stretch, the Blazers were able to get into the Spurs' ball-handlers' space and force mistakes. San Antonio turned the ball over 14 times, giving up 14 points off turnovers. That defensive pressure especially hurt the Spurs in the final two minutes of the game, when San Antonio turned the ball over two possessions in a row with the chance to tie the game.

Stephon Castle took on the majority of the ball-handling responsibilities and had an up-and-down game. Castle finished with 20 points, 9 assists, 6 rebounds, and 3 turnovers while shooting 6-of-18 from the field. He was stopped in the backcourt by Toumani Camara for an 8-second violation late in the game that gave the Blazers a shot of momentum heading into the end of the game.

Overall, it was hard for the Spurs to find an offensive rhythm outside of forcing the ball to Wembanyama in the high or low post. San Antonio shot just 43% from the field and 28% from three.

With the loss, the Spurs fall to 2-1 in the Western Conference Group C. They are in a four-way tie for first with Portland, the Golden State Warriors, and the Los Angeles Lakers. The Spurs play the Lakers, and the Blazers play the Warriors on Friday. San Antonio needs to win that game, and Portland to lose, to win the group outright. San Antonio may have a shot at the wild card spot if the Blazers and Spurs win.
