#2752. Bills vs. Chiefs, Week 12 preview: 5 questions with Arrowhead Pride

Five questions is back this week, Rumblers! All eyes are on the Buffalo Bills and Kansas City Chiefs when they play, which means there's always room to chat about the greatest current NFL rivalry. That it's happening on Thanksgiving night is gravy.

If you've been a Bills fan long enough, you already know the mystique and magic that surrounds this head-to-head series between quarterbacks Josh Allen and Patrick Mahomes. But there's so much more to these games than that alone.

To get us ready for Bills-Chiefs I spoke with Maurice Elston, who writes in-depth analysis, film study breakdowns, and more for Arrowhead Pride. Here, we dive a bit further into who and what the Chiefs click in 2026.

Below, Maurice and I tackle everything from the biggest differences between this year's team and previous rosters, plus a look at Kansas City's greatest strength and most glaring weakness. We also dive into what wide receiver Josh Downs has brought to the team since joining the team at the trade deadline.

Then, Maurice explains what played into the Chiefs' pair of losses compared to their dominant wins, and he closes with a look at what has to go right if Kansas City is to win at Highmark Stadium.

Read on below for some fantastic insight from Maurice into a team you can't ever learn enough about!

1. Catch us up with this season's Chiefs, if you can. What's different about them as compared to previous seasons?

Maurice Elston: The biggest difference is that the Chiefs finally have an efficient running game—and a coaching staff willing to lean into it and stick with it. Kenneth Walker III has given Kansas City the dynamic running back it has lacked for much of the Patrick Mahomes era. Now, with Walker injured, fifth-round rookie Emmett Johnson has demonstrated that the success of the running game can continue without him.

That has made the Chiefs a much more balanced offense. They can operate from under center, use play action and stay committed to the run instead of placing the entire game on Mahomes' shoulders. Mahomes is still capable of taking over when necessary, but he no longer has to perform heroics every week for Kansas City to win. That balance has been the defining difference between this team and the Chiefs offenses of recent seasons.

2. At 8-2, what do you see as the Chiefs' greatest strength, and their most glaring weakness — and how might either be impacted vs. Bills?

Maurice Elston: Kansas City's greatest strength is its balance. The Chiefs can run the football effectively and build their play-action passing game from it, which is something they haven't consistently had during the Mahomes era. Even without Walker, Johnson has rushed for 206 yards over the last two games and shown that Kansas City doesn't have to abandon its offensive identity.

The most glaring weakness is the lack of continuity along the offensive line. The Chiefs still haven't played a game with the original starting five they envisioned entering training camp. Injuries and lineup changes have forced players into unfamiliar roles throughout the season.

That will be important against Buffalo, especially with right tackle Jaylon Moore ruled out. The Bills know Kansas City will want to establish Johnson and protect Mahomes with play action. If Buffalo can stop the run without committing extra defenders and then attack the Chiefs' backup tackles, that could disrupt the balance that has made Kansas City so successful.

3. What has WR Josh Downs brought to/done for the offense since KC traded for him?

Maurice Elston: If the Chiefs were missing anything at wide receiver, it was someone who could consistently create separation against man and press coverage. Downs has brought them a polished route runner who understands how to defeat leverage, set defenders up with his releases and find openings in the middle of a defense.

The production has already been encouraging. In his first two games with Kansas City, Downs has eight receptions for 112 yards and a touchdown, with six of those catches resulting in first downs. That is significant production considering how little time he has had to learn Andy Reid's offense.

His presence also helps the other receivers because defenses now have another player they must respect opposite Travis Kelce. The more comfortable Downs becomes with the playbook, the more Kansas City can expand his role. He gives the Chiefs a separation skill set that has largely been missing since Tyreek Hill left Kansas City.

4. KC's two losses came against playoff/SB teams in the Broncos and Seahawks. What's the real story behind those defeats — especially only putting up a single field goal against Seattle — as compared to the way they've played in eight wins?

Maurice Elston: The two losses were different. Kansas City moved the ball and scored 24 points against Denver, but pressure and offensive-line breakdowns hurt the Chiefs in critical situations. Seattle was a complete offensive collapse.

The Seahawks committed to stopping Walker, challenged Kansas City's receivers in man coverage and generated pressure without having to compromise their coverage. Kansas City finished with only 213 yards, while Mahomes completed just six of 13 passes for 44 yards and an interception when pressured. When the receivers couldn't separate and the offensive line couldn't hold up in obvious passing situations, Mahomes became unsettled and the entire offense lost its rhythm.

Since those losses, Downs has given the Chiefs another answer against man coverage, Kahlil Benson has gained valuable experience at left tackle and Mahomes has looked increasingly comfortable in the pocket. Those are positive developments, but the real test begins now. Kansas City's upcoming stretch against contending teams will show whether those problems have truly been corrected or were merely hidden during the eight victories.

5. What do you see as the biggest key to success for the Chiefs, if they accomplish their main goal Thanksgiving night?

Maurice Elston: As dynamic as James Cook has been, the biggest key is containing Josh Allen and eliminating the explosive plays he creates outside the structure of Buffalo's offense.

Many of Allen's most damaging plays happen when a defense appears to have him stopped. A pass rusher loses containment, Allen escapes the pocket and suddenly turns a potential sack into a long run or a completion downfield. Kansas City's defensive line must maintain disciplined rush lanes instead of becoming so focused on sacks that it gives Allen an escape route.

When the Chiefs have an opportunity to bring him down, they must finish the play. Keeping Allen in the pocket, forcing him to operate on schedule and preventing those backbreaking second-reaction plays will be the biggest key to Kansas City leaving Buffalo with a Thanksgiving night victory.

My sincere thanks to Maurice Elston for his generous time and willingness to connect with me for five questions this week. I predict we'll get another round of questions later in winter. You can read more of Maurice's writing over at Arrowhead Pride. Be sure to check out my turn in the hot seat answering five of Maurice's questions, at AP!

#2751. Trump demands probe into ‘record setting error’ after third-quarter GDP data gets massive revision higher

President Donald Trump on Thursday falsely deemed the latest gross domestic product revision a mistake, claiming that it should be "immediately investigated."

Data released Wednesday from the Commerce Department revealed that the U.S. economy exhibited much stronger growth for the third quarter than initially estimated, showing that real GDP grew at a 4.3% annualized rate. That was revised upward from the department's advance estimate of 3.4%.

That revision was a "RECORD SETTING ERROR," Trump said in a Truth Social post, suggesting that the initial release could have influenced the outcome of the 2026 U.S. midterm elections, where Democrats flipped both chambers of Congress and even multiple governorships.

"I would like to wish a Happy Thanksgiving to even those who issued fake Economic Numbers, just prior to the Election, and corrected them yesterday, three weeks late, showing that the GDP was actually 4.3%, versus 3.4%, A RECORD SETTING ERROR, and one so grave that it should be immediately investigated," Trump wrote.

However, the difference between the advance estimate and the figure in the second release is far from being an all-time record. It was the largest revision since the 1.2 percentage point revision seen in the second quarter of 2020.

On an absolute basis, the latest revision was tied for 16th among all revisions from the advance estimate to the second release since 1990. It also tied four other quarters with a similar 0.9 percentage point difference — and was approximately double the average revision of 0.43 percentage points for all 147 quarters.

The upward revision shows Trump's policies are "delivering results" that defy expectations, White House spokesman Matthew Middleton told CNBC.

Revisions to U.S. economic numbers are not necessarily out of the ordinary. Initial reports count on partial survey data and early estimates. Those data are later updated with more survey responses — which sometimes leads to changes from the initial reports.

GDP data is revised repeatedly over a five year period. By the time the final revision is done, the average absolute error from the second release to the final is 1.14 percentage points.

The advance estimate for Q3 U.S. GDP, which came just days before Election Day in early November, was still better than the figure economists had expected at the time. It also showed a significant step-up in growth from the second quarter.

Because that was a strong showing for the economy, some on Wall Street consider this week's revision to be a confirmation of the economy's recent growth trend.

Jim Baird of Plante Moran Financial Advisors said immediately following the release that it "reinforces an increasingly familiar tension" that the economy is "performing quite well, but its resilience also gives the Fed an even stronger case for further rate hikes."

"The economy isn't merely hanging in there. It continues to demonstrate considerable resilience, with consumers spending, businesses investing and few signs that restrictive monetary policy is creating significant cracks in the broader expansion," the firm's chief investment officer said.

Traders are currently pricing in a nearly 100% chance of a hike at the Federal Open Market Committee's December meeting, per the CME FedWatch tool. They're also anticipating an additional hike in January.

— CNBC's Jeff Cox and Fred Imbert contributed reporting

#2750. Southington football defeats Cheshire in Apple Valley Classic with 21-point fourth quarter

CHESHIRE - Set the table. Dig in. Don't hold back. Dessert can wait until tomorrow.

Playing more like high school football teams that had to win to get in more than teams that already had CIAC postseason berths secured, Southington and Cheshire did nothing more than add to Apple Valley Classic lore Thursday morning at Alumni Field.

Southington scored 21 unanswered points in the fourth quarter to erase a four-point deficit, overcome a state record-tying field goal and pull away to a 41-24 victory.

That's a mouthful, and all before dinner time, but the fare at the 30th edition of this holiday border war was that diverse and that good.

Southington senior running back Nick Andrews, the game MVP, rushed for three touchdowns, including the go-ahead score early in the fourth quarter.

Junior quarterback Jacoby Roman ran for one TD in between throwing a pair to senior wide receiver Walker Mierzejewski as the Blue Knights raised the Apple Valley trophy for a third straight year.

In the process, they secured the No. 1 seed in the Class LL playoffs at 9-1, which would give them a home date with No. 8 NFA in the quarterfinals on Tuesday. Pairings will be made official by the CIAC on Friday morning.

Cheshire, at 7-3, slid to No. 4 in the Class L rankings and would host No. 5 Lewis Mills/Wolcott Tech in its quarterfinal.

Against Southington, the Rams got a pair of rushing touchdowns from its offensive MVP, sophomore Oliver Donroe, a rushing score from junior quarterback Ryan Plouffe and a 56-yard field goal from senior kicker Tim Reinhard. The kick gives Reinhard a share of the state record with Hall's Noe Ruelas, who boomed a 56-yarder in 2019.

Reinhard's boot, with a northeast wind at his back, kick-started a 10-point third quarter that erased a 20-14 halftime deficit and put Cheshire ahead 24-20 heading into the final frame.

Southington retook the lead at 27-24 on Andrews' third TD carry on a rapid-fire, 80-yard scoring drive that opened the fourth quarter.

Then came a key sequence: a high Cheshire snap at the 40 that, in the wild pursuit, bounded all the way down to the 4-yard line, where it was recovered by Southington's Owen Manuel.

"When I saw the ball on the ground, I knew I had to take advantage of the opportunity and make a play for my team." said Manuel.

A holding call erased a fourth touchdown run by Andrews. This time, it was Roman who found pay-turf, gaining the left pylon on fourth down from the 2 with 6:40 left.

From there, Southington turned Cheshire over on downs and got an exclamation point TD from 45 yards from the speedy and dynamic Mierzejewski, who was back in action after missing several games to injury.

The Blue Knights were without junior running back Ben Beaulieu, who suffered an ankle injury in practice. Beaulieu is expected back for the postseason.

Southington now leads the Thanksgiving series 19-11 and the all-time series with Cheshire 28-20.

Nick Andrews. With backfield partner Ben Beaulieu out with an ankle injury, Southington's senior running back stepped up with rushing touchdowns of 36, 39 and 4 yards.

#2749. Kalshi daily volume of ‘perps’ trading begins to top its prediction markets

Kalshi is known for its prediction markets across sports, politics and culture.

But now, for the first time, perpetual futures, Kalshi's second financial product, has surpassed the notional trading volume of its event contracts on consecutive days.

On Thursday, perpetual futures — commonly known as "perps" — on Kalshi saw $3.4 billion in notional volume as artificial intelligence laboratory Anthropic stock debuted on public markets while the rest of the market sold off. That exceeded about $3 billion in notional volume for Kalshi's prediction markets on the same day.

Perps are futures-style contracts where there is no expiration and traders do not need to own the underlying asset. Contracts instead continuously track the price of an asset, with funding payments to keep the contract price aligned with that of the market. Funding payments are made periodically between holders of short or long positions.

Perps trading surpassed event contracts one day in September, but on Friday perps topped prediction markets volume for a second day. Now Kalshi expects that performance to continue as it introduces perps tied to more individual stocks. On Monday, for example, Kalshi launched contracts linked to Anthropic.

Kalshi was the first to debut perps in the U.S. after winning federal regulatory approval in May to list perps tied to cryptocurrencies.

Perps trading volume includes any leverage that speculators acquire along with their trade, while event contracts are fully collateralized — for now.

Since the original launch in May, Kalshi has received approval to introduce perps tied to metals, crude oil, stock indexes and individual stocks.

Offshore perps — those available on exchanges not regulated in the U.S. — generated more than $90 trillion in notional trading volume in 2025, so it's not surprising that the product has grown so rapidly on Kalshi, according to Udesh Jha, chief risk officer at Kalshi Klear, the company's clearing house.

"We're just in very early innings. We're launching products, we're growing the participant base."

Perps were Kalshi's first product to mark its transition from a prediction market-focused platform to a full-blown financial exchange where various assets are traded. Last week, reports said that Kalshi is planning to offer trading on equities on its platform in the coming months.

At the same time, the company's perps product has come under fire. Some incumbent exchanges where futures are traded — which saw their stock prices decline following the approval of U.S. perps — believe the product was improperly approved. CME Group is suing the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, the federal derivatives regulator, for approving perps.

Others have simply expressed concerns about the efficiency of the markets. CNBC previously reported that trading volume on Kalshi's most popular perps markets — tied to bitcoin and ether cryptocurrencies — was dramatically greater than open interest, or cash actively in the markets. It's a phenomenon that isn't common with U.S.-regulated traditional futures, nor other offshore perps markets.

Kalshi said at the time it has "zero concerns" about the distinction, and that it appears because less leverage is offered compared to offshore exchanges and there are fewer incentives for traders to park capital on its perps markets.

While Kalshi started with cryptocurrencies, Owen Lau, an analyst at Clear Street, said perps tied to individual stocks likely represent the most promising opportunity for growth in the U.S.

"That's how I foresee the way perps will penetrate further into traditional asset classes," Lau said. "Perps on single stocks and [key performance indicators on individual companies] could change how investors hedge their portfolio or even make a directional bet."

Kalshi's growth has been powered by the fact that right now it's the only exchange that can actively list perps in the U.S., though crypto exchange Coinbase received approval in May to route U.S. users to offshore platforms for trading. That monopoly status, however, will probably prove short lived.

Coinbase has filed to list single-stock perps in the U.S., and President Donald Trump in August said the CFTC is working to bring Hyperliquid — a dominant player in offshore perps activity — to domestic traders.

But Jha at Kalshi isn't worried about the competition.

"What will carry us is ... making sure that we have the right products for the marketplace, making sure we have the right surveillance of the products that we are trading."

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2748. Art Rooney II backs Mike McCarthy, rips Aaron Rodgers' ultimatum

Art Rooney II heard Aaron Rodger's complaints and doesn't care. The Pittsburgh Steelers owner and president backed Mike McCarthy Thursday, Nov. 26, saying he will be the team's coach "this season, next season and beyond."

A day after the team waived Rodgers, Rooney came out and made it clear he backed the coach. The 42-year old quarterback had spent about 30 minutes on "The Pat McAfee Show" going after McCarthy, hours after he had been benched for Mason Rudolph.

"The big thing, what happened this week, is insubordination can't be tolerated in any organization," Rooney told the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette. "When you start getting ultimatums, you can't live with that. "

Rooney also had no time for Rodgers' suggestion that McCarthy is unwell.

"Mike's as healthy as the rest of us," Rooney said. "Health was not an issue. I'm not sure where that part comes from."

In a statement to ESPN, Rooney said he was "disappointed in where this went with Aaron Rodgers," but has "100% confidence in Mike McCarthy."

Rooney had an issue with Rodgers' airing his complaints where and when he did.

"There's maybe a time and place after the season, if he wanted to kind of unload," Rooney said. "He talks about wanting to be a good teammate, but you don't do that to your teammates a couple of days before the game, that's for sure.

"In general, I think, historically, what's in the locker room stays in the locker room," he said.

Rooney also said McCarthy spent the season covering for his quarterback.

"The sad thing is Mike took some of the bullets for Aaron in terms of some of the plays that were called, particularly in the red-zone troubles we had," Rooney said. "He tried to protect him, and it just wasn't working."

Rodgers said he went to McCarthy on Nov. 23 and told him to hand play-calling to another coach or release him. The Post-Gazette, citing sources, reported a sharper version from a meeting the next night. "Either let me call all the plays or just release me." McCarthy then met with Rooney and general manager Omar Kahn. Later that night, the team announced Rudolph would start when the Steelers (6-4) play the Denver Broncos on Black Friday.

Rodgers, who played for McCarthy in Green Bay from 2006-2018, said he found out from tight end Pat Freiermuth, not McCarthy.

#2747. Grading the Mavericks: Dallas’ accountability helped prevent a serious fall

The Mavericks went 4-2 over the last two weeks and sit at 11-7, in eighth place in the West. They lost back-to-back road games to Cleveland (127-117) and Boston (131-94), but rebounded with four straight wins against Golden State (116-94), the Clippers (110-100), Utah (116-114), and Denver (120-101). Cooper Flagg led the team in scoring with 23.7 points per game.

The Mavericks did not start the last two weeks well. Losing back-to-back road games in embarrassing fashion, then giving a suboptimal performance against a really hurt Golden State team was pretty dire, despite the large margin of victory. The three games since have been pretty convincing; the Mavericks are a good team somewhere in the realm of "better than bad, not elite, but can win on any night".

We have seen the emergence, or more accurately, the rebirth of Caleb Martin in the last six games. He is scoring over 10 points per game, shooting 56.5 percent from three, and grabbing 2.3 steals per game over that stretch. Martin is the true definition of a glue guy and has held the team together in their four-game win streak.

The win against Denver was incredible. It was an utterly dominant defensive performance. It is the type of win you have to build off of and refuse to let the train slow down. It was also Dallas' third NBA Cup victory, meaning if they beat Phoenix on Friday, they will win their group.

Dallas' two losses that applied to this round's grades were part of a three-game skid that lacked effort in every sense of the word. Losing to a Cleveland team that sat all of their starters after travel issues was bad, but going into Boston the next day and losing the game in roughly six minutes (after Dallas went down 24-4) was a true wake-up call. Cooper Flagg said after the loss: "I don't think we competed at a high enough level, you know, throughout the three games".

Every viewer noticed that, but having a guy in the fire acknowledge their shortcomings showed they could get through the adversity. And they did, to the tune of four straight wins. Even after the third victory in Utah, where they squandered a 17-point lead in three-and-a-half minutes, Kyrie Irving held the team, including the coaches, after practice to run in preparation for their game in Denver's altitude. It paid off with a 19-point road victory.

That is the sign of a good team. Being able to have honest conversations about your play and notice when things need to change is a key component of success. The Mavericks clearly have that. There will be more adversity and plenty more losses to come this season. If the Mavericks can lean on this framework, they will be solid, no matter what happens.

The Mavericks already have a weird roster, but the player that sticks out the most is Naji Marshall. He is not a good enough defender to justify the offensive dominance, and he turns the ball over far too much to be handling the ball at the rate he does. Part of his 20 percent usage rate is the fact that the roster is lacking ball handlers, but some of it, and perhaps the most frustrating part, is that he simply chooses to play hero ball far too often.

It's not clear when you look at his 10.9 points in 24.1 minutes per game. And sometimes he does play well enough to matter. But it's the choice to keep a dribble handoff, or the pass that he forces in transition, or the insistence on taking floaters and shrinking the floor that really hurts the team. The extension Dallas signed him to already feels cumbersome, and I expect the best version of this team to not have him on the floor. Whether that is because he is benched or traded away, I am not sure.

Maybe we have been spoiled by the likes of Luka Doncic and Cooper Flagg, or it's the guys picked behind him playing well, but Johnson Jr., the ninth overall pick in this past draft, has felt overwhelmingly underwhelming. His athleticism does not jump off the screen a lot of the time, his offensive awareness is sketchy, and he fouls a bit too much to stay in the game consistently. That being said, he has shown flashes of being a really good glue piece to a great defensive team.

One of these came against Dallas' statement win in Denver, where he scored nine points and had a team-high 10 rebounds. He played excellent defense, had a monster dunk, moved his feet incredibly well, and impacted winning on all fronts. It is this type of game that makes you wonder why he can't do this all the time. Some of it may be physical, with the shin splints still affecting him. A lot of it is probably mental. But either way, although the consistency isn't there yet, you can see the vision finally.

#2746. Rams shut down Packers in second half for a 26-13 win in NFL's first Thanksgiving Eve game

The Rams and Packers played to a tie in the first 30 minutes of Wednesday night’s game in Los Angeles, but the second half was all Rams.

Blake Corum ran for 151 yards and a score in final 30 minutes and the Rams defense shut out the Packers to pave the way to a 26-13 win. The victory is the fifth in a row for the Rams and they are now 7-4 with a Thursday night home game against the Chiefs on deck in Week 13.

The win moves the Rams ahead of the Packers in the race for NFC playoff spots and Thursday’s game between the Lions and Bears will have further impact on the chances for all four teams to advance to the postseason.

Corum’s touchdown came at the end of the Rams’ first possession of the second half and he finished the game with a career-high 32 carries for 184 yards.

That scoring drive ate up more than eight minutes. The Packers had a quick three-and-out, but briefly showed some life when linebacker Ty’Ron Hopper picked off Matthew Stafford to set the offense up in Los Angeles territory. Jordan Love turned the ball right back over when he hung a deep pass that safety Jaylen McCullough snagged out of the air. The Rams would then ride Corum and Kyren Williams on a pair of drives that covered more than 11 minutes.

The Packers’ last chance came with 6:13 left and they moved the ball to midfield before Love threw his second interception of the game. Cornerback Emmanuel Forbes got that one and the Packers were out of timeouts well before the two-minute warning.

Sticking with the ground game helped to limit Packers defensive end Micah Parsons’s impact in his return from last season’s torn ACL. He had a sack late in the first half, but Stafford only threw eight times after the break and the Packers’ run defense could not get their offense the ball often enough to get back into the game.

Love was 17-of-29 for 198 yards and a touchdown, but clearly missed wide receiver Christian Watson after he left the game with a concussion. They only picked up two first downs after halftime and they’ll hope that an extended break before their Week 13 trip to New Orleans is enough time for him to return to action.

The Rams have several injuries of their own to monitor as guard Bill Murray joined tight ends Davis Allen and Tyler Higbee in making early exits from the game. They’d like to have all those players back next week, but they showed on Wednesday night that Corum can sometimes be enough all by himself.

#2743. T.J. Lang blasts "despicable" Aaron Rodgers comments about Mike McCarthy

T.J. Lang blocked for Aaron Rodgers for eight seasons in Green Bay. Currently, Lang would be more inclined to knock Rodgers on his ass.

Lang sharply criticized Rodgers for his decision to go public with issues that should have stayed in the Pittsburgh locker room.

“I think it’s despicable,” Lang said on 97.1 The Ticket in Detroit, via Sean Keeley of Awful Announcing. “I think that Aaron Rodgers just became everything that he has bitched about and moaned about over the last 10 years with former coaches, former teammates, airing grievances out, giving opinions out in the media.

“He has blacklisted, I don’t know how many, former teammates from just simple comments telling the truth because he just — he was sick of it. Honestly, I don’t even know if I want to watch the whole thing. It’s just unnecessary. It’s a victim mentality. It’s a ‘poor me, woe is me. Oh, I thought this, and it wasn’t that.’ And I’m sick, man.

“I’ve been in a group chat with a whole lot of former teammates that feel the exact same way that I do.”

Most football players would feel that way. There’s a code in the locker room. Rodgers violated it.

“Mike McCarthy was my head coach for 8 years, and I love Mike McCarthy,” Lang said. “And I talked to Mike McCarthy on Monday night, and still have a great relationship with him.

“I haven’t talked to Aaron Rodgers in probably 5 years. I can’t remember the last time he returned a text. I don’t have a relationship with him. And I think it’s just absolutely despicable what he just did to Big Mike.”

Rodgers isn’t a whistleblower. He’s a troublemaker. He didn’t get his way, so he emptied a can of gasoline and tossed a match over his shoulder on the way out the door.

How does any of it help his now-former teammates? Rodgers’s decision to air out dirty laundry has created a massive distraction that will do nothing to help the team win.

Accurate or not, the task at hand becomes harder now that everyone is buzzing about Rodgers’s comments.

That said, no one should be surprised. It’s part and parcel of doing business with Rodgers. When it ends — and end it will — it will end poorly. Like it did in Green Bay. Like it did in New York. Like it did in Pittsburgh.

Like it will end for his next team, if there is one. And if there is, the message is clear.

With the rest of the NFC North already completing their games, the Vikings will already know where they stack up in the standings when the game kicks off.

Sep 20, 2026; Chicago, Illinois, USA; Minnesota Vikings cornerback Isaiah Rodgers (2) reacts to a call during the second half against the Chicago Bears at Soldier Field. Mandatory Credit: Matt Marton-Imagn Images

Last week, in a pivotal meeting with the San Francisco 49ers, they didn't have starting cornerback Isaiah Rodgers for the first time in the past two seasons. It led to the Vikings' worst defensive performance of the season, allowing a season-high 35 points.

A week may not have been enough time for Rodgers' quadriceps injury to heal, as he still hasn't been able to return to practice. Yet he's not the only Vikings starter that is currently on track to miss Sunday's game.

The Vikings released their latest injury report on Thursday, and two big names were still listed as DNP or 'Did Not Participate.' While Jordan Addison's inactivity was expected, Rodgers and starting left tackle Christian Darrisaw's absences aren't a great sign as Sunday's game approaches.

If Darrisaw's unable to go, odds are the Vikings will turn to free agent signee Ryan Van Demark in his place at left tackle. Demark has started one game and appeared in a total of 10 games for the Vikings this season, playing 130 snaps in the process.

#2740. Bears TE Colston Loveland carted into locker room, ruled out with knee injury

Chicago Bears tight end Colston Loveland has been ruled out with a knee injury after being carted into the locker room.

Loveland appeared to suffer the injury early in the Thanksgiving matchup against the Detroit Lions, and was taken into the medical tent in the first quarter. He was later transported to the locker room on the cart. The tight end, who put a towel over his head, was clearly emotional as he left the field.

Loveland appeared to suffer the injury on Chicago's second drive, with a Detroit defender rolling up on the 22-year-old tight end's left leg while he was blocking for Bears running back D'Andre Swift.

Shortly after the start of the second quarter, the Bears ruled Loveland out for the rest of the game.

Prior to the injury, Loveland had lost a fumble on Chicago's opening drive, after the ball was punched out by Lions defensive end Derrick Moore. Detroit went three-and-out on the ensuing drive.

Rookie tight end Sam Roush and veteran Stephen Carlson stepped up to fill in the gap left by Loveland: Carlson had two huge catches in the Bears' touchdown drive in the second quarter.

Loveland is a key piece of the Bears' offense, with the third-most receiving yards on the team this season. Chicago already lost tight end Cole Kmet to a season-ending knee injury last month, leaving glaring holes in the Bears' depth at tight end going forward.

#2739. AI debt markets shudder as Nvidia's top 'banker' returns

AI credit markets are shuddering again with Jensen Huang back as CEO of Nvidia.

Fears about the massive debt being taken on to fund the artificial intelligence buildout climbed Tuesday as Wall Street weighed what Huang's return to Nvidia means for its bankrolling of the AI boom.

Huang is considered one of the top financiers, or bankers, in the AI industry, spearheading deals with neoclouds like CoreWeave and other firms to buy chips, backed in some cases by Nvidia guarantees. His return to Nvidia has revived prospects for the company to continue "circular financing" of AI chips and data centers — raising concerns among credit investors.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child of AI debt concerns, saw its stock sink 13% and the yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 top 17%. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

At the same time, the spread on the neocloud company's five-year credit default swaps jumped 8.5% to over 1,200 basis points. Credit default swaps are insurance against nonpayment and are considered a barometer of perceived risks. Rising CDS spreads mean investors view the debt as riskier.

Huang was reinstated as CEO on Monday after stepping down on Nov. 12 amid allegations of multiple affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy. His reinstatement on Monday was cheered by investors, who pushed stocks higher, yields lower and spreads on CDS tighter. However, stocks retreated, bond yields rose and CDS spreads widened on Tuesday.

Ben Emons, founder and chief investment officer at FedWatch Advisors, called it a waning of the "Jensen effect."

The moves suggest "markets see government intervention as 'moral suasion' that carries credit and interest-rate risks," he said.

President Donald Trump told CNBC on Monday he "convinced" the board to bring Huang back.

CoreWeave wasn't the only name seeing heightened concerns in CDS spreads. Oracle's five-year CDS spread jumped 7% to 355.68. Even highly rated hyperscalers Meta Platforms and Amazon.com saw spreads widen by more than 2%. Nvidia's CDS added 3.4%.

Larry Holzenthaler, senior portfolio manager for fixed income at Catalyst Funds, believes the drama that unfolded with Huang's return to Nvidia won't necessarily sit well with credit investors.

"A lot of old school credit people are going to look at what kind of materialized there, the administration's involvement in putting him back in, and I think a lot of credit participants are going to step back and tiptoe away from that a bit," he said. "Credit market investors typically don't like that type of drama or uncertainty."

While Nvidia has commitments across the AI ecosystem, it is the neoclouds that have been under the most scrutiny. Many of the companies, which rent out AI computing power and cloud capacity, are highly leveraged and have relied on Nvidia financing to reduce their exposure to the bond market.

With so much riding on Nvidia's funding, Huang's next move is being closely watched.

During his brief absence, chief financial officer Colette Kress said during the company's earnings call that Nvidia would be "rigorously evaluating future investments" and will remain disciplined in how it commits capital.

That suggested Nvidia was going to be more judicious with financing, says Paul Meeks, head of technology research at Freedom Capital Markets.

"That concern should be put to rest with Jensen [Huang] back," he said.

Investor Steve Eisman, of "Big Short" fame and host of "The Real Eisman Playbook" podcast, believes Huang feels emboldened now that he's back in charge.

"[H]e'll double down on the circular financing," Eisman predicted on CNBC's "Squawk Box" Tuesday.

After Huang's departure, Eisman believed that the ecosystem the CEO created by pouring money into AI companies would likely unwind and leave some of the companies in "big trouble."

"Now that he's back, they're not," he said.

Others are not so certain the level of funding will continue.

"Given scrutiny of disclosure around financing and guarantees … we do not expect Mr. Huang's return to bring a quick reversion to broader customer financing," Stifel analyst Ruben Roy said in a note Monday.

Nvidia said on its earnings call that it has $118 billion in guarantees, as of Oct. 25, to back AI projects.

Concerns about the level of debt to fund the AI infrastructure buildout had already taken hold before Huang's departure and reinstatement.

"These issues have not disappeared just because the White House pressured the weak board to reinstate Jensen Huang," said economist David Rosenberg, founder and president of Rosenberg Research.

John Lloyd, global head of multi-sector and corporate credit at Janus Henderson Investors, believes having Huang back at the helm "reaffirms his existing go-to-market strategy." However, the credit market is still taking a cautious look at the neoclouds as it waits for more clarity on long-term supply/demand dynamics.

Still, Lloyd is cautiously positive on CoreWeave, which he owns in portfolios he manages, and noted all the data point to a "pretty healthy environment right now." That includes strong residual values on graphics processing units and spot compute rates, he noted.

"The company is doing everything in its power to execute," he said. "They don't need the high yield market at this time."

In the meantime, investigations into Nvidia and Huang are ramping up. Huang is expected to appear before three House committees next week. Nvidia is conducting an independent investigation into his activities, and California Attorney General Rob Bonta has launched a probe. At the federal level, President Trump said Monday that U.S. Attorney General Todd Blanche "has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

"There's other shoes to drop," Rosenberg said. "I don't think that the book has been closed on this file."

#2737. 'All about giving': Why the Lakers' Ziaire Williams enjoys giving back

As the gathering of kids began, their eyes grew wide and several of them said "oooh" when they saw the tall, slender young man with the stylish dreads approach the microphone and introduce himself as Lakers forward Ziaire Williams.

Williams had come to Inglewood High to provide meals and fellowship with about 150 kids from the Boys & Girls Clubs of Metro Los Angeles, an event he has done for his entire six-year NBA career.

Having grown up in Lancaster and attended Sierra Canyon, where he was a high school teammate of Bronny James, Williams saw himself in those kids. He, too, went to the Boys & Girls Club while a youth. He remembers the influence it made on his life and how his parents, Ziaire Williams Sr. and Marquita Fields-Williams, felt at ease because their son was safe.

Williams, who held the event last week, believes it is important to be active in the community.

"Being a part of a Thanksgiving event like this means a lot to me," he told the kids. "I appreciate all of you guys coming out to support the event. This is a time of giving and Thanksgiving. It's just all about giving back and being appreciative for your loved ones and all the small things in life.

"So, I hope you guys can meet some new friends, eat some good food, and just appreciate the small things in life. That's what it's all about."

Williams then helped to serve the food, a smile on his face, "joy" in his heart to see the kids enjoying the moment and each other, all of them relishing the opportunity to meet a Laker in person.

"I've actually been doing this every year since I've been in the NBA," Williams said. "Growing up, I was in the Boys & Girls Club. My parents were always at work. The Boys & Girls Club, it was almost like my second home growing up. It's just a great safe place after school just to hang out, do your homework, meet some new friends and occasionally Christmas, Thanksgiving, hopefully have a good meal, maybe a pizza party or something.

"So, since I've been in the NBA, I always try to just find different Boys & Girls Clubs I can partner with for just the holidays and seasons of giving. Now, just do my part to give back, give them something to smile about and, you can't go wrong with a good meal."

Initially, some of the kids looked nervous and shy when they walked by Williams.

But he greeted them all, shook hands, took pictures and listened to their stories.

This was their day and Williams was happy to be a meaningful part of it.

"Oh man, there's no words like it, man. It's hard to describe. It just makes my heart feel warm," Williams said. "I feel like God gave me this amazing ability to play basketball, but he also has allowed me to use my talents to do events like this and give back.

"And, man, it's amazing. Every time I just get to hang out with kids and just see a smile on their face, it really just brings me back to when I was their age, and it's amazing, man. There's no words that can describe it."

Lakers forward Ziaire Williams dunks over Trail Blazers guard Scoot Henderson during a game earlier this month at Crypto.com Arena. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

By most accounts, the 6-foot-9 Williams, 25, is having his best season as a pro.

He's averaging 9.1 points per game and shooting career bests from the field (53.8%) and three-point range (42.1%).

"Man, I would just say I just stuck with it. Just the same consistent work," Williams said of his success. "Sometimes it doesn't always pay off right away, but if you just keep sticking with it, keep being disciplined, your daily habits, it's bound to change quick. As the late [rapper] Nipsey Hussle always says, just never quit.

"Just the marathon always continues, and that's a principle and a moral I live by, just continuing to always try to be the best version of myself every day. And I'm just grateful to be in this position. I'm on a great team, a winning team, guys who care about the game and guys who want to compete at the highest level. And it's been an honor to play with the Lakers so far, and we still got a lot more work to do, but it's been a great start for sure."

Whether starting or coming off the bench, Williams has delivered by being the quintessential three-and D player the Lakers expected.

Before the Lakers signed Williams to a one-year, $3-million contract during free agency over the summer, they saw something else in him that made them yearn to have him as a member of their team.

"He's a beautiful human," Lakers coach JJ Redick said. "I didn't know him. We set up a workout for him in Vegas and he worked out. Rave reviews. I met with him the next day for about 45 minutes at our hotel and I was like, 'I got to get this guy on my team. Regardless of the player, I got to get this person on my team.' We sat there for 45 minutes and he looks you in the eye and it's 'yes sir, no sir. I got you, sir.' He's awesome. He's an incredible human."

Lakers coach JJ Redick congratulates forward Ziaire Williams along the sideline as he comes off the court for a timeout. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

Both of Williams' parents were in the military and he was taught to be respectful of others.

So, what Redick saw and felt was real, because Williams was raised that way.

"Yeah, it was definitely strict, but I'm thankful for it," Williams said. "Just the discipline and just prioritizing certain things. It was never hard for me to do because I just grew up with that discipline always from Day 1. So, it was definitely hard. I got yelled at. I got my butt whipped a couple times. It was all for the greater good. My parents wanted me to just be the best young man I could possibly be and put myself in a better position than they were in."

He remembers going to LeBron James' basketball camp in San Diego and winning the MVP award. That "was the coolest thing ever" to meet James. He remembers watching NBA games on TV and thinking how they "seemed like superheroes."

Now Williams is playing against James and those players he saw on TV.

But when he does his basketball camps and his Thanksgiving events as he did last week, he doesn't want the kids to view him as anything more than a humble person.

"I don't really want them to necessarily look at me as a superhero, but more so as just a person who's always willing to give back," Williams said. "I tie my shoes just the same way they do, you know? Every morning I just wake up and I thank God and I just try to be the best version of myself.

"That's what I just hope these kids get out of it is just whatever you put your mind to is possible. You just have to really believe in it and work at your craft every day. And I work on my craft every day and now it's got me here with the Lakers."

#2736. 'Show up for your guys': Nate Burleson blasts Aaron Rodgers' handling of Steelers benching

The Pittsburgh Steelers reportedly intended to keep Aaron Rodgers on their roster after benching him on Tuesday.

Rodgers made that impossible when he appeared on The Pat McAfee Show and firebombed his relationship with head coach Mike McCarthy. The Steelers promptly released the quarterback afterward.

Subsequent reports revealed that Rodgers demanded authority over play-calling or he didn't want to be there anymore, and when McCarthy disagreed, that was that.

CBS NFL analyst Nate Burleson said Thursday that whatever the facts on the ground are around McCarthy, Rodgers handled everything very poorly.

"I have praised Aaron Rodgers throughout his career. I'm a fan like so many other people. He did not handle this right. I don't care what anybody says," said Burleson on CBS's The NFL Today. "These things about Mike McCarthy, they might be true, but that doesn't remove him from accountability.

"He talked for a long time. You know what I didn't hear? I could have played better. And I feel like when you're a quarterback, I played with some great ones, everyone from Daunte Culpepper to Matt Hasselbeck, even Matt Stafford. When things were on fire, those quarterbacks stepped up and said, 'I could have played better.'

"I did hear him say this, which I appreciate. He said, 'I love my guys.' And if indeed you do love your guys, show up for your guys. Be a backup. Your team is about to play. Mason Rudolph is about to take the field. Why not step on the sideline with your high football IQ and help him win the game? If you love your guys like you say you do, be on the sideline. At the very least, when we sign a contract and get paid to do our job, we show up."

Of course, taking responsibility is not how Aaron Rodgers operates. Burleson probably knows that, but his points still stand.

Opinions about the Rodgers-McCarthy breakup have been intense, with some blaming Rodgers, others blaming McCarthy, and others blaming the Steelers organization for putting the two back together in the first place. That conversation around blame will continue in the coming weeks, but no one should expect Aaron Rodgers to take any responsibility.

#2735. Jayden Daniels: Brandon Aiyuk and I put the past behind us

Commanders quarterback Jayden Daniels and Brandon Aiyuk were once Arizona State teammates and close friends, but Aiyuk seemed to burn those bridges this year, when he posted several critical comments about Daniels on social media. After Aiyuk signed with the Commanders this week, however, Daniels said that’s all behind them.

“I’m a grown man, he’s a grown man,” Daniels said. “Things happen. You don’t know what he was going through. You don’t know what I was going through at that time.”

Daniels said he looked up to Aiyuk when they were at Arizona State together and thinks they can have a good relationship as teammates again.

“That’s somebody that looked out for me when I was 18 years old, helped me out when I first got to ASU,” Daniels said. “So, like I said, I know the character and the person he is. He was man enough to settle our differences and just move forward from there. That’s like a brother to me. Like I said, we’ve been knowing each other for some time, looked out for me, took care of me. So, just put the past behind us.”

As for the social media posts that Aiyuk made, Daniels said he was never angry about that.

“I prayed for him because he’s like family to me,” Daniels said. “I think that was a very dark moment in his life.”

Daniels declined to go into specifics about recent conversations with Aiyuk but said he hopes Aiyuk will be a good addition to the Commanders.

“That’s between us, personal, but yeah, we did chat,” Daniels said. “He works hard. Before his knee injury he was All-Pro, very explosive, big catch radius, so excited for him to have a fresh start, new opportunity to prove himself day in and day out.”

Aiyuk hasn’t played in more than two years, but he was once one of the best receivers in the NFL. If Aiyuk can play at anything close to the level he used to play at, he can be a significant addition to the Commanders’ offense. Daniels is excited about that possibility.

#2733. Toluca and Club América are just one game away from advancing to the semifinals

After the first day of the liguila, everything is still on the line for Saturday's return leg where the semifinalists will be determined. The matches between Toluca and Atlas and Monterrey and Club América have not been fully decided even with the scorelines from the first leg. Toluca defeated Atlas 2-0 but that scoreline is the most deceiving one in all of football. Meanwhile Monterrey were able to end the game in a scoreless draw against the Aguilas after being down to 10 men for 80 minutes.

Toluca opened the scoring in the first quarter of the game thanks to a golazo by Helinho. Atlas had been playing well the first 15 minutes of the game but their defense made a mistake when they allowed Helinho room to shoot. The Brazilian was able to shake the first defender but no one else came up to him to strip the ball so the player went for it from distance. There was nothing that Camilo Vargas could do to stop that rocket.

About 10 minutes later Atlas had leveled the match, or at least they thought they did when Florian Monzón scored but it was immediately called back for offsides. After that goal was called back Atlas slowed down. It was as if that call sucked all the life out of them.

At the start of the second half Atlas was just attempting to regroup themselves. They went forward to attack but Toluca was stronger than them in the beginning minutes. Then things went from bad to worse for Atlas in the 50th minute. Paulinho had gone down in the penalty area holding his leg. Initially the referee didn't call anything but then he was called to the monitor. There, it was shown that defender Adonis Frias kicked the Portuguese in the back of the leg. The referee agreed and awarded a penalty to Toluca. Helinho stepped up to take the penalty and while Vargas guessed right the shot had too much power and Toluca was up 2-0.

This goal seemed to wake up Atlas who looked to salvage a goal with the time they had left. The game even became a little spicy between the two sides as tempers flared between the two sides, especially the visitors who needed a goal to salvage the game. While the match ended 2-0, this scoreline is a dangerous one. Toluca will have to play with a lot of urgency so they don't lose sight of the score. Especially if they want to send off their coach Antonio Mohamed with a trophy.

In the first 10 minutes Monterrey had control of the game until everything changed with an expulsion. In the eighth minute Ricardo Chavez was given a yellow card for a foul on Oscar Perea. The referee was then called to the monitor to take a closer look at the action. There it showed that Chavez kicked Perea's thigh with his cleats full force and it was no doubt a red card. Now the home side was forced to play with 10 men for 80 minutes. Most would think that this would be the end of the match and the liguilla for Monterrey as América would take full control of the game. That didn't actually happen. Not because the Rayados parked the bus but because the visitors were unable to finish any of their chances. A lot of that was thanks to goalkeeper Luis Cardenas who had multiple big saves to salvage a draw.

While they were down a player, the Rayados didn't just rely on defending their net as they would make their way up the field to attack. They knew when they had to go up and attack and when they had to make their way back to defend. It also helped that América's coach Guillermo Almada did not make wise choices regarding his lineup. There were many questions as to who Almada decided to start in the game. The Uruguayan decided to bench Luis Angel Malagon, Henry Martin and Carlos Alvarez just to name a few. Defender Ramon Juárez was even left off the roster even though he has proven himself time and time again. Even with an advantage for the majority of the game the Aguilas were unable to score. This was a scenario where they should have scored a minimum of three goals. Instead they struggled in the final third and at times it looked as if they were lost and were unsure of what they were supposed to do. They will head back home for the second leg where all they have to do is draw to move forward as they are the higher seed. However, if they want to make it to the final they need to come up with a plan or they will once again find themselves eliminated in the playoffs.

#2732. Lakers, behind Luka Doncic, must crack Wemby code, exploit these areas to beat Spurs

The stars are aligning for a special regular-season game between the Lakers and Spurs on Friday night in San Antonio.

It's two of the NBA's best teams and the Western Conference's top teams matching up for the first time this season, featuring the top early-season MVP candidates in Lakers guard Luka Dončić and Spurs big man Victor Wembanyama.

Luka Doncic of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a free throw. NBAE via Getty Images

And it's also, essentially, a win-or-go-home NBA Group Play game for West Group C.

For the Lakers, the Spurs will be the biggest test for this still-newish group, with the reigning Western Conference champions equipped to poke the holes that most of the Lakers' previous opponents haven't been able to.

But the Lakers are also equipped to test the Spurs in ways not every team is able to.

And it starts with Dončić — one of the league's best offensive players who can force the Spurs to have to adjust their defensive strategy of keeping Wembanyama around the basket as much as possible.

Even amid a shooting slump, the Slovenian superstar's pull-up shooting and playmaking force defenses to be more aggressive with their coverages when he's the ball handler in pick and rolls.

San Antonio Spurs center Victor Wembanyama walks up the court. AP Photo/Darren Abate

Or even when he's isolating his defender, with opponents often showing proactive help to crowd driving and passing lanes.

If Austin Reaves is available against the Spurs after missing the previous five games due to a bruised left heel, the Spurs won't be able to load up their defense on Dončić in the same way.

The Spurs' defensive scheme allows for their perimeter defenders to be more aggressive with the intent of funneling actions toward Wembanyama, who is the league's best rim protector in large part due to his 7-foot-4 frame.

But the Lakers' use of Dončić starting possessions off the ball before receiving a screen to get the ball can test the Spurs' preference.

Austin Reaves (15) of the Los Angeles Lakers clapping. NBAE via Getty Images

If Wembanyama is too far in a drop and Dončić's defender doesn't stay attached, Dončić has the room he needs to get his shot off.

Wembanyama has the size and agility to play closer to the level of the screen while being in position to recover to protect the rim.

It's a cat-and-mouse game Dončić is just as equipped as any player to win against the Spurs, who once again have one of the league's best defenses.

Another area the Lakers are equipped to exploit is the Spurs' stingy transition defense.

The Spurs are one of the best teams at preventing scoring on fast breaks, but the Lakers are one of the best. They'll have to remain consistent with their throwahead passes without turning the ball over.

And defensively, Walker Kessler empowers the Lakers to prioritize protecting the paint against Wembanyama and forcing the Spurs, who are shooting 33.7% on 3-pointers (No. 25), to beat them with outside shots.

Friday will be a big test for the Lakers.

But they're equipped to come out of the matchup better than expected.

#2731. James Jones: I'm on text threads with former Packers, none are on Aaron Rodgers' side

In the dispute between Steelers coach Mike McCarthy and ex-Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers, players who were with both of them in Green Bay are squarely on McCarthy’s side.

That’s the word from former Packers wide receiver James Jones, who echoed the comments of former Packers offensive lineman T.J. Lang, both of whom say that in text threads of former Packers players, everyone agrees that Rodgers’ criticism of McCarthy was out of line.

“How do you jump on Pat McAfee’s show and say all this? Come on, man,” Jones said on the Speakeasy podcast. “My text thread is full of texts and group chats with a lot of my teammates right now, and none are on 12’s side, especially the ones that know Mike.”

Jones said that even if everything Rodgers said about McCarthy’s shortcomings as a coach is true, airing dirty laundry in public is unacceptable. Jones said that when a coach and a player have the kind of personal history that McCarthy and Rodgers have, they should address their differences privately.

“I don’t even care if it’s the truth,” Jones said. “Have the conversation with Mike in there. This is not something you go to the media bashing somebody. I would not do this to people that I love.”

It’s safe to say that there’s no love anymore between Rodgers and McCarthy. But there is a lot of love for McCarthy among former Packers players, who are not happy that Rodgers chose to publicly attack their coach.

#2729. Bucks vs. Nets Player Grades: Turner, Burries, and rest of team come down to earth in dreadful showing

In what might have been the worst Bucks game I've watched in some time—which is saying something—the Milwaukee Bucks lost to the 3-13 Brooklyn Nets 104-97. It really felt like the Bucks got the monkey off their backs against the Raptors and came into this game without a care in the world. They didn't play as a team, nor did they play with any passion. The worst part is that there aren't going to be many games this season where the Bucks have a genuine 50-50 chance of winning, and for Milwaukee to put up that kind of performance against a Brooklyn team that really tried its hardest to lose was really disappointing. The game also brings the Bucks to 0-3 in the NBA Cup. Read our full summary of the game here and catch a six-minute audio recap on the Bucks+ podcast, Bucks In Six Minutes, below.

37 minutes, 28 points, 3 rebounds, 5 assists, 3 steals, 5 turnovers, 10/20 FG, 3/9 3P, 5/5 FT, 0

I mean, Herro was just about the team's only source of creation, so I do want to credit him for that. Overall, though, this wasn't all that impressive of a game. I didn't think Herro was as willing to get his teammates involved as he had been, settling for a fair few bad shots and dominating the ball too much; granted, he would have had a few more assists if anyone could make a shot, but alas. And then the turnovers, man; most of them felt like killer live-ball giveaways.

18 minutes, 2 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 0 turnovers, 0/7 FG, 0/4 3P, -7

Oof. Really did not like Rollins' game. He made some nice reads to open the third, but overall, most of his shots came after he dribbled the air out of the ball for 10-14 seconds, then settled for step-back threes or wild drives at the rim. It was almost as if Rollins was trying to force his way into a groove, rather than playing instinctively and letting opportunities come to him. Moreover, we can usually rely on Rollins' defence being solid, but even that part of his game felt off; there was one possession in the third where the Bucks had finally taken the lead, were in a groove, and Rollins was guarding someone on the right wing; he was in perfect position to contain the ball, but he lunged for the steal, didn't get it, and his man went right past him, leading to the open three. Absolute coach killer.

31 minutes, 13 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 turnover, 5/11 FG, 3/7 3P, -12

Not too many complaints about Kuzma's game. He was just about Milwaukee's only credible defender on Randle, and overall, I thought he did a solid job making the Nets veteran work. Also made some solid reads to get into the paint and then spray out to shooters.

34 minutes, 21 points, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 2 steals, 9/15 FG, 1/5 3P, 2/4 FT, +5

Jaquez was solid in the middle game and a key part of the Bucks' best runs. He finished well around the rim and got his teammates involved. Still, can't go 1/5 from three when it feels like most of them are wide open. Just can't happen.

25 minutes, 3 points, 6 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 3 blocks, 3 turnovers, 1/2 FG, 1/2 3P, -1

Obviously, people will look at the scoring numbers, but I would say Turner's lack of production was a byproduct of the Bucks' lack of ball movement. Against the Raptors, the ball was moving, and that's how he got his opportunities; against the Nets, everything was stagnant and slow, so Turner shot just twice. The big man still impacted the game in other ways, but he also had a few backbreaking live-ball turnovers. Overall, it wasn't a good game for Turner by any means, but I thought other factors also contributed to him not playing well.

22 minutes, 13 points, 14 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 blocks, 1 turnover, 6/10 FG, 1/2 3P, -6

On balance, Ware was probably one of Milwaukee's better players. He created extra opportunities, with five of his 14 rebounds coming on the offensive glass, and he would have scored more points if just a few of those tip-ins had gone in. Also, continues to rack up blocks and be a presence around the rim (on both ends). I will say that the Nets were happy to play much higher in their pick-and-roll coverage with Ware in the game because they didn't trust his decision-making with a numbers advantage; he has to improve in that facet of the game.

21 minutes, 3 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 turnovers, 1/6 FG, 0/2 3P, 1/2 FT, -4

Burries ended his hot streak with a stinker. He couldn't find the same creases he had been finding (which is weird against one of the league's worst defensive teams), and he also didn't impact in other areas the way he usually does. Burries also made a really odd turnover in the fourth quarter: he caught the ball with no awareness that MPJ was right there and got it ripped away; part of that was on Ware for passing him the ball there, but Burries needs to have better awareness.

21 minutes, 8 points, 3 rebounds, 1 turnover, 4 PF, 3/6 FG, 2/5 3P, 0

I mean, Ament got most of his looks off catch-and-shoot opportunities, but he also got handed a few grenades as the shot clock was winding down. The rook was the third defender (behind Kuzma and Jaquez) on Randle, and despite a major weight disadvantage, he was decent in that matchup; Ament clearly read the scout, forcing him to his right hand and into help as much as possible—good stuff. He had a few possessions where he rotated late from the weak side in help, though.

12 minutes, 4 points, 4 rebounds, 3 assists, 2/7 FG, 0/5 3P, +2

Weird minutes for Jakucionis, who played three minutes in the first half and then was thrown into the fire at the end without really getting a feel for the game. He missed all his threes, but most were the right shots to take (the one bad one being his corner three, where he had Herro wide open for the extra pass up top). Also got baited into a bad foul late as the Bucks were making a charge.

11 minutes, 2 points, 2 assists, 1/2 FG, -6

I don't know. I guess LeVert was fine in his minutes. As I'll get to below, my issue was with how much he played.

This was the first game where I truly felt Jenkins played a key role in the team losing, mainly because of rotations. He opened the second quarter with a Rollins-Green-LeVert-Kuzma-Turner lineup that had a -78.6 net rating in 3:18, with Rollins—who, news flash, has been struggling—being the only creator (and boy did he try to create!). Then, to start the fourth quarter, Jenkins threw out a Jakucionis-Burries-LeVert-Ament-Ware lineup that had a -40.0 net rating in 2:38. One downside to starting all the veterans—a unit that, to their credit, played well together in this one—is that you can eventually get to lineups like these. Granted, I get that with all of this you're robbing Peter to pay Paul to some degree: if you take one guy from one lineup to help another, that original lineup will lose something. Even so, Jenkins can strike a much better balance than he did last night.

DNP-CD: Jericho Sims, Ousmane Dieng, Bogoljub Markovic, Gary Trent Jr.

Inactive: Pete Nance, Cormac Ryan, Kam Jones,

Even the Bucks can get high on their own supply and, dare I say it, disrespect their opponent.

Jenkins: "We abandoned what was working for us in the first half. We didn't have rhythm to our offence. I guess we got bored with the stuff that was working well for us."

Kuzma: "I think we were just thinking for ourselves… How can I get myself going instead of how can I get the team going? That's the story of our season right now."

I can't stress enough how much the Nets tried to give this game to the Bucks. They shot 41.6% from the field and 26.3% from three. Michael Porter Jr. went 4/14, for goodness' sake; you have to win when that happens. It felt like the team entered this game thinking they'd get a win; they were playing a team just as bad, and another win would come easy while they all played for their own numbers.

The Bucks had 14 turnovers for the game, and nine came in the last quarter.

Assistant coach Brad Jones noted at halftime that all 47 of the Bucks' first-half points came after touching the paint in that possession—every one.

I don't get the LeVert minutes sometimes. Like, if he stands out in his first stint, sure, throw him out there for another; but if his first stint doesn't stand out in any way, why put him back in? Surely you'd rather give those minutes to Dieng or even a guard. Sometimes the rotations can feel incredibly random.

The Bucks head to Detroit on Friday to face the Pistons, kicking off Milwaukee's back-to-back, with OKC in town the following night. Catch the game at 6:30 p.m. CST on MY24.

#2727. Here's a roadmap to the Aaron Rodgers developments, one of the craziest stories in years

If you were traveling on Wednesday or otherwise not plugged in for one of the most rollicking, unpredictable days in years if not ever, you’re in luck.

We’ve reconstructed the full timeline as to the events regarding now-former Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers.

It actually started on Monday, when coach Mike McCarthy was asked whether he has considered using one of the team’s other three quarterbacks when the offense is struggling. McCarthy, whose body language in hindsight suggests he was dreading the question, said without equivocation, “Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback.”

On Tuesday, Rodgers took his usual reps at practice. He conducted his usual midweek press conference. Unbeknownst to most of the media or the general public, the Steelers were changing their play-calling procedures to have quarterbacks coach Tom Arth, and not McCarthy, communicate the calls to Rodgers through the speaker in his helmet.

Then came Tuesday night. At 11:10 p.m. ET, the Steelers issued a statement — not from coach Mike McCarthy or G.M. Omar Khan or owner Art Rooney II but from team spokesman Burt Lauten — that Mason Rudolph will start on Friday against the Broncos and Rodgers will be inactive.

The news forced the Broncos to make a quick pivot in their preparations from Rodgers to Rudolph. As one source put it, the Broncos weren’t sure whether it was a good thing or a bad thing.

It was definitely a bad thing, all things considered, for the Steelers.

By Wednesday morning, reports emerged that it was a coach’s decision. At 8:00 a.m. ET, McCarthy informed the team of the decision.

Rodgers wasn’t there. As it turns out, he was excused.

Enter Pat McAfee. He teased a surprise guest at 1:40 p.m. ET. As expected, it was Rodgers.

Rodgers took a flamethrower to McCarthy, airing out dirty laundry that created a major mess for his now-former team. He exposed the fact that, at halftime of the Week 11 loss to the Eagles, McCarthy broke down the locker-room gathering by saying, “1-2-3 Packers.”

Rodgers also said McCarthy was falling asleep during some meetings and missing other meetings, expressing (in passive-aggressive fashion) concern for McCarthy’s health.

But Rodgers also said it was nothing new, and that McCarthy had done the same things in Green Bay, when they worked there together a decade ago.

Rodgers also made it clear he wouldn’t stay with the Steelers. After his session with McAfee and company, Gerry Dulac of the Pittsburgh Post-Gazette reported that the Steelers were expected to release Rodgers. By the official close of business, they’d waived him.

A question lingers as to whether the Steelers will try to deny Rodgers the balance of his $22 million salary. It won’t be easy; they’ll need to both void the guarantees and wipe out his eligibility for termination pay (in the form of his remaining salary) under the Collective Bargaining Agreement.

McCarthy met with reporters on Wednesday, after all hell had broken loose. He admitted to the “1-2-3 Packers” comment, calling it an “[e]mbarrassing moment.”

McCarthy also said that Rodgers was benched after issuing an “ultimatum” that was “clearly outside of his job description.”

ESPNFL thereafter reported that McCarthy “has the full support” of the organization, and that the team is “not planning to make any immediate changes.” The key word may be “immediate"; with few viable options only two days before a Black Friday game against the Broncos, changes could come later.

The overriding takeaway is that someone with the Steelers knew or should have known that McCarthy and Rodgers couldn’t co-exist. Because, obviously, they didn’t co-exist.

If, as Rodgers claims, McCarthy believed Rodgers got him fired in Green Bay, McCarthy shouldn’t have wanted a reunion with Rodgers. And if, as Rodgers claims, McCarthy was doing the same kinds of things in Green Bay that he did in Pittsburgh, Rodgers shouldn’t have wanted a reunion with McCarthy.

The reunion has now ended abruptly, with seven games left in the 2026 regular season. It remains to be seen whether Rodgers wants to play with another team, or whether another team wants him. Any team that signs him risks being on the receiving end of a future gripe session from Rodgers in which he lists all the things that team and/or the coach did wrong — without ever acknowledging his own role in the reality that it didn’t work out.

The good news, if there is any, is this: If Rodgers never plays again, his final NFL pass will have been a touchdown.

Which is a better outcome than if he’d retired after the 2025 season. His final pass that year was a pick-six in the playoffs.

#2725. Fernando Mendoza perfected Aaron Rodgers 'guitar fake' since joining Raiders

There were few criticisms to be leveed against Fernando Mendoza coming out of college. The Heisman Trophy winner led the Indiana Hoosiers to an undefeated season and a National Championship. But one of the narratives surround his potential success in the pos was the fact that he had worked almost exclusively out of the shotgun and it remained to be seen whether he could transition to working under center at the NFL level.

Not only has he put those questions to bed in his first two starts, he has far exceeded expectations in one aspect in particular -- the play action fake.

"It's been a journey," Mendoza said of his learning curve on play fakes, "especially all the under center fakes and making sure that your play-action fakes look the same as your handoffs, and that's been a big point of emphasis and pride, especially with all the coaches. Coach [Mike] Sullivan has been working with me intently on that throughout this entire process, and I still have a long way to go."

The above play action bootleg resulted in a touchdown pass to TE Ian Thomas. And he had a couple nice play action fakes early in the Week 10 games against the Seahawks to lead out a touchdown drive. Which speaks very highly of his football IQ and work ethic.

"I've seen him make great strides in that area," said head coach Klint Kubiak. "Obviously, not playing a lot of under center football, he was very intentional about it going from April, and then we took time off, and he came back and definitely that was a part of his game that grew when we weren't with him. So obviously, extremely self-motivated to get better in all facets of the game."

The thing is though, that Mendoza's skill in the play action fake is not just proficient, it's already downright elite. His execution of it is being compared to Aaron Rodgers and his signature guitar fake.

"Yeah, Aaron Rogers, he also went to Cal, so it's a little funny, but it was something that we've worked on a lot," Mendoza added. "It's a part of Coach [Klint] Kubiak's scheme, and that's something that we were even talking about after the game, that in April when I first showed up, the guitar fake, it's what we call it, looked horrendous. So then actually being a productive part of the offense this past Sunday was a cool moment to see the development."

It's also interesting that during Aaron Rodgers's now infamous rant on the Pat McAfee show on Wednesday which may have gotten him cut by the Steelers, Rodgers mentioned Mendoza and praised him for his play against Denver.

Rodgers may have his character flaws that have had three teams get rid of him in recent years. But there's no questioning he is one of the greatest to ever spin it in the NFL. Hare to understate how impressive it is to be compared so directly to that caliber of QB in just your second NFL start.

Now the Raiders just need to develop more of a run threat to allow more opportunities for Mendoza to show it off.

This article originally appeared on Raiders Wire: Fernando Mendoza perfected Aaron Rodgers 'guitar fake' since joining Raiders

Brook Lopez playing in an NBA game for the Clippers against the Mavericks. NBAE via Getty Images

Redden was the same member of the Clippers' front office who finalized the decision to move Kawhi Leonard from Los Angeles to the Raptors over the offseason.

According to Fischer, the Clippers have a few veteran pieces who could be dealt, including center Brook Lopez and wing Derrick Jones Jr.

Fischer added the club wants to assess what its roster will look like with Brandon Ingram staying healthy.

Ingram was acquired in the trade with Toronto for Leonard. He came over to Los Angeles with Gradey Dick, two first-round draft picks, including a first-round pick swap, and two second-round draft picks.

Brandon Ingram making his season debut for the Clippers on Monday night against the Rockets. Getty Images

Ingram missed the first month and a half of the season due to a partially torn Achilles tendon he suffered with the Raptors last season. He made his season debut on Monday.

Ingram was the Clippers' third-leading scorer in their 116-104 loss to Houston on Monday, but he gave the team a glimmering hope about his health. Ingram was on the court for 27 minutes and tallied 16 points, seven rebounds and four assists.

#2723. Micah Parsons: Our play in second half Wednesday night was inexcusable

Packers defensive end Micah Parsons made his return to the lineup against the Rams on Wednesday night, but he wasn’t able to help his team to a win in Los Angeles.

Parsons picked up a sack late in the first half that sent the game to the break with the score tied 13-13, but he couldn’t make an impact in the second half because the Rams barely threw the ball. After running 12 times for 46 yards in the first half, the Rams ran 35 times for 194 yards while outscoring the Packers 13-0 in the final 30 minutes.

After the game, Parsons said it is “just inexcusable” to allow opposing teams to “dictate games like that” and said the aftermath has to be an increased level of accountability for the entire defense.

“We need to figure out who’s going to be a dog or not,” Parsons said. “That’s just the reality of it. To me, to be honest and this is around the league, if someone can line up 50 times and run down your mouth and you don’t have an answer for it, that is player — that’s accountability. That’s just how I really feel about it. I wasn’t able to get on the field because of it, because they didn’t want to waste my plays on first and second down I think the whole thing is just bad and we’ve got to figure out who’s willing to play with us or who’s playing against us at this point.”

The Packers don’t have the luxury of having time to figure things out. The loss dropped them to 6-5 on the season and the makeup of the NFC means that they’re going to have to win most of their remaining games to have any chance of advancing to the postseason.

#2721. Simmons' 15 points, Cherry's school-record 9 blocks, lead San Diego State over Maryland 75-71

LAS VEGAS (AP) — Tae Simmons scored 15 points, including seven in the final 4:35, and Bear Cherry had a school-record nine blocks as San Diego State rallied to beat Maryland 75-71 on Thursday at the Players Era 16 tournament.

San Diego State plays No. 5 Texas Tech or Oregon in the fifth-place game on Friday.

The Aztecs (6-1) trailed 65-59 with 4:52 remaining before scoring 12 straight points. Isaiah Sy hit a 3-pointer to pull San Diego State within one, and Simmons followed with a driving layup that gave the Aztecs a 66-65 lead with 3:10 left.

Cherry made two free throws before Simmons converted a three-point play to push the lead to 71-65 with 1:24 remaining.

Mike McNair Jr. answered with a 3 for Maryland, but Simmons hit an 11-foot hook shot with 29 seconds left and added two free throws to seal it.

Latrell Davis led San Diego State with 16 points and six rebounds. Chance Gladden and Elzie Harrington added 12 points apiece, while Cherry finished with 10 points and six rebounds.

McNair scored 22 points and made six 3-pointers for Maryland (3-4). DJ Wagner and Kaden House each added 12.

Maryland: Hosts American University this Wednesday.

San Diego State: Plays UNLV at home on Dec. 5.

#2720. Tony Romo re-emerges, as an employee of a gambling company

The unofficial path for a sports-media second act is becoming more and more clear.

Tony Romo, whose career with CBS ended earlier this year in the aftermath of a DUI arrest that would have been overlooked if the company was otherwise happy with his work, will be the “chief predictions officer” at Betr.

The press release leans in to Romo’s reputation for being able to predict plays before they happen. This overlooks the fact that he got away from doing that after his first year or so in the CBS booth. It also glosses over the reality that his access to practice and production meetings equipped him with the ability to make “predictions” that (based on his inside information) also could be called “spoilers.”

It’s not entirely clear what he’ll be doing. The release says that Romo will “serve as a prominent face of the Betr brand, appearing in original content, delivering sports predictions and engaging fans across the company’s expanding ecosystem of gaming products.”

Of course, Betr won’t want his predictions to be too accurate — as it relates to its sportsbook. The sportsbooks only win when the customers lose. If the customers win too much, the sportsbook will land in one of two chapters: 11 or 7.

Prediction markets are different. The companies make money on the transactions, with the participants balancing out wins and losses in the zero-sum game of who’s right and who’s wrong.

The next question is whether this is the first step for Romo, or whether it’s his primary post-CBS play. With the NFL and its broadcast partners aligned with the Coke and Pepsi of sports gambling, Romo’s relationship with one of the various companies that hopes to establish itself as the RC Cola won’t improve his shot at getting back into a prominent position in a broadcast booth.

Regardless, Romo is back. At least he’s not working for an illegal offshore sportsbook.

Either way, his message will be the same to the masses as it is to those who are looking for a fresh foothold in sports media.
